# World-Class Full-Product Audit — 2026-06-11

**Method.** 26 specialist finder agents over the full codebase (~120k LOC: every controller, service, reducer, screen, query, workflow step, Dockerfile, deploy script), cross-auditor dedup, then one adversarial verifier per finding (default stance: refute; locked product decisions classified `by_design`, not bugs). 167 + 140 agents across two runs (~6M tokens). Session-limit interruptions were recovered from journals; verification coverage and gaps are stated honestly below.

**Coverage caveats (read first).**
- 112 of 116 round-1 findings were adversarially verified; 4 verifications died mid-run (listed under *Unverified*).
- The two re-run finders (team-chat, chat-ux) produced 15 findings whose verification pass was interrupted — listed under *Unverified*.
- The 2-refuter confirmation panels, completeness-critic gap round, and 3 scorer agents never completed; scores below are the lead auditor's calibrated synthesis of the verified evidence, and high-severity findings stand on a single verifier.
- Static analysis only — no live-browser testing in this pass.

**Result: 0 critical · 4 high · 43 medium · 43 low · 9 info confirmed** (+19 unverified, 14 by-design, 3 refuted). No cross-tenant leak, no auth bypass, no data-loss path was found by any of the 26 auditors — the multi-tenancy, HMAC, and event-safety invariants held under adversarial review.


---

## Confirmed findings


## HIGH (4)

### 0. Template quick-reply button replies (inbound type "button") are silently dropped — reply lost AND the 24h window stays closed

**Severity:** high · **Location:** `apps/api/src/lib/providers/meta.ts:488-562` · **Area:** messaging-provider

**Root cause:** When a customer taps a QUICK_REPLY button on a sent template, Meta delivers an inbound message with `type: "button"` (payload `button: { text, payload }`) — NOT `type: "interactive"` (that shape is only for interactive button/list messages, which ARE handled at meta.ts:511-548). parseWebhook has no `button` branch and the MetaMessage interface (meta.ts:247-260) has no `button` field, so the row falls through to the media gate `META_MEDIA_TYPES.includes(mediaKind)` (meta.ts:561-562) and is dropped with `continue` — no message row, no rawPayload, no unread bump. The controller returns 200 so Meta never redelivers. Contact.lastInboundAt is only bumped inside ingestInboundMessage, so the DB 24h window never opens either.

**Impact:** Customer replies to quick-reply template campaigns are permanently lost (Meta got a 200; no history sync), the conversation shows no activity, unread doesn't bump, and the agent cannot free-form reply because the DB 24h window never opened. Direct hit on the pilot's broadcast/re-engagement flow.

**Recommended fix:** Add a `button` branch in parseWebhook mirroring the interactive one: push a NormalizedInboundMessage with body = m.button.text and interactiveReply = { kind: "button_reply", id: m.button.payload ?? m.button.text, title: m.button.text } (also lets ask_question/branch steps match on payload). Add `button?: { text?: string; payload?: string }` to MetaMessage.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced the full chain — parser branches on text(488)/interactive(511)/reaction-skip(557) then the media gate drops type "button" via continue; grep confirms zero inbound "button" handling anywhere in apps/api (the only `type: "button"` hit at meta.ts:697 is the OUTBOUND interactive-send body). Silent loss + closed window both real.

### 1. message:new for a conversation outside the loaded list slice is silently dropped — the conversation never surfaces in the inbox list while the tab stays foregrounded

**Severity:** high · **Location:** `apps/web/src/features/team-chat/hooks/use-team-events.ts:611-620` · **Area:** realtime-client-state

**Root cause:** onMessageNew bails when the conversation isn't in the loaded slice and the frame carries no full row: `const idx = prev.findIndex(...); if (idx === -1) { if (!newConversation) return prev; ... }`. The server only attaches `newConversation` for brand-new conversations and reopens (apps/api/src/lib/providers/ingest.ts:696-718 `isNewConversation || reopened`; outbound: apps/api/src/messages/messages.service.ts:1756-1762 `if (conversationIsNew ...)`). The list page size is 25 (apps/api/src/lib/queries/conversations.ts:30 `CONVERSATIONS_PAGE = 25`). So an inbound to an existing OPEN conversation that has fallen below the top-25 (or below the loaded page of any filter view) hits idx===-1 with no newConversation and is dropped from list state.

**Impact:** An agent actively watching the inbox hears the notification ding and sees the sidebar/title unread count bump (useConversationCounts refetches on inbound message:new), but the conversation row is invisible in every list view and unreachable via Load-more — a new customer message effectively disappears from triage until the agent blurs/refocuses the tab or the socket reconnects. The 2026-06-02 M2 fix covered reopens, not this open-conversation path.

**Recommended fix:** In onMessageNew's idx===-1 branch, when `newConversation` is absent, trigger recovery instead of returning prev: either fire runCoalescedResync (bypassing the broad-view skip for this specific case — the skip was about teammate status churn, not unknown rows), or fetch the single row (`/api/conversations?...` head page or a by-id lookup) and insertByRecency. Alternatively have the server attach `newConversation` whenever the conversation's previous lastMessageAt is older than some threshold.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced the full chain from the code. (1) The inbox-list reducer drops the frame: apps/web/src/features/team-chat/hooks/use-team-events.ts:611-620 — `onMessageNew` does `const idx = prev.findIndex(...); if (idx === -1) { if (!newConversation) return prev; ... }`. (2) The server attaches `newConversation` ONLY for brand-new or reopened conversations: apps/api/src/lib/providers/ingest.ts:696-718 (`isNewConversation || reopened ? {...} : undefined`); outbound likewise only when `conversationIsNew` (apps/api/src/messages/messages.service.ts forward/send paths). So an inbound to an EXISTING open/pending conversation that has fallen below the loaded list slice hits idx===-1 with no row payload and is silently discarded. (3) No fallback fires: `onMessageNew` (lines 575-712) never calls `scheduleFilterResync`, which in any case no-ops for the default "active"/"all" views (lines 520-522). (4) The row is unreachable via Load-more: keyset pagination fetches strictly OLDER than the cursor (apps/api/src/lib/queries/conversations.ts:92-99, page size 25 at line 30/86), while the inbound just advanced the row's lastMessageAt to NOW — newer than the loaded head — so no page can ever contain it. (5) Recovery exists only on socket reconnect (resyncWithBackoff, lines 564-571) or visibilitychange→visible (lines 1012-1022) or a filter switch — i.e. a continuously foregrounded, connected tab (the agent actively triaging) never converges. (6) The discrepancy is user-visible: use-conversation-counts.ts:152-162 refetches authoritative counts on every inbound (sidebar pills bump) and notification-sound-provider.tsx dings app-wide — the agent is told a message arrived but no list view can show it. Not by_design: the ingest.ts comment at 684-692 documents that the team fixed this exact idx===-1 mechanism for REOPENS (the 2026-06-02 M2 fix: "a reopened thread stayed invisible in a teammate's default Active view") — the open-but-below-slice case is the same mechanism left uncovered; and scheduleFilterResync's own comment ("the broad view will surface them whenever lastMessageAt next advances") asserts behavior the drop branch does not deliver for off-slice rows, so intent contradicts implementation. Severity high (not critical): no data loss — the message and unread count are correct in the DB and the thread is findable via search/contacts/?c=; but a customer message silently vanishing from the primary triage list during normal pilot use (any view with >25 matching conversations + a dormant open conversation getting a reply) is exactly the "silently drops customer messages from triage" bug class this project treats as serious, and the trigger conditions are routine for a busy inbox. Incidental recoveries (tab refocus, reconnect) bound the duration in practice but are not designed mitigations.

### 2. metaFetch blindly retries non-idempotent POSTs on 5xx/timeout — double-send path for message sends and double-ring path for placeCall, contradicting the OutboundSendAttempt ambiguity design

**Severity:** high · **Location:** `apps/api/src/lib/providers/meta.ts:72-120 (sends at 630, 736, 939, 1186; placeCall at 1272-1318)` · **Area:** messaging-provider

**Root cause:** [Merged: two auditors reported the same root cause — metaFetch's blanket once-retry — via two consumer surfaces: message sends (messaging-provider) and placeCall (calls-module).] Every Meta call goes through metaFetch, which retries ONCE on any 5xx response and on any network error/20s timeout, re-sending the identical buffered body (meta.ts:91-96 for 5xx, 98-112 for errors/AbortError; AbortError is explicitly classified retryable mid-loop). This wrapper is used by sendText, sendInteractive, sendMedia, and sendTemplate — the customer-visible message POSTs, which have no idempotency key on Meta's side. A 5xx-after-accept or a stalled-but-eventually-processed request therefore delivers the message TWICE to the customer. One layer up, the send worker explicitly classifies exactly this situation as AMBIGUOUS and refuses a BullMQ retry to avoid a double-send: messages.service.ts:823-829 — "AMBIGUOUS — a Meta 5xx (Meta MAY have… [truncated in source]. The calls-module duplicate adds: all seven calling methods share metaFetch (comment at meta.ts:1227); for sendText this retry posture was previously adjudicated safe per that auditor, but placeCall (meta.ts:1272-1318) is a NEW non-idempotent consumer — each successful attempt rings the customer's phone. If attempt #1 times out (20s cap) or returns 5xx AFTER Meta actually initiated the call, attempt #2 places a second real call. The duplicate from attempt #1 has no local Call row until its webhook arrives (ingestCallEvent inserts it with no initiator), and if the customer answers it, its SDP-answer frame matches n… [truncated in source]

**Impact:** A Meta 503/timeout blip on a send can deliver the same WhatsApp message twice to the customer — the exact failure class (customer-visible duplicate) the worker-level attempt guard, the broadcast BroadcastSendAttempt guard, and the workflow skipped_after_crash journal were all built to prevent. Frequency is low per send but non-zero over months of pilot traffic and Meta incidents. For calls: rare but customer-visible — the customer's phone rings twice (or they answer a dead phantom call), and the team's call history shows a stray unattributed outbound call; the calls-equivalent of a double-send.

**Recommended fix:** Make metaFetch's retry opt-in per call: keep it for idempotent/safe calls (fetchMedia, markIncomingRead, fetchTemplates, media upload) and disable the 5xx/timeout retry for the four /messages send POSTs and the /calls initiation POST (pass `retries: 0` or `idempotent: false`). Let the existing worker-level classification own the retry/refuse decision for sends — it already does this correctly. For placeCall, surface transient failures to the agent as the existing 'provider_rejected' (the agent can re-click) — or wrap initiation in the same pre-attempt guard pattern messages use.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced from current code. metaFetch (apps/api/src/lib/providers/meta.ts:72-119) retries ONCE on any 5xx (91-96) and on AbortError/20s-timeout/network error (99-111), re-POSTing the identical buffered body. It is the transport for all four customer-visible message sends (sendText :630, sendInteractive :736, sendMedia :939, sendTemplate :1186) and placeCall (:1277) — none of which carry an idempotency key (Meta's /messages and /calls APIs have none). This directly contradicts the codebase's own documented invariant, enforced one layer ABOVE in three places: messages.service.ts:823-833 explicitly classifies a Meta 5xx ("Meta MAY have accepted the message then errored its RESPONSE") and transport-error/timeout as AMBIGUOUS and REFUSES retry "to avoid a DOUBLE-SEND"; broadcast-runner.ts:1248-1253 ("one missing message beats a duplicate"); workflows/runner.ts skipped_after_crash journal (:337-456). Because metaFetch's retry fires BELOW these guards and a successful retry surfaces no error at all, the guards never engage — the customer receives the message twice while the DB records one row (the first attempt's wamid is never captured, so its status webhooks drop as unknown). The most plausible trigger is the timeout path: a client-side abort at 20s does not undo Meta's server-side processing, and the automatic re-POST then duplicates with near-certainty. NOT by_design: the metaFetch comment's rationale ("5xx is transient by Meta convention") is contradicted by the more careful, later analysis in messages.service.ts; metaFetch retry appears in no locked-decision list; and the prior audit false-positive "retry-guard 5xx kept=safe" covers the provablyNotSent guard at messages.service.ts:831 (different layer) and in fact re-confirms the invariant this finding shows being bypassed. The 2026-06-01 audit only praised metaFetch's centralization structurally (docs/production-audit-2026-06-01.md:1416), never ruling on double-send semantics. Severity: HIGH is correct, not critical — the double-send is conditional on a Meta 5xx-after-accept or timeout-after-accept (transient upstream ambiguity, not deterministic or attacker-triggerable), bounded to one duplicate (META_FETCH_MAX_ATTEMPTS=2), with no cross-tenant/auth/data-loss dimension; but over months of pilot traffic and Meta incidents it is a customer-visible duplicate-message path a pilot will plausibly hit in normal use. placeCall double-ring is the same mechanism with lower impact. The proposed fix (opt-in retry: keep for idempotent calls like fetchMedia/markIncomingRead/fetchTemplates/media upload, disable for the 4 /messages POSTs + /calls, letting the existing worker-level classification own retry/refuse) is consistent with the existing architecture.

### 3. server.requestTimeout=30s on the api kills large media uploads the product explicitly supports (95MB docs, client allows 90s)

**Severity:** high · **Location:** `apps/api/src/main.ts:312` · **Area:** reliability-failure

**Root cause:** main.ts sets `server.requestTimeout = 30_000` on the NestJS HTTP server — Node (>=18) counts this against receiving the ENTIRE request including the body, and destroys the socket on expiry. But the media send path is a synchronous multipart POST to this same server: messages.controller.ts accepts files up to 100 MiB via multer diskStorage, and the client (reply-box.tsx) enforces Meta's caps — image 4.8MB, video/audio 15MB, document 95MB — sets a 90s AbortController for media uploads, and its own comment anticipates 'a 4-minute upload' for borderline documents. Caddy streams request bodies (request buffering off by default), so a slow client body reaches Node slowly. Any upload whose transfer…

**Impact:** A pilot agent sending a large document or a video over a normal uplink gets a guaranteed, retry-proof send failure — core chat functionality (media send) broken for a whole size/bandwidth class, with no server log clearly attributing it (the request dies as a socket timeout, not a handler error).

**Recommended fix:** Raise `server.requestTimeout` to cover the worst legitimate upload (e.g. 300s, Node's default) and keep slow-loris protection via the already-set `headersTimeout=10s` (header phase is the actual slowloris vector); or set requestTimeout per-connection lower only for non-upload routes. Keep the client 90s cap in sync with whatever value is chosen.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced the full chain. (1) apps/api/src/main.ts:312 sets server.requestTimeout=30_000 on the NestJS HTTP server; on Node 24 (package.json engines '>=24 <25', apps/api/Dockerfile FROM node:24-slim) this caps receipt of the ENTIRE request INCLUDING the body — enforced via the 30s connections-checking interval, so a still-streaming body is killed 30-60s in with a 408 + socket destroy. The comment at main.ts:308-309 ('covers the slowest legitimate upload preflight while still bounding a stuck body') reveals the author treated it as an idle/stuck-body bound; it is a total-receipt cap and an actively flowing upload never resets it. (2) The media send is a synchronous multipart POST to this exact server: reply-box.tsx:769 POSTs FormData to /api/messages/media; deploy/Caddyfile.template:224-271 routes /api/* directly to 127.0.0.1:4000 with NO request_buffers directive, and Caddy streams request bodies by default, so the body arrives at Node at client-uplink speed (dev hits :4000 directly). No per-route timeout override exists anywhere in apps/api/src. (3) The product explicitly supports uploads that cannot complete in 30s: multer fileSize 100 MiB (messages.controller.ts:86), client caps document=95MB / video=15MB (reply-box.tsx:75-80) with a comment anticipating 'a 4-minute upload' (:72-74), and a 90s media AbortController (:747) — 3x the server's body budget. A 95MB doc needs ~13-25 Mbps sustained uplink to fit the 30-60s effective window; a 15MB video needs ~2-4 Mbps. Below that, the upload is destroyed mid-flight, the bubble shows 'failed' + Retry, and Retry deterministically fails again — a retry-proof break of core media send for a whole size/bandwidth class. Not by_design: no locked decision covers HTTP server timeouts, and the in-code comment shows intent to support these uploads, defeated by a misunderstanding of Node's requestTimeout semantics. Severity high is correct: a pilot agent sending a large PDF or a video on an ordinary (e.g. 10 Mbps or mobile) uplink hits a guaranteed failure of core chat functionality in normal use, misattributed client-side as 'server didn't respond' and with no clear server log (socket timeout, not handler error). One fix-detail correction: the client itself aborts media at 90s (reply-box.tsx:747), so the server budget needs to comfortably exceed 90s (e.g. Node's 300s default) and the two should be kept in sync, with headersTimeout=10s retained as the slowloris guard.


## MEDIUM (43)

### 1. No password recovery path anywhere — a forgotten password permanently locks the user out

**Severity:** medium (finder said high) · **Location:** `apps/web/src/app/login/login-form.tsx:51-95` · **Area:** ux-screens

**Root cause:** The login form has no 'Forgot password?' link and no reset route exists anywhere in apps/web/src/app (grep for forgot/reset across app, lib, features returns nothing UI-facing). The only password change is self-service in /settings/account and it requires the CURRENT password (change-password-form.tsx:36-37). Admins cannot reset an agent's password: team-settings.tsx patchUser only accepts { role, deactivated } (team-settings.tsx:125). The workaround of deactivating and re-inviting the same email is blocked server-side: invites.service.ts:87-91 throws ConflictException 'email already in use' because User.email is globally unique. Net: any agent (or the admin themselves) who forgets a passwor…

**Impact:** A pilot team WILL hit this in normal use (forgotten passwords are routine). The affected agent is permanently locked out; the admin has no recovery tool; support escalation requires manual database edits on the production VPS.

**Recommended fix:** Either a minimal self-serve reset (Better Auth ships email-based reset — needs an SMTP/resend integration) or, cheaper for pilot scale, an admin-side 'Reset password' action in /settings/team that generates a one-time set-password link reusing the existing invite-token + accept-form machinery.

> Verifier (high confidence): The core claim is independently verified: there is NO self-serve password recovery and NO admin password-reset. (1) apps/web/src/app/login/login-form.tsx:50-94 renders only Email/Password/error/SubmitButton — no recovery link, and no forgot/reset route exists anywhere in apps/web/src/app. (2) Better Auth is configured without `sendResetPassword` (packages/shared/src/auth/better-auth-config.ts:77-99), and better-auth 1.6.11's forget-password route hard-fails without it (node_modules .pnpm better-auth dist/api/routes/password.mjs:43: "Reset password isn't enabled."); there is zero email-sending infrastructure in the repo (no nodemailer/resend/smtp/sendgrid hits), so even the raw endpoint is dead. (3) The only password-writing path for existing users is ChangePasswordController (apps/api/src/auth/change-password.controller.ts:79-130), which is SessionGuard'd and requires the CURRENT password. (4) Admin PATCH /api/users/:id accepts only { role, deactivated } (apps/api/src/users/users.schemas.ts:3-8). (5) Deactivate-then-reinvite is indeed blocked: invites.service.ts:87-92 throws ConflictException because User.email is globally unique (schema.prisma:375). No memory file, locked decision, or deferred-list entry declares this deliberate — the auth catch-all route comment (apps/web/src/app/api/auth/[...all]/route.ts:16) even anticipates "future password-reset endpoints", so this is a real unbuilt gap, not by_design.

HOWEVER, the severity is overstated. The finding's headline — "permanently locks the user out; the admin has no recovery tool; support escalation requires manual database edits" — is wrong for the common case. The auditor checked only the deactivate path and missed the hard-delete path: DELETE /api/users/:id (users.controller.ts:150-158, users.service.ts:311-353) cascade-deletes Session+Account, all authorship FKs are onDelete: SetNull (schema.prisma:930 Message.sender, :1279 InternalNote.author, etc.), and the User row removal frees the globally-unique email — so the subsequent re-invite of the same address passes the invites.service.ts:89 findUnique check. This action is exposed in the UI: team-settings.tsx:138-144 has a "Delete user" confirm dialog explicitly stating "Messages and notes they wrote stay in the inbox, but the author becomes 'Removed user.'" So a locked-out agent IS recoverable in-product by an admin (delete + re-invite same email), at the real cost of permanently degrading attribution (author → "Removed user", conversations unassigned) — painful in a product whose core value is attribution, but not a lockout and not a DB edit. The only truly in-product-unrecoverable case is the SOLE admin forgetting their own password (self-delete is refused at users.service.ts:326-328; agents can't manage users; the superAdmin platform shell has no team-user tooling — apps/api/src/admin/ contains only admin-analytics and admin-teams controllers), which does require operator DB intervention — a bounded edge at pilot scale where the operator owns the VPS. Per the calibration bar (high = correctness bug or outage a pilot hits in normal use; medium = real but bounded/edge): a pilot will hit forgotten passwords in normal use, but the consequence is a destructive-yet-available admin workaround plus one edge case needing DB surgery — real but bounded ⇒ medium. The proposed fix direction (admin-side one-time set-password link reusing invite-token machinery, or Better Auth email reset once SMTP exists) is sound.

### 2. A queued/running broadcast cannot be stopped from the UI — server cancel endpoint exists, and the server's own error message tells users to use it

**Severity:** medium (finder said high) · **Location:** `apps/web/src/app/(app)/broadcasts/[id]/broadcast-detail.tsx:247-268` · **Area:** ux-screens

**Root cause:** The backend implements POST /api/broadcasts/:id/cancel (broadcasts.controller.ts:98-104), valid 'while scheduled, queued, or running' (broadcasts.service.ts:613-619). No frontend code calls it: grep for any broadcast '/cancel' fetch across apps/web/src returns zero hits. The detail page header renders only a Retry-failed button and a status badge (broadcast-detail.tsx:247-268); the list row's delete button is explicitly DISABLED while running/queued with tooltip 'Wait for the broadcast to finish' (broadcast-delete-button.tsx:32, 67-71). Worse, the server's delete-refusal copy literally instructs the user to 'Cancel the broadcast (or wait for it to finish) before deleting it' (broadcasts.serv…

**Impact:** An operator who launches a broadcast with the wrong audience or template (the wizard fires on a single click — see separate finding) has no way to stop irreversible, customer-visible WhatsApp sends mid-flight. At pilot scale a few-hundred-recipient run takes minutes — exactly the window where mistakes are noticed. The error toast they see while trying to delete points at a control that doesn't exi…

**Recommended fix:** Add a 'Cancel broadcast' button on broadcast-detail.tsx (visible while status is scheduled/queued/running/paused) and optionally on the list row, wired to POST /api/broadcasts/:id/cancel with the existing useConfirm pattern. The backend is already done.

> Verifier (high confidence): Every claim reproduces from the code. (1) The backend cancel endpoint exists and is fully built: apps/api/src/broadcasts/broadcasts.controller.ts:98-106 (@Post(":id/cancel") with SessionGuard + @RequireCapability("broadcasts:manage")), apps/api/src/broadcasts/broadcasts.service.ts:601-637 (CAS status flip to canceled, plus removeScheduledBroadcast for delayed jobs). The runner cooperatively honors it between recipients and its own comment says it is FOR operators: apps/api/src/lib/broadcast-runner.ts:667 "Cooperative cancel — operators flip the broadcast row to `canceled`", with checkCanceled() at :680-692 and the bail at :698. (2) No frontend caller exists: exhaustive grep of apps/web/src finds the only broadcast mutations are DELETE /api/broadcasts/:id (broadcast-delete-button.tsx:45), POST :id/retry (broadcast-detail.tsx:93), and GETs; no fetch to :id/cancel and no Stop/Cancel/Abort control under any other name in the broadcasts UI. (3) The detail-page header renders only the conditional Retry-failed button + status badge (broadcast-detail.tsx:247-268), and Retry is explicitly hidden while scheduled/queued/running. (4) The list-row delete button is disabled while running/queued with tooltip "Wait for the broadcast to finish" (broadcast-delete-button.tsx:32, 67-71). (5) The server delete-refusal copy literally instructs "Cancel the broadcast (or wait for it to finish) before deleting it." (broadcasts.service.ts:744) and is surfaced verbatim in the toast (broadcast-delete-button.tsx:46-52) — pointing the user at a control the UI never exposes. Not by_design: no locked decision, doc, or memory entry defers the cancel UI; the backend docstrings, capability gating, and error copy all assume an operator-facing cancel exists, so this is a forgotten frontend hook-up, not a tradeoff. User impact is concrete: with SEND_GAP_MS pacing a few-hundred-recipient run takes minutes, and an operator who notices a wrong audience/template mid-flight has no in-product way to stop irreversible customer-visible WhatsApp sends (only a raw curl with a session cookie would work); scheduled broadcasts have a delete escape hatch, queued/running do not. Severity corrected high → medium per the calibration bar: this is not a correctness bug or outage hit in normal use — the system behaves as designed and the damage path requires an operator mistake — so it is "real but bounded" (mistake-recovery path only). It sits at the top of medium because the consequence when triggered is irreversible bulk customer sends and the product's own error message advertises the missing control.

### 3. Team suspension does not disconnect live sockets — suspended org keeps real-time read access to customer conversations

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/api/src/admin/admin-teams.controller.ts:98-106` · **Area:** authn

**Root cause:** When a superAdmin suspends an org via `PATCH /api/admin/teams/:id/status`, the handler only walks the member list and calls `invalidateSessionCache(m.id)` for each user. It never calls `disconnectUserSockets`/`SessionInvalidationService.revoke`. The HTTP path is correctly cut (the next request mis-hits the busted cache, the slow path reloads the user with `team.status`, and `SessionGuard` throws `org_not_active` within ~15s — session.guard.ts:92-94), but a member who already has a live Socket.io connection keeps it. The gateway's `@SubscribeMessage` handlers read `client.data.teamId`/`userId` stamped at handshake and never re-check team status per event, and re-auth only runs on a fresh handshake.

**Impact:** An org suspended by platform staff (non-payment, abuse, TOS) retains live real-time visibility into its in-flight customer WhatsApp conversations on any tab that was open at suspension time, for as long as that connection stays alive. HTTP mutations are blocked, but the streamed read access contradicts the suspension boundary and the handler's own stated behavior.

**Recommended fix:** On the `suspended` transition, disconnect the org's live sockets in addition to busting the cache. Inject `SessionInvalidationService` into `AdminTeamsController` and, for each member, call `this.sessionInvalidator.revoke(m.id, 'suspension')` when `body.status === 'suspended'` (revoke already busts the cache AND calls disconnectUserSockets). Keep the cache-only bust for the `active` (approve/reactivate) transition so re-activated members aren't needlessly kicked. Add a 'suspension' reason to the revoke call's logging.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced the claim from the code. The suspension handler (PATCH /api/admin/teams/:id/status) only calls invalidateSessionCache per member — it never injects SessionInvalidationService nor calls disconnectUserSockets, so already-connected Socket.io clients of a suspended org keep their connection and team-room membership. Socket auth runs only at handshake (socket-auth.service.ts doc-comment lines 32-36; org-status gate at lines 139-141 is handshake-slow-path only); no per-event or periodic re-check exists (no setInterval/sweep in realtime.gateway.ts or ws-adapter.ts; no team.status domain event/fanout). @SubscribeMessage handlers trust client.data.teamId/userId stamped at connect, and the socket auto-joins teamRoom on connect (gateway line 219), so live fanout (new customer messages, etc.) keeps streaming. Not by_design: every sibling privilege-revoking transition (deactivation, role change, deletion, team-deletion, password change, sign-out) uses SessionInvalidationService.revoke which busts cache AND disconnects sockets (callers at users.service.ts:293/351, team-root.service.ts:97, change-password.controller.ts:148, internal-session.controller.ts:65), the gateway's own disconnectUserSockets doc-comment (realtime.gateway.ts:847-850) names this exact 'already-open tabs keep receiving live team events' failure mode, and the suspension handler's own comment claims 'a suspend cuts API + socket access on the very next request' — true only for HTTP and NEW handshakes, false for live sockets. Web-side mitigation is only an RSC redirect to /pending on navigation ((app)/layout.tsx:60) — client-cooperative, not a server boundary. Severity stays medium: bounded to the org's own data (no cross-tenant leak), read-only (HTTP mutations 403 within the 15s window or immediately post-bust), requires a tab open at suspension time, and self-heals on any reconnect (re-handshake fails after the cache bust), tab close, or sleep past the 30s recovery window — but on a stable connection a suspended org retains live real-time read access to in-flight customer WhatsApp conversations indefinitely, contradicting a platform enforcement action.

### 4. Workflows nav item is visible to every role but the index page calls an admin-only API, so agents/managers land on a generic error boundary

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/web/src/app/(app)/workflows/page.tsx:27-31` · **Area:** authz-capabilities

**Root cause:** GET /api/team/workflows is @RequireRole("admin") (admin OR superAdmin via canManageUsers; managers and agents are excluded), but the web side has two drifts: (1) the AppRail's PRIMARY_ITEMS statically includes { href: "/workflows" } with no role filtering — the items useMemo only appends Settings — so every agent sees a first-class Workflows nav icon; (2) /workflows/page.tsx computes canManage = canManageUsers(user.role) but only uses it to hide the 'New workflow' button — it does NOT redirect non-admins, and unconditionally calls listWorkflows() → api("/api/team/workflows"), which returns 403, and api() throws ApiError for any non-401 non-OK status. The RSC render throws and the route's error boundary shows a generic failure.

**Impact:** No security impact (the API correctly 403s), but a guaranteed bad-UX path the pilot's agents and managers WILL hit in normal use: a permanently visible rail icon that resolves to "Workflows failed to load" with a useless Retry button. It reads as an outage, generates support noise, and retry-spams a 403. Also surfaces a quieter product inconsistency: managers default to ALL capabilities yet are excluded from even viewing workflows.

**Recommended fix:** Pick one consistent posture: either (a) filter the /workflows rail item by canManageUsers(role) (the role is already available in app-rail via currentUser) AND add `if (!canManageUsers(user.role)) redirect("/inbox")` to workflows/page.tsx, mirroring its own sub-pages; or (b) if agents should see workflows read-only (e.g. to find manual-trigger workflows), relax the GET list route and keep mutations admin-only. Option (a) is the 5-line fix consistent with every sibling page.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced every link of the claim from the code. (1) GET /api/team/workflows is @RequireRole("admin"); RoleGuard maps that to canManageUsers(role) which passes only admin/superAdmin — managers and agents receive 403 (ForbiddenException). Workflows is also absent from the capability matrix (ALL_CAPABILITIES), so an admin cannot grant managers/agents access via the role-permissions grid. (2) AppRail's PRIMARY_ITEMS statically includes /workflows and the items useMemo only appends Settings — no role filtering — so every manager/agent sees a first-class Workflows nav icon (only superAdmin is redirected out of the (app) shell). (3) /workflows/page.tsx computes canManage but uses it only to hide the New-workflow button; it unconditionally awaits listWorkflows(), and api() throws ApiError on any non-OK non-401 status, so the RSC render throws for managers/agents and lands in workflows/error.tsx → SegmentError ("Workflows failed to load." + Try again, which reset()s into the same 403). (4) The drift is demonstrably unintentional, not a design choice: both sibling pages (/workflows/new, /workflows/[id]) gate with `if (!canManageUsers(user.role)) redirect("/workflows")` — redirecting non-admins to the one workflows page that errors on them — and no middleware/layout gates the path. No locked product decision covers workflows nav visibility. Impact: no security hole (API correctly 403s), but a guaranteed user-visible dead-end every pilot manager/agent hits by clicking an always-visible rail icon; it reads as an outage and the Retry button re-spams the 403. Severity medium is correct: bounded to one page, recoverable ("Back to inbox" escape), no data/auth/security impact — does not meet the high bar of an outage or core-flow correctness bug. Fix is the 5-line option (a): filter the rail item by canManageUsers and add the same redirect the sibling pages already have (or deliberately relax the GET to read-only for all roles).

### 5. Legacy Meta webhook proxy buffers the entire POST body into memory with no size cap (req.arrayBuffer), and neither Caddy nor Next.js caps it → unauthenticated remote OOM of the web (auth/pages) container

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/web/src/app/api/webhooks/meta/[teamId]/route.ts:98` · **Area:** webhook-security

**Root cause:** The still-live legacy proxy (Caddy routes `/api/webhooks/meta/*` → web:3000 per Caddyfile block (4)) reads the whole request body with `await req.arrayBuffer()` before forwarding to NestJS. Next.js App Router route handlers impose no default body-size limit on manual stream reads (the old pages-router bodyParser sizeLimit does not apply), and the Caddyfile has no `request_body { max_size }` directive, so the buffer is unbounded. The endpoint is unauthenticated (signature is verified downstream on NestJS, but only AFTER the proxy has already buffered the full body). The web container is capped at mem_limit 2g / heap 1536MB; a multi-hundred-MB to GB POST forces a contiguous ArrayBuffer allocation that can OOM-kill the container.

**Impact:** An unauthenticated attacker who knows the legacy path can repeatedly POST oversized bodies to OOM-kill the web container (login + all pages). restart:unless-stopped recovers in seconds but the attack is trivially repeatable, keeping the user-facing surface flapping. The file is scheduled for deletion 2026-06-19 (9 days out) but is fully live now.

**Recommended fix:** Cap the proxy body before buffering: stream-read with a hard byte ceiling (e.g. reject >10mb to match the NestJS cap) and return 413, or add `request_body { max_size 10MB }` to the `/api/webhooks/meta/*` handle in Caddyfile.template. Better still, accelerate deletion of this proxy now that the canonical `/webhooks/meta/*` NestJS route exists, if access logs show no legacy hits.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced from code. The still-live legacy Meta webhook proxy at apps/web/src/app/api/webhooks/meta/[teamId]/route.ts buffers the entire POST body with `await req.arrayBuffer()` (line 98) and no length guard, then copies it again with `Buffer.from(body)` (line 104), so peak memory is ~2x the body size. Nothing upstream caps it: Caddyfile block (4) reverse-proxies /api/webhooks/meta/* to web:3000 with no `request_body { max_size }` (grep for max_size/request_body in deploy/ is empty), and Next.js App Router route handlers impose no default body-size limit on manual stream reads. The endpoint is unauthenticated at the web layer (HMAC is verified downstream on NestJS) and proxy.ts treats /api/webhooks/* as public with NO edge rate-limit (its limiter covers only /login, /register, /api/invites/, POST /api/auth/*). The asymmetry is real: the canonical NestJS path is protected by bodyParser limit "10mb" (main.ts:150-151), and the api ip-rate-limit explicitly skips /webhooks/* (main.ts:127) and runs downstream of the web buffer anyway. The web container is mem_limit 2g; a single ~1.8GB POST, or a few concurrent ~100MB POSTs (doubled by Buffer.from), pushes RSS past the cgroup ceiling → OOM-kill (exit 137). The --max-old-space-size=1536 heap cap does not bound this since ArrayBuffers/Buffers are off-heap native memory; the cgroup is the only ceiling. restart:unless-stopped recovers but the attack is trivially repeatable, flapping login + pages. Severity is medium (not high): it is a deliberate attack rather than normal-use, impact is bounded to the web/pages/auth container (the NestJS api owning webhooks/socket/inbox stays up, active websocket sessions keep working), it auto-recovers, single pilot tenant, and the file is scheduled for deletion 2026-06-19. Not critical: no cross-tenant leak, auth bypass, data loss, or double-send. Fix is the one already used on the canonical path: a 10mb body cap (stream-read with a hard ceiling → 413) or a `request_body { max_size 10MB }` directive on the Caddy handle, or accelerate deletion of the proxy.

### 6. Meta access token embedded in URL query string leaks into logs and the HTTP 502 error response on upload-session timeout

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/api/src/lib/providers/meta.ts:1110-1118, 101-103` · **Area:** crypto-secrets

**Root cause:** uploadHeaderMedia is the ONLY Meta call that puts the plaintext access token in the URL: `startUrl.searchParams.set("access_token", config.accessToken)` (meta.ts:1116). Every other call uses an Authorization header. metaFetch's timeout branch builds its error as `meta request timed out after ${META_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS}ms: ${input}` (meta.ts:101-103) — `input` is the full URL object, so the message contains the decrypted token. When both attempts time out (Meta blip, 20s default), the error propagates to WhatsappService.uploadHeaderMedia's catch (apps/api/src/team/whatsapp/whatsapp.service.ts:669-674) which (a) logs it via `this.logger.error("template media upload failed", err)` — token lands in server logs — and (b) returns the error message in the 502 BadGatewayException detail to the caller.

**Impact:** On a Meta-side timeout, the team's long-lived WhatsApp Cloud API access token (full send/template/media control of the business number) is written to server logs and returned in a 502 body to any team member with the default templates:manage capability — undoing the envelope-encryption + decrypt-on-demand posture for that credential.

**Recommended fix:** Send the token via header on the upload-session call too — Graph API accepts `Authorization: Bearer` on /{app-id}/uploads (drop the access_token query param). Belt-and-braces: in metaFetch's timeout message, strip the query string (`new URL(input).origin + pathname`) so no future query-param credential can leak through error text.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced the full leak chain from code. uploadHeaderMedia's upload-session start call is the ONLY Meta call placing the decrypted access token in the URL query string (verified via repo-wide grep; all other calls use Authorization headers). metaFetch's timeout branch interpolates the full URL object into the Error message, so after both attempts abort (META_FETCH_MAX_ATTEMPTS=2, 20s default each), the thrown error message contains the plaintext token. In WhatsappService.uploadHeaderMedia's catch, the timeout error is a plain Error — not MissingAppIdError and not MetaSendError (throwIfMetaSendError only re-throws MetaSendError instances) — so it reaches logger.error (token persisted to stdout/docker logs in plaintext, undoing the envelope-encryption decrypt-on-demand posture) and is then returned verbatim as `detail` in a BadGatewayException 502 body. The only global exception filter (PrismaExceptionFilter, @Catch scoped to Prisma error classes) does not intercept HttpExceptions, and no redaction/masking helper exists. Severity stays medium, not high: trigger requires a double Meta-side timeout (edge condition, not normal pilot use), and exposure is bounded — the 502 recipient is an authenticated same-team user already holding templates:manage (though the raw token grants full send/template/media control of the business number outside the platform's capability/audit/rate-limit model), plus operator-readable logs. Not cross-tenant, not unauthenticated. Fix is cheap and matches the finding's suggestion: use Authorization header on the /uploads session call (the adjacent leg-2 call already does `OAuth <token>` via header) and strip query strings from metaFetch's timeout message.

### 7. Global message search ORs an un-indexed mediaCaption arm, making Message_body_trgm_idx unusable — and the arm is provably redundant

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/api/src/lib/queries/global-search.ts:163-183` · **Area:** db-queries-indexes

**Root cause:** searchAllMessages (the 'Messages' tab of global inbox search) filters `teamId AND (body ILIKE '%q%' OR mediaCaption ILIKE '%q%') ORDER BY timestamp DESC LIMIT 26`. Postgres can only use indexes for an OR via BitmapOr, which requires an index on EVERY arm; mediaCaption has no trgm (or any) index, so the planner can never use Message_body_trgm_idx for this query — its only intended consumer (schema.prisma:952-961 says the GIN is 'for the GLOBAL inbox-search Messages tab' and rationalizes skipping a caption index because 'the body index already covers the common case', which is wrong for an OR: one un-indexed arm disables the whole bitmap path). The planner falls back to walking Message_teamId_…

**Impact:** Messages-tab search degrades to O(team's total message count) per keystroke for selective/no-match terms (every Message row of the tenant walked + heap-filtered). Pilot-bounded today (tens of ms at ~100k rows) but it is the first search-latency cliff as message volume grows — and meanwhile the body trgm GIN is maintained on every Message insert (the hottest-write table) while serving zero query pl…

**Recommended fix:** Drop the mediaCaption OR-arm from searchAllMessages — body always contains the caption (invariant upheld at all three write sites and documented in the schema), so results are identical and the planner can BitmapAnd Message_body_trgm_idx with the teamId filter. Optionally add the same ≥3-char minimum the team-chat search controller enforces. (Adding a mediaCaption trgm GIN would also work but maintains a second index over data already present in body.) Same simplification applies to the conversa…

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced all three legs of the claim. (1) Query shape: searchAllMessages filters `{ teamId, OR: [body ILIKE, mediaCaption ILIKE] }` ordered (timestamp DESC, id DESC). (2) Index reality: Message_body_trgm_idx is the only trgm index on Message; mediaCaption has no index in any migration (checked 0_init plus all 10 later migrations). Postgres can only serve an OR via BitmapOr, which requires an indexable path for EVERY arm — one un-indexed arm forces the whole OR to a heap filter, so the body trgm GIN is unusable for its sole intended consumer (the schema comment at 952-961 names this exact query as the index's purpose and at 962-968 claims 'selective terms still ride the trgm path', which the OR structurally prevents). The fallback plan walks Message_teamId_timestamp_id_idx newest-first filtering every row — O(team's total messages) for selective/no-match search terms. (3) Redundancy: the OR arm provably never adds results — every write site stores the identical string in body and mediaCaption (ingest 576/587, media send 1419/1438, forward 1876/1896) and the only other writers NULL the caption while body keeps it (meta.controller 356-368 'caption preserved', inbound-media sweeper 95), so mediaCaption ⊆ body always; the captionMatched snippet branch at global-search.ts:211-214 is consequently dead code. Not by_design: no locked decision covers it, and the schema comment's rationale ('the body index already covers the common case') is a false premise about OR index semantics that contradicts the adjacent comment's stated intent. The repo itself fixed the identical bug class for the Contacts search by adding Contact_email_trgm_idx in migration 20260601130000. Severity stays medium: results are correct and latency is pilot-bounded today, but it is a growth-linear search cliff plus a GIN trgm index paying write cost on the hottest table while serving zero query plans. The proposed fix (drop the mediaCaption OR arm) is sound and result-identical.

### 8. Team suspension never disconnects live sockets — suspended org keeps full realtime read access on open tabs

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/api/src/admin/admin-teams.controller.ts:98-106` · **Area:** realtime-server

**Root cause:** The superAdmin suspend endpoint only busts the 15s session cache (`invalidateSessionCache(m.id)` per member) and never calls `SessionInvalidationService.revoke()`, which is the method that exists precisely to 'drop the cache AND disconnect every socket' and is used by every other revocation path: user deactivation/role-change (users.service.ts:293), user deletion (users.service.ts:351), team deletion (team-root.service.ts:97), password change (change-password.controller.ts:148), and signout via the internal bridge (internal-session.controller.ts:65). Socket auth (including the team-status gate at socket-auth.service.ts:139-141) runs ONLY at handshake; an already-connected socket is never re-validated.

**Impact:** A suspended (abusive / revoked / non-paying) organization retains live read access to its inbound WhatsApp stream and all realtime team data for as long as any tab stays open — potentially days — defeating the org-approval/suspension gate the platform shell enforces. Same-tenant data only (not a cross-tenant leak), which is why this is medium rather than high.

**Recommended fix:** In `AdminTeamsController.setStatus`, when `body.status === "suspended"`, inject `SessionInvalidationService` and call `revoke(m.id, "suspension")` for each member instead of bare `invalidateSessionCache` (revoke already does the cache bust). That reuses the exact pattern deactivation uses; sockets attempting to reconnect will then fail the teamStatus check in socket-auth.service.ts:140.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced the finding from the code. AdminTeamsController.setStatus (suspend) only calls the bare invalidateSessionCache(m.id) per member — it never injects SessionInvalidationService or calls revoke(), which is the one method that disconnects live sockets (revoke -> realtime.disconnectUserSockets). Socket team-status enforcement exists only in the handshake middleware (socket-auth.service.ts:139-141); a stable, already-connected Socket.io connection never re-runs it, and grep across realtime/ and lib/events/ confirms no other mechanism (event subscriber, sweeper, per-frame guard) kicks suspended-team sockets. Every comparable revocation path DOES call revoke(): role-change/deactivation (users.service.ts:293, whose comment explicitly names lingering sockets as the failure mode), user deletion (users.service.ts:351), team deletion (team-root.service.ts:97), password change (change-password.controller.ts:148), signout bridge (internal-session.controller.ts:65). Not by_design: the controller's own comment claims a suspend "cuts API + socket access on the very next request" — the stated intent is immediate cutoff, but a live socket makes no next request, so the implementation only covers future handshakes (invalidateSessionCache does clear both the userId and cookie-hash caches, so reconnects are correctly rejected). Impact: a suspended org's open tabs retain full realtime READ access (inbound WhatsApp stream, team events) until the socket naturally drops — potentially days — defeating the timeliness of the suspension gate. Bounded factors keep it medium: same-tenant data only (no cross-tenant leak), HTTP write/read access dies within ~15s via SessionGuard, suspension is a rare superAdmin operator action, and exposure ends on any reconnect/reload. Reporter's severity (medium) is correct. Fix is the one-liner the finding proposes: call sessionInvalidator.revoke(m.id, "suspension") per member (revoke already busts the cache; reconnect handshakes then fail the teamStatus gate).

### 9. Render-body cache seed re-runs on every render, resurrecting the stale SSR thread snapshot after every eviction of the deep-linked conversation

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/web/src/features/inbox/components/inbox-shell.tsx:325-328` · **Area:** realtime-client-state

**Root cause:** The `else if (initialThread && initialActiveConversationId && !cache.has(initialActiveConversationId)) { cache.set(initialActiveConversationId, initialThread); }` branch is commented "First-render cache seed" but runs on EVERY render where the entry is missing. `initialActiveConversationId`/`initialThread` stay constant for the whole client session (page.tsx doesn't re-run on client-side chat switches). So after the shell's own freshness machinery deletes that entry — evictIfBackground on message:new/note:new/message:failed (lines 662-666), onConnect's `cache.clearExcept(displayedIdRef.current)` after a reconnect (lines 754-760), or onConversationDeleted (685-688) — the very next shell render re-seeds the stale SSR snapshot.

**Impact:** Clicking back to the deep-linked conversation after it received messages in the background paints an SSR-era snapshot (potentially hours old — missing messages, old notes/contact/status) for one round-trip until the initialMayBeStale full refetch converges. This is exactly the flash-of-stale class the snapshot-on-leave + eviction design exists to kill, and it recurs all day on the most-used thread.

**Recommended fix:** Gate the seed to run once: `const seededRef = useRef(false); ... else if (!seededRef.current && initialThread && ...) { seededRef.current = true; cache.set(...); }` — the lastSyncedInitialId sync block (which fires on a genuine new SSR value) already re-seeds and can reset the flag.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced the full mechanism from the code. The seed at inbox-shell.tsx:325-328 is render-body code guarded only by `!cache.has(id)` — no once-guard exists — while `initialThread`/`initialActiveConversationId` are RSC props that stay frozen for the whole client session (confirmed by the code's own comment at :303-312: openConversation switches threads in pure client state, page.tsx never re-runs). Therefore every freshness eviction of the deep-linked id (evictIfBackground :662-666 on background message:new/note:new/message:failed; onConversationDeleted :685-688; reconnect clearExcept :754-760) is undone on the very next shell render — and that render is deterministic, because the same message:new frame that triggers eviction also fires useTeamEvents.onMessageNew → setConversations (use-team-events.ts:575,611), re-rendering the shell in the same tick. Click-back then hits openConversation :425-435, which treats the resurrected entry as a cache HIT (ThreadCache.has is a bare Map.has, thread-cache.ts:34-36, no TTL) and synchronously paints the SSR-page-load-era snapshot — missing all messages/notes/status/assignment changes since the hard load, including the inbound the agent just clicked to read. This directly defeats the documented invariant at :645-648 ("evict → fresh fetch… never stale"). Impact is bounded: displayedFromCache=true → initialMayBeStale → runFullRefetch fires immediately on mount (use-conversation-events.ts:998 → :716, delay 0 at :721), so the stale paint self-heals in one round trip with no data loss or wrong writes. Not covered by any locked decision (the comment itself shows re-seeding was never the intent) nor by the 2026-06-09 audit's don't-re-flag items. Deterministic, daily-recurring on the most-used thread (every hard refresh creates a deep-linked ?c= thread), but a one-RTT self-healing visual staleness = medium, matching the reported severity. The proposed once-guard fix (reset via the lastSyncedInitialId sync block :314-324) is sound.

### 10. Reconnect-recovery jitter timer is never cleared on unmount — a leaked runFullRefetch can markRead a thread the agent already left, clearing team-wide unread for a message nobody saw

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/web/src/features/inbox/hooks/use-conversation-events.ts:716-722` · **Area:** realtime-client-state

**Root cause:** onConnect schedules the recovery with `window.setTimeout(() => recover(), delay)` where delay is 0-1500ms jitter on a reconnect. The timeout handle is not stored and the effect cleanup (lines 1418-1448) clears statusFlushRaf, activityRefreshTimer and listeners but not this timer. If the agent switches threads inside the jitter window, the old hook unmounts but the timer still fires runFullRefetch for the OLD conversationId: the fetch runs, the setData is a silent no-op on the unmounted hook, but the side effect at lines 940-946 still executes — `if (document.visibilityState === "visible" && fresh.conversation.unreadCount > 0) void markRead();` — POSTing /read for a conversation the agent is no longer viewing.

**Impact:** Narrow window (reconnect + chat-switch within ≤1.5s + inbound on the abandoned thread in that window), but the consequence is the project's most-guarded invariant: team-wide unread silently cleared for a customer message no one viewed, dropping it from triage. Reconnects are exactly when inbound bursts arrive (queued webhooks), making the coincidence less unlikely than it looks. Secondary: each leaked timer also burns a full-thread fetch.

**Recommended fix:** Store the timeout id in a variable scoped to the effect (or a ref) and clearTimeout it in the cleanup; alternatively guard recover()'s markRead calls with `dataRef.current.conversation.id === conversationId` plus an `unmounted` flag set in the cleanup.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced the full chain from code. (1) use-conversation-events.ts:721-722 schedules the reconnect recovery via `window.setTimeout(() => recover(), delay)` with a 0-1500ms jitter and discards the handle; the effect cleanup (1418-1448) clears statusFlushRaf, activityRefreshTimer, and all listeners but never this timer. (2) The thread is keyed by displayedId (inbox-shell.tsx:1074), so a chat switch unmounts the hook — the leaked timer fires runFullRefetch with the OLD conversationId closure. (3) runFullRefetch's fetch has no abort coupling (apiFetch/fetchWithSessionGuard are thin wrappers); setData is a no-op post-unmount, but lines 940-946 still execute `void markRead()` because document.visibilityState IS "visible" (the agent is visible — on a different thread) and fresh.conversation.unreadCount > 0. markRead (618-625, useCoalescedAsync with no unmount guard) POSTs /api/conversations/{oldId}/read. (4) Server-side (conversations.service.ts:653-705) the CAS zeros the TEAM-WIDE unreadCount, publishes conversation.read (clearing the badge for every agent), and sends a Meta read receipt to the customer. This violates the documented invariant "markRead fires ONLY when the agent is actually viewing — visible AND on the thread" and is NOT covered by the locked mount-time over-call decision (that one is safe only because the server short-circuits already-read; here unread is genuinely >0 and is wrongly cleared, plus a blue tick goes to the customer for a message nobody read). Severity stays medium: the trigger is a tight conjunction (reconnect while on thread A + chat-switch within ~1.5s jitter + fetch latency + unseen inbound on A in the gap), and the conversation row still surfaces in the list via lastMessageAt/preview — only the unread/triage badge signal is silently lost. Real but bounded/edge → medium, not high. The proposed fix (store the timeout id, clearTimeout in cleanup, or guard recover with an unmounted flag) is correct and trivial.

### 11. contacts:bulk_updated never converges the DISPLAYED thread — shell skips it, live hook doesn't subscribe, and the contact panel's softRefresh self-heal is a no-op because ThreadWorkspace renders from the LRU cache

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/web/src/features/inbox/components/inbox-shell.tsx:676-684` · **Area:** realtime-client-state

**Root cause:** The documented contract (thread-reducers.ts:384 and CLAUDE.md 'Coalesced bulk fanout') is that bulk frames evict cached snapshots and clients refetch. Three pieces miss the displayed thread: (1) the shell's onContactsBulkUpdated explicitly skips it — `if (id === displayedIdRef.current) continue;` (inbox-shell.tsx:681); (2) useConversationEvents has no contacts:bulk_updated subscription at all (MANUAL_LIVE_HOOK_EVENTS at use-conversation-events.ts:1255-1263 lists only message:new/status/failed, note:new, conversation:activity/deleted, connect), so the live `data.contact` (header stage stepper, reply-box token resolution, snippet $var.contact.*) never updates; (3) ContactPanel DOES listen but its softRefresh self-heal is a no-op because ThreadWorkspace renders from the LRU cache.

**Impact:** A teammate's bulk tag/stage/field operation that includes the contact currently open in another agent's thread leaves that agent's contact panel, header stage stepper, and snippet token resolution stale until they switch chats and back (the cache-hit full refetch finally reconciles) or reconnect. The dead softRefresh also burns a full inbox re-SSR (the heavy ?c= page) for zero effect. Bounded and self-healing on chat-switch.

**Recommended fix:** Subscribe useConversationEvents to contacts:bulk_updated: when payload.contactIds includes dataRef.current.contact.id, fetch `/api/contacts/lookup?ids=<id>` and run the existing applyContactUpdate reducer (add contacts:bulk_updated handling to the exclusions doc). Then ContactPanel's per-contact contact:updated path receives the patched prop and the softRefresh call can be dropped.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced all three legs of the claim. (1) The shell's bulk handler explicitly skips the displayed thread (inbox-shell.tsx:678 `if (id === displayedIdRef.current) continue;`) on the stated assumption that useConversationEvents keeps it fresh (comment at lines 638-639). (2) That assumption is false for this event: useConversationEvents subscribes only to MANUAL_LIVE_HOOK_EVENTS (use-conversation-events.ts:1255-1263, no contacts:bulk_updated) plus THREAD_REDUCER_EVENTS (thread-reducers.ts:317-333, no contacts:bulk_updated — it's in REDUCER_EXCLUSIONS at line 384, whose coverage 'shell evicts, list refetches' structurally omits the displayed thread). Server bulk paths suppress the per-contact contact:updated socket frame (contacts.service.ts:602 suppressSocketFanout:true, skipped at fanout-rules.ts:192) and emit only an ids-only coalesced frame (contacts.service.ts:627-633), so no other frame converges the live thread's data.contact. (3) ContactPanel's clean-path self-heal (contact-panel.tsx:557-558 softRefresh = router.refresh()) is a no-op for its rendered state: its contact prop is thread.data.contact from the client LRU cache (inbox-shell.tsx:1276-1277, contact-panel.tsx:145), and the SSR sync block (inbox-shell.tsx:313-328) discards a refreshed initialThread when the id is unchanged and cached — in the common path Next's router never even saw the replaceState ?c=, so initialThread is null. The re-seed effect (contact-panel.tsx:324-350) therefore never fires; the comment at 536-537 claiming it does is wrong, and the refresh wastes a full inbox RSC pass. Concrete user-visible impact: after a teammate's bulk tag/stage/field op including the open contact, the conversation LIST converges (use-team-events.ts:965-993 refetches fresh rows) while the open thread's contact panel, header stage stepper (message-thread.tsx:1485), and snippet $var.contact.* token resolution (reply-box.tsx:351-371 resolves against the live contact) stay stale until chat-switch-back (cache-hit → initialMayBeStale → runFullRefetch, use-conversation-events.ts:716) or reconnect — a visible list-vs-thread inconsistency plus possible stale values inserted into an outbound snippet. Not by_design: the locked decision is coalescing the bulk fanout (1 frame per batch), which the finding's fix preserves; the displayed-thread gap contradicts the code's own documented contract. Severity medium is correct per the bar — real but bounded/edge (requires concurrent bulk op touching the currently-open contact, self-heals on switch/reconnect, no data loss or customer-send fault).

### 12. ask_question inbound-resume bypasses the stale/not-due guard on job-kind alone, allowing premature + double execution of a post-wait step when the timeout job wins the race

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/api/src/lib/workflows/runner.ts:201-208` · **Area:** workflow-engine

**Root cause:** The stale/not-due resume guard (runner.ts:201-208) skips a `waiting` run whose `waitUntil` is still in the future ONLY when `!input.isInboundResume`. For an inbound (`ask_question` early-reply) job the guard is unconditionally bypassed (worker.ts:153 sets `isInboundResume = job.id.startsWith("inbound-")`). The guard never re-verifies that the run is actually parked on the ask_question step. Nothing cancels the ask_question TIMEOUT job (`resume-${runId}-${waitSeq}`) when the contact replies (queue.ts:154-162 documents this explicitly). Consider a workflow `ask_question → wait Nh → side-effect step`: if a reply lands just before the ask_question timeout deadline, BOTH the inbound-resume job an…

**Impact:** Under the (narrow) reply≈timeout race, a customer-visible follow-up send after the ask_question fires twice and ahead of schedule. This is the residual the project memory already tracks as the 'ask_question lock TTL' / 'chat-send-receive-2 recovery path' item. The run lock reduces but does not eliminate it because the inbound bypass blindly trusts currentStepId.

**Recommended fix:** When `isInboundResume` is true, gate execution on the run actually being parked on an ask_question awaiting a reply — e.g. require a live `WorkflowAwaitingReply` row for this run (or that `currentStepId` resolves to an `ask_question` node with a trailing `waiting` stepLog entry) before bypassing the not-due guard; otherwise treat it like a stale resume and no-op.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced the core claim from the code (file lives at apps/api/src/lib/workflows/runner.ts as cited). The not-due guard (runner.ts:201-208) is bypassed for any job whose id starts with "inbound-" (worker.ts:147-154), and the runner never re-verifies that the run is actually parked on an ask_question awaiting a reply — the only workflowAwaitingReply reference in the runner is the park-time upsert (runner.ts:593). Reproducible interleaving for graph ask_question→wait Nh→side-effect: (1) reply lands seconds before the ask_question timeout deadline; ingest consumes the awaiting row, sets pendingAnswer, enqueues inbound-${runId}-${msgId} (resume-on-inbound.ts:50-77); (2) the dangling timeout job resume-${runId}-${seq} (never cancelled — queue.ts:154-162) is due, passes the guard, sees pendingAnswer, takes the "answered" branch (ask-question.ts:246-338), advances through the wait and parks with currentStepId=next side-effect step and waitUntil=now+Nh (runner.ts:512-543); (3) the inbound job then acquires the now-free run lock, bypasses the not-due guard on job-kind alone, sets status=running, and executes the post-wait step IMMEDIATELY — the wait is silently skipped and downstream steps (incl. customer-visible WhatsApp sends) fire hours/days early. The ordering is realistic: the per-team concurrency cap defers jobs via moveToDelayed(+250ms) (worker.ts:136-145). HOWEVER, the "double execution" half of the title is overstated and refuted: the Redis run lock (runner.ts:80-103, queue.ts:177-227 with heartbeat renewal at TTL/3) serializes lanes (loser returns "skipped" without touching state), and a sequential second lane reloads the persisted currentStepId, so the step executes once (prematurely), never twice; the later legitimate wait-resume job hits the terminal guard (runner.ts:169-175) or the not-due guard. Net: real correctness bug = premature execution / skipped wait under a narrow reply≈timeout race; not a double customer send, not data loss. Severity medium is correct (real but bounded timing edge: seconds-wide window around a deadline hours/days out). The suggested fix (require a live WorkflowAwaitingReply row or an ask_question currentStepId before honoring the inbound bypass) is sound and cheap. Not a locked decision — queue.ts's own comments claim the guard+lock cover the races, so this gap is an oversight, not a documented tradeoff; it is adjacent to but distinct from the already-tracked "ask_question lock TTL" residual (which the heartbeat renewal addressed).

### 13. Redis runs with maxmemory-policy=allkeys-lru — BullMQ requires noeviction; eviction silently corrupts queues, run-locks, and delayed jobs

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `docker-compose.yml:119` · **Area:** queues-jobs

**Root cause:** The redis service is started with `--maxmemory 200mb --maxmemory-policy allkeys-lru`. BullMQ hard-requires `noeviction` (the installed bullmq 5.76.10 console-warns 'IMPORTANT! Eviction policy is allkeys-lru. It should be "noeviction"' on every connection — redis-connection.js:434-435). Under memory pressure — the exact scenario the compose comment says the cap exists for ('a runaway pub/sub or stuck stream evicts cold entries instead of crashing the queue') — LRU evicts the COLDEST keys first, which are precisely: (a) delayed-job hashes for scheduled broadcasts queued days ahead (untouched since enqueue → prime eviction candidates → broadcast silently never fires; recovery is boot-only, see …

**Impact:** If Redis ever approaches 200mb, instead of loud enqueue failures (already handled by dispatcher retry + workflow-waiting sweeper), the system silently loses scheduled broadcasts, drops queued customer-visible message sends, and can double-execute non-idempotent workflow side effects (Meta sends) via evicted run-locks. Bounded today: steady-state working set is tiny (job IDs + small payloads), so e…

**Recommended fix:** Change to `--maxmemory-policy noeviction` (one word in docker-compose.yml). Keep the 200mb maxmemory; write failures will surface through the existing console error handlers, dispatcher retries, and sweepers, and the BullMQ boot warning disappears.

> Verifier (high confidence): Every element of the claim reproduces from the code. docker-compose.yml:119 runs redis with `--maxmemory 200mb --maxmemory-policy allkeys-lru`; the installed bullmq@5.76.10 hard-warns on every connection that the policy must be "noeviction" (redis-connection.js:434-435) because eviction silently deletes queue state rather than degrading gracefully. The compose comment (lines 114-118) justifying allkeys-lru as "evicts cold entries instead of crashing the queue" is premised on a misunderstanding: the coldest keys ARE the load-bearing ones — delayed-job hashes for scheduled broadcasts (recovery is boot-only, broadcasts.service.ts:67-90) and idle wf-run-lock keys (queue.ts:195-212, whose eviction breaks the single-flight guard whose own doc-comment says it prevents non-idempotent duplicate WhatsApp sends). The codebase itself acknowledges eviction as a real failure mode — workflow-waiting.ts:12 lists "Redis eviction" as a strand cause its 60s sweeper recovers — but that recovery covers ONLY workflow runs, not the broadcast-schedule queue, the message-sends queue (HTTP returns {queued:true} before the Meta send; an evicted job = silently dropped customer message, surfaced only by the client watchdog), or the run locks (eviction is unrecoverable for mutual exclusion). Not by_design: this isn't among the locked decisions, and a documented tradeoff whose premise is contradicted by the library's own runtime warning is a correctness flaw, not a deliberate one. The proposed fix is correct and safe: with noeviction, maxmemory 200mb still bounds the keyspace under the 256m mem_limit (no OOMKill risk), and writes past the cap fail loudly into the existing dispatcher-retry + sweeper recovery layers. Severity stays medium: the trigger (Redis nearing 200mb) is improbable at pilot scale — all four queues cap retention (removeOnComplete 24h/count 500-5000 tiny payloads, removeOnFail 7d), the event bus is in-process, and there is no Socket.io Redis adapter — so this is real-but-latent, with serious consequences (lost scheduled broadcasts, dropped queued sends, double Meta sends via evicted locks) only under an edge condition. One slight overstatement in the finding: actively-held run locks are kept LRU-hot by the TTL/3 heartbeat eval, lowering (not eliminating — Redis LRU is sampled/approximate) their eviction odds.

### 14. Scheduled broadcasts have boot-only recovery: 2-attempt fire exhaustion leaves status=scheduled forever; a partial first attempt's retry CAS-miss leaves status=queued forever

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/api/src/lib/broadcasts/schedule-worker.ts:32-58` · **Area:** queues-jobs

**Root cause:** Three stranding paths for a scheduled broadcast, all recovered ONLY by BroadcastsService.onModuleInit (i.e. the next deploy/restart): (1) The fire job has `attempts: 2, backoff: fixed 5s` (schedule-queue.ts:38-40) — a ~10s Postgres blip exactly at fire time exhausts both attempts; the `failed` listener (schedule-worker.ts:95-100) only logs, so the row stays `status=scheduled` and nothing ever fires it. (2) Partial first attempt: `fireScheduled` CAS-flips scheduled→queued (line 35-38), then does `db.broadcast.findUnique` + `publish` (lines 44-55) BEFORE `startBroadcast` (line 57). If the findUnique/publish throws after the CAS committed, the job fails and BullMQ retries — but the retry's CAS …

**Impact:** A scheduled campaign silently does not fire (or strands at `queued` having sent nothing) until the next deploy or VPS restart — for a pilot that may be days. The customer sees a broadcast that says 'scheduled'/'queued' and never goes out, with zero operator signal beyond a single console.warn from the failed job.

**Recommended fix:** Two small changes: (a) in fireScheduled, when the CAS matches 0 rows, check whether the row is `queued` and call startBroadcast(broadcastId) anyway (idempotent — runBroadcast's own queued→running CAS dedupes); also move the cosmetic publish after the startBroadcast handoff. (b) Add a runtime sweeper tick (copy workflow-waiting.ts) that re-enqueues `status=scheduled AND scheduledAt < now() - 60s` (idempotent jobId `bcast-<id>`) and re-fires `status=queued AND updatedAt < now() - grace` rows.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced both stranding paths from the code. (1) Attempt exhaustion: the fire job has attempts:2 / fixed 5s backoff (schedule-queue.ts:39-40); a DB/Redis blip spanning ~10s at fire time exhausts both attempts, the worker's 'failed' listener only logs (schedule-worker.ts:95-100), and the row stays status=scheduled with no runtime recovery — the only reconciler is BroadcastsService.onModuleInit (broadcasts.service.ts:51-91), i.e. the next deploy/restart. Verified no sweeper in apps/api/src/lib/sweepers/ touches Broadcast status and workflow-worker.service.ts has no broadcast reconcile tick. (2) Partial-attempt retry defeat: fireScheduled CAS-flips scheduled→queued (schedule-worker.ts:35-38), then runs findUnique (line 44) BEFORE the startBroadcast handoff (line 57). publish() cannot throw (bus.ts:168-203 swallows all subscriber + outbox errors), so findUnique is the one throwable statement post-CAS; if it throws (pool stall P2024 / Postgres restart), the BullMQ retry's CAS matches 0 rows (status is now 'queued') and the early return at lines 39-42 misclassifies this as 'Canceled, deleted, or already fired' — startBroadcast is never called and the broadcast strands at 'queued' having sent nothing, until the boot reconciler's queued-orphan sweep (broadcast-runner.ts:1476-1479, 1545-1550) at next restart. This is a genuine logic bug (the retry mechanism is structurally a no-op for any failure after the CAS commit), not a documented tradeoff: the author explicitly configured maxStalledCount:3 (schedule-worker.ts:87-92) BECAUSE 'row stays scheduled, fires never until restart' was deemed unacceptable for the stall variant, and the codebase's own pattern (lib/sweepers/workflow-waiting.ts, 60s tick) recovers the identical stranded-row-without-job class for workflows at runtime. Severity stays medium: real and customer-visible (a billed, scheduled campaign silently never sends, possibly for days), but bounded — the trigger needs an infra blip coinciding exactly with fire time, the UI shows the past-due scheduled/queued status, and manual recovery exists (cancel is valid on scheduled and queued, broadcasts.service.ts:607-619, then clone/recreate). Not 'high' because it is not hit in normal use.

### 15. Broadcast-schedule worker has no 'error' listener and no fatal-error self-respawn — the only one of the four BullMQ workers without it

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/api/src/lib/broadcasts/schedule-worker.ts:60-101` · **Area:** queues-jobs

**Root cause:** The workflow worker (lib/workflows/worker.ts:203-233), send worker (messages/send-worker.service.ts:113-133), and webhook worker (lib/outbound-webhooks/worker.ts:247-269) all register a Worker 'error' listener that detects fatal classes ('Connection is closed', WRONGPASS, NOAUTH), closes the wedged worker, and self-respawns after 1s — each with an explicit comment that without this 'one unrecoverable Redis blip stalls ALL processing until a manual restart'. startBroadcastScheduleWorker registers ONLY a 'failed' listener (lines 95-100). Consequences: (a) a Worker 'error' emission has no listener — Node EventEmitter throws, landing in the process-level uncaughtException handler (main.ts:51-53)…

**Impact:** After a Redis incident that wedges BullMQ workers, workflows/sends/webhooks recover automatically but the broadcast scheduler does not — scheduled broadcasts stop firing with no operator signal, compounding the boot-only-recovery gap in the previous finding. Same silent-customer-facing-failure mode, different trigger.

**Recommended fix:** Copy the ~25-line fatal-error self-respawn block from lib/workflows/worker.ts:203-233 into startBroadcastScheduleWorker (add a `shuttingDown` flag to its WorkerGlobals, mirror the close+disconnect+setTimeout respawn).

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced the core claim from the code. The broadcast-schedule worker is the only one of the four BullMQ workers without the fatal-Redis-error self-respawn block: schedule-worker.ts registers only a 'failed' listener, while lib/workflows/worker.ts:203-233, messages/send-worker.service.ts:113-133, and lib/outbound-webhooks/worker.ts:247-269 all detect fatal classes ("Connection is closed"/WRONGPASS/NOAUTH), close the wedged worker, and respawn after 1s — each with comments stating that without it "one unrecoverable Redis blip stalls ALL processing until a manual restart". The schedule worker is started exactly once at boot (workflow-worker.service.ts:255, onModuleInit) behind an `if (state.worker) return` guard (schedule-worker.ts:61) with no re-arm path, so a wedge persists until process restart. This is an omission, not a decision: schedule-worker.ts:74-75 explicitly states the intent to "Match every other worker's lock semantics (workflow/send/webhook...)", and lines 105-111 note this worker "previously had an UNCAPPED close" — a pattern of missed sibling-parity hardening. One correction to the finding's mechanism: the missing 'error' listener does NOT cause an EventEmitter throw into main.ts's uncaughtException handler — BullMQ 5.76.10's QueueBase.emit override (queue-base.js:89-103) catches ER_UNHANDLED_ERROR and falls back to console.error, so errors are still logged and the process never crashes. The substantive gap is solely the missing self-respawn. Impact is real but edge-triggered: after a fatal Redis error class, workflows/sends/webhooks auto-recover while scheduled broadcasts silently stop firing (rows stay `scheduled`, no operator signal) until a restart lets the boot reconciler recover them. Severity medium is correct — silent customer-facing scheduled-send failure, but requires a rare Redis incident class a pilot won't hit in normal use, and the boot reconciler + restart:unless-stopped bound the blast radius.

### 16. Inbound location and contact-card (vCard) messages silently dropped with no placeholder — customer content lost, window/unread not updated

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/api/src/lib/providers/meta.ts:559-562` · **Area:** messaging-provider
