**Root cause:** Customer messages of type `location` (a shared pin — common in support/delivery conversations), `contacts` (vCard), `order`, and `unsupported` have no parser branch and fall through the media-type gate at meta.ts:561-562, getting dropped with bare `continue`. Unlike the reaction skip (explicitly documented as deferred at meta.ts:550-557), these are undocumented fall-throughs. The webhook returns 200 (meta.controller.ts:251), so Meta never redelivers and there is no rawPayload row to recover from. As with the button-type drop: unreadCount, lastMessagePreview, and Contact.lastInboundAt are never updated (all happen only inside ingestInboundMessage, ingest.ts:642-669), so the agent gets no sign…

**Impact:** A customer sharing their address as a location pin, or forwarding a contact card, vanishes without trace — no bubble, no unread, no window refresh. Bounded to these message types (customers usually follow up with text), hence medium rather than high.

**Recommended fix:** Minimum viable: add a catch-all branch that ingests unknown non-status message types as a text row with a typed placeholder body (e.g. "📍 Location shared" with lat/long from m.location, "Contact card: <name>") and the raw payload preserved — this restores unread/window correctness even before proper rendering exists.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced from the code. The Meta parser (apps/api/src/lib/providers/meta.ts, parseWebhook) has exactly four message-producing branches: `text` (line 488), `interactive` button/list replies (line 511), an EXPLICIT documented skip for `reaction` (lines 550-557), and the media gate (lines 561-562) restricted to META_MEDIA_TYPES = ["image","video","audio","document","sticker"] (line 262). Inbound `location`, `contacts` (vCard), `order`, `button` (template quick-reply), and `unsupported` types match none of these and hit the bare `continue` at line 562 — no normalized event, no log line, nothing. The MetaMessage interface (lines 247-260) doesn't even model location/contacts fields, and a grep across apps/api/src confirms no other code path consumes these types. Downstream, meta.controller.ts returns 200 (`@HttpCode(200)` line 134; `if (events.length === 0) return { ok: true, ingested: 0 }` line 186), so Meta never redelivers, and there is no webhook-body persistence outside Message.rawPayload (no webhook log table exists in prisma/schema.prisma) — the content is unrecoverable. Because no event is ingested, the unreadCount increment, lastMessagePreview advance, and Contact.lastInboundAt bump (all inside ingestInboundMessage, ingest.ts:642-669) never run. I also verified the secondary impact: the in-app 24h window is computed from lastInboundAt (reply-box.tsx:174 computeWindowStatus), so a location-only inbound leaves the reply box stale-closed even though Meta's real window reopened. This is NOT by_design: it appears in no locked decision or deferred-features list, and the codebase's own comment at meta.ts:550-557 states that silently-dropped types are "indistinguishable from a parser bug" — which is exactly what these undocumented fall-throughs are. Severity stays MEDIUM: concrete user-visible silent loss of customer content (a shared address pin or contact card vanishes — customer sees delivered ticks, agent gets no bubble/unread/ding), but bounded to these less-common message types in text-dominant pilot conversations; not an outage, auth, or cross-tenant issue. The suggested fix (placeholder-body ingestion preserving rawPayload) is sound and would restore unread/window/preview correctness.

### 17. Cloud-API rate/limit error codes 130429 / 131048 / 131056 are not normalized to rate_limited — send-worker retry never engages AND the broadcast runner's entire 429 backoff machinery never fires on the errors a real broadcast actually hits

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/api/src/lib/providers/meta.ts:1616-1623` · **Area:** messaging-provider

**Root cause:** [Merged: reported independently by the messaging-provider and broadcasts-bulk auditors — same root cause, same file/lines.] normalizeMetaSendError maps only `numericCode === 4 || numericCode === 80007` to `rate_limited` (meta.ts:1616-1623); everything else unrecognized falls through to `provider_rejected` (1654-1659). But the WhatsApp Cloud API's actual limit errors are 130429 ("Rate limit hit" — per-second message throughput, the most common pacing rejection), 131048 ("Spam rate limit hit" — quality/messaging-limit restriction on the phone number), and 131056 (business↔consumer pair rate limit). None are mapped. Downstream consequences: (a) send-worker categorizeSendError marks them non-recoverable (send-worker.service.ts:292-295 — `recoverable = error === "rate_limited" || error === "send_failed" || status >= 500`; these come back as HTTP 400), so BullMQ never retries a purely-transient pacing rejection and the agent gets a permanent red bubble; (b) the broadcast runner's whole rate-limit defense — single 3s retry, per-broadcast 429 streak tracking, and the cross-lane 60s pauseAllLanes (broadcast-runner.ts:1021-1038) — is gated on `normalizeMetaSendError(err)?.code === "rate_limited"`, so on 130429/13… [truncated in source]

**Impact:** Under burst (broadcast + concurrent inbox sends), throughput-limit rejections become permanent failures instead of paced retries: red bubbles for agents, falsely-failed broadcast recipients, and continued hammering during a pair/spam rate-limit window. A pilot at messaging tier 1K sending a >1k broadcast, or a number whose quality rating dips mid-send, gets the entire remaining audience burned as `failed` in minutes with zero backoff — false-failed recipients on transient throughput errors, plus continued hammering of a spam-flagged number, which Meta penalizes with further quality-rating loss. Recoverable only via manual retry-failed after the …

**Recommended fix:** Map 130429, 131048, and 131056 to `rate_limited` in normalizeMetaSendError (one line each in the existing numericCode check) — or a distinct `messaging_limit` code that broadcast-runner treats like rate_limited but with a much longer pause for 131048, since spam limits last hours, not seconds. All downstream behavior (BullMQ retry, broadcast backoff + lane pause, forward loop break) then engages automatically. Update the stale 4/80007 comment in broadcast-runner.ts:1015-1020.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced from the code. normalizeMetaSendError (apps/api/src/lib/providers/meta.ts:1589-1660) maps only numeric codes 4 and 80007 to "rate_limited" (1616-1623); it never inspects httpStatus, and a repo-wide grep confirms 130429/131048/131056 appear NOWHERE in the codebase, nor is there any HTTP-429-status fallback. Per Meta's error reference (linked in the file at 1559), the send-path rate errors are exactly those three (130429 throughput, 131048 spam/quality, 131056 pair limit, all HTTP 400); code 4 is app-level Graph throttling and 80007 is the Business Management API — so the existing mapping covers the wrong layer for message sends. Downstream both consequences are real: (a) send-worker.service.ts:305-315 sets recoverable = code==="rate_limited" || httpStatus>=500, so these HTTP-400 provider_rejected errors are terminal — no BullMQ retry, permanent red bubble — even though messages.service.ts:831-833 already deletes the OutboundSendAttempt row (provably-not-sent), making a retry safe; (b) broadcast-runner.ts:1021 gates the ENTIRE backoff machinery (track429Hit:1028, pauseAllLanes 60s:1035, jittered 3-4s retry:1039) solely on code==="rate_limited"; the else branch (1066-1076) marks the recipient failed immediately, so lanes keep hammering at full speed and burn the remaining audience + quality rating. The stale comment at 1015-1020 ("flagged number ... 4/80007") confirms the intent existed but targeted the wrong codes — this is an oversight, not a locked decision (no deferred/by-design item covers Meta error-code normalization). Severity stays medium, not high: the runner's own pacing (SEND_CONCURRENCY=5, SEND_GAP_MS=200, broadcast-runner.ts:103-104, ~10-25 msg/s) is under the default 80 mps Cloud API throughput so 130429 needs a low-throughput/unverified number; a persistent 131048 spam window would not be cured by the 60s pause anyway (recipients still fail after one retry, just paced); and inbox sends degrade to red bubble + manual resend. Real and user-visible, but bounded to burst/limit-window conditions rather than a normal-use outage.

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/api/src/lib/providers/ingest.ts:214-238` · **Area:** messaging-provider

**Root cause:** When Meta delivers `status: "failed"` with an `errors[]` array (the normal surfacing path for post-acceptance failures: 131047 outside-window on an API-accepted send, 131049 per-user marketing frequency caps, 131026 undeliverable, quality-based blocks), the parser only console.errors the codes (meta.ts:601-609) and the NormalizedStatusUpdate carries just `{ externalId, status, timestamp }` (meta.ts:613-619). ingestStatusUpdate then writes only `data: { status: evt.status }` (ingest.ts:226-229) and the published `message.status_changed` event carries only the status string (ingest.ts:231-238). The status webhook's rawPayload is discarded — the Message row's rawPayload is the original send-tim…

Diagnosis requires SSH + journald grep, per message. Especially painful for broadcasts where recipients flip to failed after being marked sent.

**Recommended fix:** Carry `errors[0].code` + `error_data.details` on NormalizedStatusUpdate, persist to a nullable `statusErrorCode`/`statusErrorDetail` pair on Message (or into rawPayload via a jsonb merge), and include them in the message.status_changed payload so the bubble tooltip can show the reason.

Notably the code comment at meta.ts:598-600 explicitly says 'Surface WHY Meta failed delivery — otherwise a `failed` status is a silent red icon with no reason' — the author's intent was to surface it, but the implementation only reaches the process log. (2) Ingest: apps/api/src/lib/providers/ingest.ts:226-229 persists only `data: { status: evt.status }`; the status webhook's rawPayload (which DOES ride on the normalized event — a minor inaccuracy in the finding's description that doesn't change the conclusion) is discarded, and the Message model has no failure-reason column (prisma/schema.prisma:858-925) — the row's rawPayload stays the send-time payload. (3) Event/wire: MessageStatusChangedEvent (packages/shared/src/events/types.ts:113-123) and the socket frame (apps/api/src/realtime/fanout-rules.ts:116-123) carry only the status string. (4) UI: bubble-meta.tsx StatusTicks renders a bare red AlertCircle aria-label 'Failed to send' for status==="failed" — no tooltip, no reason. (5) The asymmetry confirms this is a gap, not a design choice: the SYNCHRONOUS failure path does carry reasons everywhere (message.send_failed fanout includes `reason`+`detail`, fanout-rules.ts:127-137; OutboundSendAttempt.failureReason; BroadcastRecipient.errorMessage at send time; UnprocessableEntityException with normalized code/detail in messages.service.ts:855-861). Only the async post-acceptance class — which is exactly how Meta surfaces frequency caps (131049), undeliverable (131026), and quality blocks — loses the reason. (6) Broadcast impact verified: only broadcasts.service.ts and broadcast-runner.ts touch BroadcastRecipient; nothing subscribes to async failed statuses, so a recipient that fails after acceptance shows a reasonless red icon on the Message and a stale 'sent' on the recipient row. Not covered by any locked decision or documented deferral. Severity stays MEDIUM: not a correctness bug or outage (the failed state itself is correct and delivered in realtime); it is a real diagnosability/UX gap a pilot will hit in normal template/broadcast use, but bounded — agents do see the failure, and the reason is recoverable from server logs by an operator.

### 19. Template status never syncs automatically — Meta pause/disable/reject webhooks ignored, sync is a manual button only

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/api/src/lib/providers/meta.ts:462` · **Area:** messaging-provider

**Root cause:** parseWebhook drops every webhook field except "messages" and "calls" (meta.ts:462: `if (change.field !== "messages" && change.field !== "calls") continue;`), so Meta's `message_template_status_update` webhooks (sent when a template is approved, paused for quality, or disabled) are discarded. The only status sync is the manual `POST /api/team/whatsapp/templates` wired to a "Sync" button (whatsapp-templates.controller.ts:61-64; templates-view.tsx:142-146 — invoked from onClick only, not on mount, and nothing periodic exists). A template Meta pauses (quality-based pausing of marketing templates is routine) stays `approved` in the local MessageTemplate row, passes sendTemplateInternal's approved…

**Impact:** A scheduled broadcast against a Meta-paused template mass-fails all recipients with an opaque error; newly-approved templates also never appear sendable until someone clicks Sync. Bounded (errors do surface; manual sync recovers), but it will bite a pilot running marketing templates.

**Recommended fix:** Cheapest: parse `change.field === "message_template_status_update"` in parseWebhook into a normalized template-status event and upsert the local row's status (the per-team webhook already authenticates via HMAC). Alternatively (or additionally) call syncTemplates lazily when the template picker/broadcast form loads with a stale syncedAt.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced the full chain. parseWebhook drops every webhook field except "messages"/"calls", so Meta's message_template_status_update events (pause/disable/approve/reject) are discarded; grep confirms the string appears nowhere in the codebase and meta.controller.ts:185 is the only ingest path. fetchTemplates is reachable only via syncTemplates → POST /api/team/whatsapp/templates (Sync/Refresh button); no sweeper or periodic job syncs templates. The UI's only automatic sync fires solely when the local catalog is EMPTY (reply-box.tsx:438-442, new-broadcast-form.tsx:195-198), which never covers status drift on an existing catalog — minor correction to the finding's "manual button only" phrasing, no change in conclusion. The stale local status is load-bearing: send-template-internal.ts:114 and broadcasts.service.ts:169 gate on the local row, and the broadcast runner's loadTemplate (broadcast-runner.ts:1601) re-checks nothing at run time. Concrete impact: a Meta-paused template stays locally "approved", a scheduled broadcast launches and mass-fails per recipient with opaque provider errors while the app claims the template is fine; newly Meta-approved templates stay locally "pending"/unsendable until someone clicks Sync. Severity stays medium: errors do surface, the Sync button fully recovers, no data loss or normal-chat impact — bounded but will bite a pilot running marketing templates. Fix note: parsing the field alone is insufficient — the Meta App webhook subscription must also include message_template_status_update (onboarding is manual today).

### 20. Broadcasts send to soft-deleted contacts — no deletedAt re-check at fire time (worst for scheduled sends)

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/api/src/lib/broadcast-runner.ts:589-662, 828-973` · **Area:** broadcasts-bulk

**Root cause:** Audience resolution filters `deletedAt: null` everywhere at CREATE time (all/by_tag/selected modes and resolveAudienceGroupMembers), but the runner never re-checks contact liveness at SEND time. `grep deletedAt broadcast-runner.ts` returns zero hits: the recipient page query (refill(), lines 649-655) selects `where: { broadcastId, status: "queued" }` with a contact select of id/name/phoneNumber/email/location/customFields only, and processOneRecipient's only contact gate is `!recipient.contact.phoneNumber` (line 966). For an immediate broadcast the window is seconds; for a SCHEDULED broadcast (delayed BullMQ job, can be days out) any contact soft-deleted between create and fire — including v…

**Impact:** Customer-visible WhatsApp template messages delivered to contacts the team explicitly deleted (the de-facto opt-out path), billed by Meta and risking user spam reports that damage the number's quality rating. Most likely on scheduled broadcasts where the create→fire gap is hours/days.

**Recommended fix:** Add `deletedAt: true` to the recipient contact select and, in processOneRecipient, short-circuit `if (recipient.contact.deletedAt) { markRecipientFailed(recipient.id, "contact was deleted after the broadcast was created"); bumpCounters({failed:1}); return; }` — mirrors the existing missing-phone guard at line 966. (Don't filter in the refill WHERE: rows would linger `queued` forever inside a `completed` broadcast.)

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced every link in the chain. (1) Contact deletion is soft-only: contacts.service.ts:444-447 and :498-501 set deletedAt; no hard delete path for contacts exists. (2) Broadcast audiences are resolved with deletedAt:null at CREATE time only (broadcasts.service.ts:210/:235/:290; audience-groups.ts:148/154/191-196) and frozen into BroadcastRecipient rows via createMany (broadcasts.service.ts:352). (3) The runner never re-checks liveness: grep for deletedAt in broadcast-runner.ts yields zero hits; refill() (lines 649-655) pages queued recipients with RECIPIENT_SELECT (589-619) that omits deletedAt, and processOneRecipient's only contact gate is the missing-phone check at line 966 before claiming the send attempt and calling sendTemplate. (4) Scheduled broadcasts fire via a delayed BullMQ job whose handler (lib/broadcasts/schedule-worker.ts:35-57) only CAS-flips scheduled→queued and hands off to startBroadcast — no audience re-validation, and the gap can be days (broadcasts.service.ts:369-375). (5) Nothing cancels queued recipients on contact.deleted — only broadcasts.service.ts and broadcast-runner.ts touch broadcastRecipient. Not by_design: the N3 'deletedAt nuance' deliberately left in the 2026-05-25 pass-2 review covers workflow//v1 lookups with a revive-on-inbound rationale that cannot apply to an outbound scheduled send, and the 2026-06-01 audit's 'broadcast send is correct (resolver filters deletedAt)' statement only examined create-time resolution. There is no opt-out/unsubscribe mechanism anywhere in the codebase, so contact deletion is the team's only removal lever. Concrete impact: a billed, customer-visible WhatsApp template is delivered to a contact the team explicitly deleted, risking spam reports against the number's quality rating — most plausible on scheduled broadcasts (shipped 2026-05-26) where the create→fire gap is hours/days. Severity medium is correct: real but bounded to the schedule-then-delete sequence (or a seconds-wide mid-run window for immediate sends); no data loss, no cross-tenant leak, no double-send. The finding's suggested fix (add deletedAt to the contact select + short-circuit in processOneRecipient, NOT in the refill WHERE which would strand queued rows in a completed broadcast) is sound.

(1) importCsv's createMany block (apps/api/src/contacts/contacts.service.ts:1037-1068) inserts the new rows and the method returns at :1134-1147 with NO bus.publish anywhere in between — no contact.created, no contact.updated, no coalesced contact.bulk_updated. (3) The REVIVE path inside the SAME method publishes contact.created per row (:1022-1030, comment: "same contract as reviveSoftDeletedByPhone — to every subscriber a revived contact is a new one"), so the inconsistency is internal to one method — bulk-volume suppression cannot be the design rationale (a 5000-row all-revive import already publishes 5000 events). (4) Consumers genuinely miss out: contact.created and contact.tag_changed are public outbound-webhook event types (packages/shared/src/outbound-webhooks/public-events.ts:63,65, mapping cases :698,:731), so n8n/partner integrations subscribed to "On Contact created" receive revived contacts but not created ones; the internal workflow tag trigger contact_tag_updated dispatches ONLY from contact.updated carrying tagChanges (apps/api/src/lib/events/subscribers/workflow-dispatch.ts:174-195), which the identical bulk tag-add path publishes per contact (contacts.service.ts:581-623) but import never does — so "when tag X added" automations silently skip import-applied tags. No doc, memory file (project_contact_csv_tags_stage.md), or method doc-comment (:644-653) records event-silence-on-import as deliberate; prior audits (production-audit-2026-05-30.md fix #3, production-audit-2026-06-01.md:706-707) treated the revive-path publish as the expected contract for import. One precision correction to the finding: there is no internal "On Contact created" workflow trigger (the dispatcher doesn't subscribe to contact.created), so the workflow-engine impact is limited to tag triggers; the contact.created miss matters for outbound webhooks and the socket/contacts-list only. ### 22. API docs document a webhook envelope shape that is never sent — actual deliveries are the flat wire payload

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/web/src/app/docs/api/page.tsx:286-305` · **Area:** external-api

The actual delivered body is the FLAT wire shape: `{ team_id, ...toWirePayload(...) }` — no `event_id`, no `occurred_at`, no `data` wrapper, and `channel` is `{ id, name, source, meta, waId, profileName, created_at }`. The shared mapper's own doc-comment compounds this: public-events.ts:73-79 says 'Real envelopes carry the wrapper fields (event_id, event_type, occurred_at, team_id, channel) — see PublicEnvelope', and line 441 claims event_id 'Matches the X-CCP-Event-I…

**Impact:** A partner who writes their receiver from the documented envelope (`payload.data.message.id`, `payload.event_id` for dedup, `payload.channel.phone_number_id`) parses undefined on every delivery. Dedup-by-event_id, the documented pattern, is impossible from the body — it only exists on the X-CCP-Delivery header.

**Recommended fix:** Replace the envelope block on the docs page with the real flat wire shape (team_id + event_type + per-event blocks), document X-CCP-Delivery as the dedup id, and delete/correct the stale PublicEnvelope wire claims in public-events.ts (lines 73-79, 441).

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced every claim. The public docs page (apps/web/src/app/docs/api/page.tsx:286-304) documents an enveloped delivery shape — event_id, occurred_at, a data wrapper, and channel:{source, phone_number_id, display_phone_number} — that the runtime never sends. The subscriber builds the flat wire payload { team_id, ...toWirePayload(...) } (outbound-webhooks.subscriber.ts:245-248, with an in-code comment confirming dedup rides on the X-CCP-Delivery header, not the body), and the worker POSTs that stored payload verbatim (worker.ts:381) with only X-CCP-Event + X-CCP-Delivery headers (worker.ts:402-403). toWirePayload (public-events.ts:1149+) emits flat { event_type, contact, assignee, message, channel, sender } with channel = { id, name, source, meta, waId, profileName, created_at }. The X-CCP-Event-Id header claimed at public-events.ts:441 exists nowhere else in the codebase. The stale doc-comment at public-events.ts:73-79 ('Real envelopes carry the wrapper fields… see PublicEnvelope') describes the pre-refactor enveloped form that the locked 'flat partner-compatible wire shape' decision replaced — so this is stale docs contradicting the locked decision, not the decision itself; not by_design. Concrete impact: a partner coding to the documented envelope (payload.data.message.id, payload.event_id for dedup, payload.channel.phone_number_id) parses undefined on every delivery. Severity stays medium, not high: it is a docs-only defect (runtime is correct and self-consistent), the per-event Sample payload sections directly below on the same page render the TRUE flat shape via toWirePayload (page.tsx:347-353), the signature-verification docs are accurate, and dedup is still achievable via the X-CCP-Delivery header — so the harm is bounded to integration friction/misleading first-read rather than an outage.

### 23. DELETE /v1/contacts/:id documented as hard-delete-with-cascade but implemented as a soft delete that preserves everything

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/api/src/external/v1/external-v1.service.ts:809-841` · **Area:** external-api

**Root cause:** Three public-facing docs say this endpoint hard-deletes: the docs page ('Hard-delete and cascade conversations + notes + tags + broadcast recipient rows', apps/web/src/app/docs/api/page.tsx:100-103), the controller route table ('DELETE /v1/contacts/:id — hard delete', external-v1.controller.ts:74), and the contact.deleted webhook sample ('Hard delete; conversationIds are included for cleanup', public-events.ts:358). The implementation is a tombstone: `data: { deletedAt: new Date() }` with the explicit comment 'PRESERVE its conversations, messages, and media'. The contact.deleted event also always publishes `conversationIds: []` (line 836-838), so the documented 'conversation_ids included for…

**Impact:** A partner using the API for GDPR/erasure requests believes phone number + message history are purged when they are fully retained (and the contact is silently revivable by ingest). Their downstream cleanup keyed on conversation_ids also never fires. Compliance/expectation gap, not a data-loss bug.

**Recommended fix:** Fix the three doc sites to say 'removes the contact from the directory (soft delete); conversation history is preserved; contact a hard-purge path for erasure requests', and either populate conversation_ids on the event or document it as empty for soft deletes.

> Verifier (high confidence): The finding is independently reproducible from the code. The implementation is unambiguously a soft delete (tombstone via deletedAt, conversations/messages/media preserved, contact revivable by ingest), and the service's own comment says a hard GDPR purge is "a separate, explicit path" — so the BEHAVIOR is deliberate and matches the 2026-05-22 schema-foundation decision to soft-delete Contact. But the finding's actual claim is the documentation contradiction, and that is real: three user/partner-facing surfaces (the rendered /docs/api page, the controller route table, and the outbound-webhook event catalog shown in both the docs page and the settings webhooks UI) all promise hard-delete-with-cascade and a populated conversation_ids array. No code path delivers either: every contact.deleted publisher hardcodes conversationIds: [] and the wire mapper passes it through verbatim. The stale internal JSDoc on ContactsService.remove ("Hard-delete... FK cascade clears Conversation / Message / InternalNote / BroadcastRecipient / Tag joins") directly above a soft-delete body confirms these are relics of the pre-soft-delete implementation that were never updated. Concrete impact: a partner using DELETE /v1/contacts/:id for erasure (GDPR) requests is told by the public docs that phone number + message history are purged when they are fully retained, and any downstream cleanup keyed on conversation_ids never fires. Severity stays MEDIUM: it is a docs/contract gap with real compliance-expectation consequences, but no code defect, no outage, no data loss, and the fix is doc-only (plus optionally documenting conversation_ids as always empty for soft deletes). Not 'by_design' because while soft delete is the locked decision, the contradictory public documentation is not — it's plain staleness.

### 24. POST /v1/contacts/upsert silently drops firstName/lastName/language/countryCode/tagIds when the contact already exists

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/api/src/external/v1/external-v1.service.ts:797-806` · **Area:** external-api

On the create branch all fields apply. On the update branch (`created: false`) the call to updateContactInternal forwards ONLY name, email, location, customFields, and stageId — firstName, lastName, language, countryCode, and tagIds are silently discarded even though updateContactInternal supports the first four and the input was schema-validated.

**Impact:** The canonical CRM-sync flow (n8n upsert on every CRM change) appears to succeed (200, contact returned) but first/last name, language, country and tag assignments never update after the first sync. Silent partial data loss a pilot partner will hit in normal use and find hard to diagnose because the create path works.

**Recommended fix:** Forward firstName/lastName/language/countryCode in the update branch (updateContactInternal already handles them, including the derived-name recompute), and either apply tagIds (e.g. additive connect of valid team tags) or reject/document tagIds as create-only.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced from the code. ExternalUpsertContactSchema is aliased to the create schema and accepts firstName/lastName/language/countryCode/tagIds, all of which the create branch applies. The update branch of upsertContactInternal forwards only name/email/location/customFields/stageId to updateContactInternal — even though updateContactInternal natively supports firstName/lastName/language/countryCode including derived-name recompute, so the omission is a one-spread oversight, not a structural constraint. tagIds has no update-path handling at all. No code comment, doc, or locked decision declares these fields create-only (docs/integrations-classA-prompt.md:113 just says "Find-or-create by phoneNumber"), and git history (ed3cbdf, bfed478) shows the 5-field subset was written when the schema already carried the extra fields — drift carried forward, not design. Impact: a partner CRM-sync via the documented upsert endpoint gets 200 + created:false but first/last name, language, country, and tag assignments never propagate after first create — silent partial staleness that is hard to diagnose. Severity stays medium (not high): only secondary directory fields are affected (no message flow, no security, no data destroyed), PATCH /v1/contacts/:id and the dedicated tag endpoints apply these fields correctly as a workaround, and the response body exposes the stale values to an inspecting caller.

### 25. Bulk tag add/remove publishes spurious contact.tag_changed / contact.updated events for contacts whose tags did not change

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/api/src/external/v1/external-v1.service.ts:1149-1204` · **Area:** external-api

**Root cause:** bulkContactTagsInternal never reads each contact's pre-state; after the raw SQL writes it recomputes added/removed from the POST-state: `added = validTagIds.filter((t) => tagIds.includes(t))` and `removed = validTagIds.filter((t) => !tagIds.includes(t))`. For tag-add, a contact that ALREADY had the tag still gets `added=[tag]` (the tag is in its post-state); for tag-remove, a contact that NEVER had the tag gets `removed=[tag]` (absent from post-state). Both pass the `if (added.length > 0 || removed.length > 0)` gate, so a no-op contact still publishes contact.tag_changed (the 'On Contact Tag updated' workflow trigger + partner webhook) and contact.updated with non-empty tagChanges. The fabri…

**Impact:** A bulk call over up to 500 contacts fires false 'tag added/removed' triggers — workflow runs (which can send WhatsApp messages) and partner webhook deliveries — for every contact that was already in (or never in) the target state. Idempotent re-runs of a partner's bulk sync re-fire the full trigger fan-out every time instead of being no-ops.

**Recommended fix:** Pre-read tag membership for the affected (contactId, tagId) pairs (one query on _ContactToTag) before writing, and only publish contact.tag_changed / non-empty tagChanges for contacts whose membership actually changed — matching the single-contact path's no-op short-circuit.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced from the code. bulkContactTagsInternal (apps/api/src/external/v1/external-v1.service.ts) never reads pre-state (ownContacts at 1095-1103 selects id only) and computes added/removed from the POST-write re-read (1149-1162). After `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, every validTagId is in every contact's post-state, so for tag-add `added === validTagIds` for ALL contacts (including ones that already had the tags); after DELETE, `removed === validTagIds` for ALL contacts (including ones that never had the tag). The gate at 1182 always passes since validTagIds is non-empty (throw at 1114-1116), so every contact in the bulk publishes contact.tag_changed and contact.updated with non-empty fabricated tagChanges (and a wrong `before` at 1191-1196). This is NOT by design: the internal admin bulk path (contacts.service.ts:527-536, 586, 605-622) explicitly pre-snapshots membership with the comment "a tag-add of a tag the contact already had → no diff → no workflow trigger", and the external single-contact path no-op short-circuits (external-v1.service.ts:990-994) and gates publishContactTagChange on real changes (1493). The locked bulk-tag decision covers socket coalescing only, not diff computation. Concrete impact: workflow-dispatch.ts:174-196 fires one contact_tag_updated workflow trigger per spurious tag per contact (workflows can send WhatsApp messages; silent defaults to false), outbound webhooks (public-events.ts:731-745) deliver false tag_changed payloads to partners, and audit.ts:161+ writes false tag_added/tag_removed ConversationEvent rows into the conversation activity log. withIdempotency only dedupes identical Idempotency-Keys, so a partner's periodic re-sync re-fires the full fan-out every run. Severity medium is correct: real and customer-visible but bounded to the /v1 bulk endpoint (500 contacts x 50 tags per call), requires a tag-triggered workflow or webhook subscription to matter, and `silent: true` exists as a blunt workaround.

### 26. Transient WebRTC 'disconnected' state instantly terminates live calls (POST /end + teardown)

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/web/src/features/calls/hooks/use-call.ts:527-541` · **Area:** calls-module

**Root cause:** pc.onconnectionstatechange treats 'disconnected' identically to 'failed' and 'closed': it immediately POSTs /api/calls/:id/end and runs tearDown(). Per the WebRTC spec, 'disconnected' is frequently TRANSIENT (brief packet loss, WiFi hop, network interface switch) and routinely returns to 'connected' on its own; only 'failed' is terminal. The code comment assumes the sequence only occurs when 'Customer hangs up → connectionState goes disconnected → failed → closed', but the same handler fires on any agent-side network blip mid-call — which permanently ends the call server-side (endCall flips the row terminal and calls Meta's terminate, calls.service.ts:843-876), so the call cannot recover eve…

**Impact:** A pilot agent on flaky office WiFi will see live customer calls drop permanently on a 1-2 second network blip ('calls keep cutting out'), with the row recorded as completed/agent-hangup. Long-lived calls make transient blips likely in normal use.

**Recommended fix:** On 'disconnected', start a grace timer (e.g. 5-10s) and only end the call if the state has not returned to 'connected'/'connecting' when it fires; keep the immediate path for 'failed'/'closed'. Clear the timer in releaseMedia().

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced from the code. apps/web/src/features/calls/hooks/use-call.ts:527-542 treats connectionState "disconnected" identically to terminal "failed"/"closed": it immediately POSTs /api/calls/:id/end and runs tearDown(), with no grace timer (grepped the whole calls feature — the only timers are the pickup-watch, the 60s ring timeout which covers only status==="ringing", the panel ticker, and toast expiry). Per the WebRTC spec, "disconnected" is a potentially-transient state (intermittent ICE consent failures) that routinely returns to "connected" on its own; only "failed" is terminal. The server-side endCall (apps/api/src/calls/calls.service.ts:843-901) is irreversible: CAS to terminal status (completed, reason hangup_by_agent for a connected call), then Meta provider terminate kills the customer's leg too — so even if the agent's PC recovers a second later, the call is dead on both sides. The code comment (lines 517-519) shows the handler was designed only for the customer-hangup sequence (disconnected→failed→closed); the one-shot teardownFired flag dedupes redundant POSTs but provides no recovery window. Not by_design: no locked decision or doc covers this; the 2026-06-01 pass2 audit examined this exact handler but only for the missing ring-timeout (since fixed at lines 468-485). Severity stays medium, not high: the finding's "1-2 second blip" trigger is slightly overstated (Chrome needs several seconds of consecutive failed consent checks to reach "disconnected", during which audio is already broken), a true interface/IP change couldn't recover anyway (Meta's API has no ICE-restart action, so only same-path transient loss is salvageable), and the impact is bounded to the calls feature with an immediate redial workaround. The proposed fix (5-10s grace timer on "disconnected" only, cleared in releaseMedia, immediate path kept for "failed"/"closed") is the standard WebRTC pattern and correct.

### 27. Any permission-request failure writes a self-imposed 24h calling lockout; claimed idempotency is not implemented

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/api/src/calls/calls.service.ts:508-523` · **Area:** calls-module

**Root cause:** requestPermissionInternal's catch handler treats EVERY sendCallPermissionRequest failure — Meta 5xx, network error, the 20s metaFetch timeout, any 4xx — as a Meta rate-limit and inserts a CallPermissionRequest row with rateLimitedUntil = now+24h ('We don't try to parse the wire message'). initiateCall then reads ONLY the NEWEST row (findFirst orderBy requestedAt desc, lines 151-158) and returns {reason:'rate_limited', retryAt:+24h} (lines 161-169). Compounding it: requestPermission's doc-comment (lines 452-455) claims 'Idempotent over a fresh-enough live row — if one already exists, returns its id without re-hitting Meta', but the method body (456-477) has no such check — it always re-hits M…

**Impact:** One transient Meta/network failure during the (automatic) permission request bricks outbound calling to that contact for 24 hours with a misleading 'rate_limited' reason — Meta never actually rate-limited. Any later valid permission row is shadowed by the newest failed row.

**Recommended fix:** Only write rateLimitedUntil when the Meta error body actually indicates the 1/24h or 2/7d cap (MetaSendError carries status + body text — match on it); rethrow transient errors without persisting a cooldown. Either implement the documented short-circuit on a live row in requestPermission, or fix the doc-comment. Consider reading the newest NON-rate-limited live row in the pre-flight.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced all three sub-claims from the code. (1) requestPermissionInternal's catch (calls.service.ts:508-523) handles EVERY failure class — MetaSendError for any non-OK status (meta.ts:1252-1258), missing-id parse error (meta.ts:1261-1264), network errors and metaFetch timeouts — by writing a CallPermissionRequest row with expiresAt=epoch(0) and rateLimitedUntil=now+24h. This contradicts the schema's own documentation ("Set when Meta returned 1/24h or 2/7d", prisma/schema.prisma CallPermissionRequest model), so it is not a cleanly documented tradeoff. (2) initiateCall reads only the newest row (findFirst orderBy requestedAt desc, calls.service.ts:151-155) and returns {reason:"rate_limited", retryAt:+24h} (161-169) without re-attempting, so one transient failure blocks out-of-window outbound calling to that contact for 24h. The UI copy (inbox-shell.tsx:67-68) tells the agent to "wait for them to accept the request" when no request was ever delivered; worse, the first click's "permission_required" copy says "Permission request sent to the customer" on the failed send. No recovery path exists: the web app never calls POST .../call-permission (grep over apps/web/src = 0 hits), and the permission_granted webhook only mutates Contact (ingest-call.ts:485-519), never CallPermissionRequest. (3) requestPermission's doc-comment (calls.service.ts:447-449) claims live-row idempotency that the body (456-477) and requestPermissionInternal (484-524) do not implement — every call re-hits Meta, and a direct-API re-request while a live row exists would trip Meta's genuine 1/24h cap and write a newer failed row that shadows the older live row in the findFirst. Severity stays medium, not high: metaFetch already retries transient 5xx, the lockout is per-contact and self-heals in 24h, an inbound customer message opens the 24h service window which bypasses the whole permission gauntlet (calls.service.ts:146-150), and messaging/in-window calling are unaffected. Not by_design: calls are absent from the locked-decision list, and the in-code comments (schema + method doc) describe a more correct behavior than the code implements.

### 28. Permission REQUEST is conflated with permission GRANT — expiresAt is stamped +72h at send time and the grant webhook never touches the row

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/api/src/calls/calls.service.ts:151-193` · **Area:** calls-module

**Root cause:** initiateCall's out-of-window gate treats `livePerm.expiresAt > now` as 'permission live' (lines 156-157). But expiresAt is computed locally at REQUEST time in the provider — meta.ts sendCallPermissionRequest returns `expiresAt = new Date(Date.now() + 72h)` immediately after the request POST succeeds (meta.ts:1267-1270) — and the `permission_granted` webhook side-path only mutates Contact (clears callPermissionRevokedUntil, resets the counter), never the CallPermissionRequest row (ingest-call.ts:495-502). There is no granted/denied state anywhere. So for 72 hours after merely SENDING a request, the pre-flight passes and placeCall is attempted against Meta even when the customer never responde…

**Impact:** The local permission gate is decorative for the 72h window after any request: agents get confusing 'provider_rejected' errors for ungranted/denied contacts (instead of a clear pending-permission state), and each attempt is a wasted business-initiated call attempt against the number Meta quality-rates.

**Recommended fix:** Add a status (pending/granted/denied) or grantedAt/grantExpiresAt to CallPermissionRequest; have the permission_granted/denied webhook update the matching row (Meta's payload carries the request context); gate permLive on granted state, and map the not-yet-granted case to a distinct 'permission_pending' reason for the UI.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced every step of the claim. (1) The out-of-window pre-flight in initiateCall treats the mere existence of a non-expired CallPermissionRequest row as a live permission: calls.service.ts:156-157 `permLive = livePerm?.expiresAt && livePerm.expiresAt.getTime() > now`. (2) That expiresAt is stamped locally at REQUEST-send time: meta.ts sendCallPermissionRequest returns `new Date(Date.now() + 72h)` right after the POST succeeds (meta.ts:1266-1269), before the customer has responded. (3) The permission_granted webhook handler (ingest-call.ts:485-502) mutates ONLY db.contact (clears callPermissionRevokedUntil, resets the unanswered counter) and never touches CallPermissionRequest; grep confirms exactly three usages of the model repo-wide (the gate read + two creates), and the schema (prisma/schema.prisma:2114-2132) has no granted/denied/status column. No 'denied' phase is even parsed (meta.ts:341-344 maps only granted/revoked). Net effect: for 72h after any permission REQUEST, the gate passes regardless of grant status, placeCall is attempted against Meta for ungranted/declined contacts, Meta rejects, and the catch maps it to generic `provider_rejected`, which the UI renders as "WhatsApp rejected the call. Make sure calling is enabled on your number." (inbox-shell.tsx:73-74) — misleading the agent/admin toward a config problem instead of a pending-permission state. Not by_design: no locked decision or design doc covers this (the meta.ts:1232 'plan caveats #4' reference points at a file absent from the repo); the comment 'the agent re-clicks once granted' (calls.service.ts:174) shows the code ASSUMES grant by re-click time — the conflation is a modeling gap, not a documented tradeoff. Severity stays medium: Meta's own enforcement is the backstop (no unauthorized call ever connects), the 5/24h cap counts only connected calls, and grant-path calling works — but the local gate is decorative for the 72h window and the error copy is actively wrong on a normal pilot path (out-of-window outbound calling). The finding's quality-rating impact claim is speculative (the API rejects pre-dial, no call is placed), so the impact is misleading diagnostics + wasted API attempts, not high-bar outage/correctness loss.

### 29. Calls never surface or reorder the inbox conversation list in realtime — a missed call from a new contact is invisible until refetch

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/web/src/features/team-chat/hooks/use-team-events.ts:995-1001` · **Area:** calls-module

**Root cause:** ingest-call.ts deliberately bumps Conversation.lastMessageAt on call activity 'so a missed / incoming call ... [rises] for triage' (ingest-call.ts:326-337) and creates a brand-new Conversation when a never-seen number calls (ingest-call.ts:136-144). But the conversation-LIST hook subscribes only to message:new / conversation:assigned / conversation:status / conversation:read / conversation:deleted / contact:updated / contacts:bulk_updated — no call:* frames — so the client list neither inserts the new conversation nor reorders an existing one. The thread-level state IS wired (THREAD_REDUCER_EVENTS auto-wires call reducers into both consumers, thread-reducers.ts:329-332), but the list-level h…

**Impact:** Pilot scenario: a new customer calls, nobody answers the 60s toast — the missed call leaves no visible trace in the inbox list (the conversation row doesn't appear/reorder) until an agent navigates or reloads. The DB bump means the list is also silently out of order vs the server until then.

**Recommended fix:** Handle call:incoming / call:ended (status missed) in the list hook: bump/insert the affected conversation (the frames already carry conversationId, contactId, contactName), falling back to the existing single-conversation refetch path used for unseen conversations on message:new.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced the full chain. Calls create/bump conversations server-side (ingest-call.ts creates a new Conversation for an unseen caller at :135-145 and bumps lastMessageAt at :332-337, with an explicit comment that the bump exists so missed/incoming calls rise 'for triage'), and call:incoming / call:ended frames fan to the TEAM room (fanout-rules.ts:540-549, 572-623) carrying conversationId/contactId/contactName. But the conversation-LIST hook (useTeamEvents) subscribes to exactly seven events (use-team-events.ts:995-1001) — no call:* frames — and I enumerated every other list-mutation channel (onOptimisticListBump = send-sites only; reconnect resync; visibilitychange resync; filter-change refetch; scheduleFilterResync = only from non-optimistic assigned/status/contact frames): none is reachable from a call. Thread-level state IS wired (thread-reducers.ts:329-332), confirming the asymmetry the finding describes. No alternate realtime surface exists: the toast self-expires at 60s, useLiveCalls is just a count badge, and CallsHistory fetches only on mount/filter-change. This is real, not by_design — the server-side comment proves the product intent (calls should surface for triage); the DB half shipped and the socket-consumer half didn't, and no locked decision covers it. Severity stays MEDIUM, not high: (1) the team-wide toast + audible ring cover the live 60s window; (2) the closed-conversation subcase actually self-heals — the reopen publishes conversation.status_changed → team-room conversation:status → onStatus idx===-1 → scheduleFilterResync() → debounced refetch (a nuance the finding overstated with 'nor reorders an existing one'); (3) the data is durable and converges on any tab-visibility transition, reconnect, filter change, teammate non-optimistic frame, Calls-view navigation, or reload. The bounded failure: an agent continuously watching the inbox sees no persistent trace of a missed call from a new contact (or no reorder of a buried open thread) until some unrelated trigger fires — delayed visibility, not data loss.

### 30. Chat image bubbles and gallery tiles download full-resolution originals for thumbnail-size display — no image thumbnail variant despite an existing thumb pipeline

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/web/src/features/inbox/components/message-bubble/media-blocks.tsx:234-257` · **Area:** frontend-performance

**Root cause:** ImageBlock renders `<img src={media.url}>` into a fixed 320x240 (w-80, aspect-4/3) slot, where media.url is the /api/media/:id route that 302s to the full-size UploadThing original. The contact panel's Files-tab grid (ThumbTile in attachment-gallery.tsx:185-208) does the same for images: `const thumbSrc = isVideo ? media.thumbnailUrl ?? media.url : media.url` — videos get a server-extracted poster JPEG, images get the raw original in an aspect-square tile. The server already has the full machinery to fix this: apps/api/src/lib/media-thumbnail.ts extracts video posters in the 2-phase inbound-media pipeline, and apps/api/src/media/media.controller.ts already serves `GET :messageId/thumb` (line…

**Impact:** An image-heavy thread (common for the pilot: product photos, receipts, screenshots) downloads N full-size originals — typically 100KB-5MB each through WhatsApp, larger for agent uploads — to paint 320px boxes, all eagerly on mount. On the project's own 3G perf baseline this is the dominant byte cost of opening a thread, delays the gate-reveal settle loop (image reflows keep the thread hidden longe…

**Recommended fix:** In the existing 2-phase inbound media pipeline (and the outbound upload path), generate a downscaled JPEG (~640px long edge) for kind=image using sharp or the already-installed ffmpeg, store it exactly like the video poster (thumbnailUrl → /api/media/:id/thumb), and point ImageBlock and ThumbTile at `media.thumbnailUrl ?? media.url`. Keep media.url for MediaLightbox. This preserves the no-lazy instant-reveal behavior while cutting per-image bytes 10-50x; backfill is optional (old rows fall back …

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced from the code: ImageBlock renders the full-resolution original (media.url = /api/media/:id, a 302 to the UploadThing original) into a fixed 320x240 slot, eagerly (no lazy — a separate locked decision the proposed fix preserves), and the contact-panel gallery ThumbTile does the same for images (videos get the server-extracted poster). The thumbnail pipeline is genuinely video-only: mediaThumbnailUrl is only written in the inbound-video branch of the Meta webhook controller; the /api/media/:id/thumb route and client thumbnailUrl mapping already exist and would serve image thumbs unchanged; no sharp is installed and no image resize exists on inbound or outbound (agent uploads accepted up to Meta's 5MB image cap and stored verbatim). No locked decision or perf-baseline doc defers image thumbnails, so this is not by_design. Concrete impact: first open of an image-heavy thread on the project's own 3G baseline downloads N full originals (common inbound case ~100-300KB each after WhatsApp sender compression; up to 5MB for agent-uploaded originals) to paint 320px boxes, plus UploadThing egress cost. Severity stays medium (real, hit in normal pilot use, but bounded): the 302 carries a 1-year immutable cache so the cost is first-view-per-browser; the eager wave is bounded by the ~30-message paged slice; gallery tiles are lazy. One impact sub-claim in the finding is wrong and should be discounted: fixed-size 4:3 slots mean image loads cause zero reflow, so slow images do NOT delay the switch-back settle/reveal gate — they just show calm placeholders longer.

Combined with the missing cancel affordance (previous finding), one misclick is an unstoppable mass send.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced every element of the finding. (1) new-broadcast-form.tsx submit() (lines 342-402) POSTs /api/broadcasts with no confirmation dialog; the file contains zero confirm/useConfirm references; the Send button (lines 638-658) calls submit directly. (2) Default scheduleMode is "now" (line 131), so a user who built a broadcast intending to schedule it and forgets the toggle mass-sends instantly. (3) Server-side, send-now fires startBroadcast via setImmediate (broadcasts.service.ts:384-386) and "all" mode targets every non-deleted contact in the team (service.ts:210) — sending begins immediately with no grace window. (4) The "unstoppable" claim is accurate at the product level: POST /api/broadcasts/:id/cancel exists (broadcasts.controller.ts:98) and the runner supports cooperative mid-run cancel (broadcast-runner.ts:667-692), but NO web UI calls it — broadcast-detail.tsx only has retry+GET, broadcasts-browser.tsx only GETs — so a pilot user cannot stop an in-flight mass send. (5) The inconsistency contrast holds: a single contact delete is gated behind a destructive useConfirm dialog (contacts-client.tsx:62,129,292). Not by_design: no locked decision or memory/doc records one-click broadcast send as deliberate, and the fully-built but UI-unexposed cancel endpoint suggests omission rather than choice. Severity stays medium (not high): it is a UX-safety/affordance gap rather than a correctness bug — the button is disabled until the entire multi-step wizard is complete (readyToSend, line 326) and the footer shows the recipient count with an optional recipients-preview dialog, so the trigger requires user error at the end of a deliberate flow — but the consequence (irreversible, paid, customer-facing WhatsApp template sends to potentially the whole contact base, with no in-product abort) is the highest blast radius in the product. The suggested fix (gate Send-now behind the existing useConfirm; optionally also surface a Cancel button wired to the existing endpoint) is cheap and consistent with the product's own patterns.

"hidden md:flex" : "flex"`), a mobile back button at thread-header.tsx:129-137, and MobileShellChrome (section-shell.tsx:41). Strongest evidence it's an unimplemented intent rather than a decision: the Sheet primitive's own doc comment at apps/web/src/components/ui/sheet.tsx:11-13 says it is used for "the inbox ContactPanel on small screens" — but no such usage exists (Sheet is imported only by contact-detail-drawer.tsx, outbound-webhooks-manager.tsx, mobile-shell-chrome.tsx). No locked decision or memory file documents a desktop-only contact panel. ### 33. Whole-tenant delete and bulk contact delete use the same lightweight yes/no confirm as trivial actions — no typed-confirm proportional to blast radius

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/web/src/app/(platform)/platform/organizations/[id]/delete-team-button.tsx:33-50` · **Area:** ux-screens

**Root cause:** Deleting an entire customer organization (every contact, conversation, message, member account, uploaded file — per its own dialog copy) is guarded by the same generic useConfirm yes/no dialog used to revoke an invite or delete a tag; there is no type-the-org-name step and no soft-delete/undo. Similarly, bulk-deleting N contacts from /contacts ('This also removes their conversations, messages, and notes') is a single yes/no confirm (contacts-client.tsx:570-579) — and because Meta Cloud API has no history sync, that WhatsApp history is unrecoverable by design. The dialogs' copy is excellent, but the friction is identical for a 1-row action and a whole-tenant wipe, which invites confirm-fatigu…

**Impact:** One mis-click past a familiar-looking dialog destroys an entire tenant (platform shell) or a customer's full WhatsApp history (contacts bulk) with no recovery short of the nightly pg-backup restore.

**Recommended fix:** For org delete and for bulk contact deletes above a threshold (e.g. >25), extend the confirm dialog with a typed-confirmation input (org name / the word DELETE) — the ConfirmDialog already accepts custom content; no new pattern needed.

> Verifier (high confidence): The org-delete half of the finding is independently verified: both whole-tenant delete paths — superAdmin deleting a foreign org (delete-team-button.tsx) and an org admin deleting their own org (team-settings.tsx deleteOrg) — use the same generic useConfirm yes/no dialog used for trivial actions like revoking an invite, and the backend (TeamRootService.destroy) performs a hard cascade delete (db.team.delete) plus fire-and-forget UploadThing blob deletion with no soft-delete or undo; the only recovery is the nightly scripts/pg-backup.sh restore. No typed-confirmation pattern exists anywhere in the app, and ConfirmOptions (confirm-dialog.tsx:28-37) cannot express one — the finding's fix note that 'ConfirmDialog already accepts custom content' is incorrect. An aggravator the reporter missed: the destructive confirm button is autoFocus (confirm-dialog.tsx:120), so a keyboard-activated trigger + key-repeat/second Enter can complete a whole-tenant wipe in effectively one gesture. However, the bulk-contact-delete prong is REFUTED: contacts.service.ts remove() and bulk() are SOFT deletes (deletedAt tombstone) that explicitly PRESERVE conversations, messages, and media, and tombstoned contacts are revivable via re-create/re-import/ingest upsert — so 'destroys a customer's full WhatsApp history... unrecoverable' is false for that path (the dialog copy claiming it removes conversations/messages is stale and overstates destruction — a separate cosmetic copy bug). Severity stays medium on the org-delete prong alone: real, concrete, unrecoverable total-tenant data loss one autofocused Enter past a familiar dialog, but bounded — only org admins / the superAdmin can trigger it, it requires first clicking a clearly-labeled destructive button, and the dialog copy is explicit. Operator-error guard gap, not normal-use breakage, so not high; not low because the consequence is irreversible tenant destruction with recovery limited to a nightly backup (up to ~24h loss). Not a locked decision — no memory/CLAUDE.md entry locks the confirm pattern for tenant deletes.

### 34. RSC→NestJS retry is disabled in prod on a factually wrong premise; api restarts surface hard error boundaries with no retry

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/web/src/lib/api-client.ts:24, 70-111` · **Area:** nextjs-platform

**Root cause:** The server-side `api()` client fetches NestJS at `BASE = process.env.INTERNAL_API_URL ?? "http://127.0.0.1:4000"` (line 24). In prod, compose sets INTERNAL_API_URL to `http://api:4000` — the api container DIRECTLY over the docker network, NOT through Caddy. `fetchWithDevRestartRetry` only retries network throws on idempotent verbs AND only in non-production (`process.env.NODE_ENV === "production" → throw`, line 99). Its rationale comment (lines 70-78) states the retry isn't needed in prod because 'prod has Caddy in front so the proxy returns 502 (an HTTP response, not a fetch throw)'. That premise is wrong for this code path: RSC→api never traverses Caddy (Caddy only fronts BROWSER traffic),…

**Impact:** During any api downtime where the web process stays up (api OOM auto-restart ~10s per the heap notes in CLAUDE.md, or an api-only recreate), every RSC page render / hard navigation throws immediately and drops the user to the (app) SegmentError boundary instead of transparently retrying. Bounded to api-restart windows and recoverable via 'Try again', but it's a user-visible error the existing retr…

**Recommended fix:** Allow the idempotent-GET retry in prod too (the real prod failure for this path is ECONNREFUSED, which retry handles), or at minimum correct the comment so the wrong 'Caddy returns 502' premise doesn't justify leaving retry off. Consider widening the budget beyond 6s if it's meant to cover deploy windows.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced every step of the finding's reasoning. (1) The RSC api() client targets INTERNAL_API_URL, which prod compose sets to http://api:4000 — a direct docker-network hop that never traverses Caddy (the compose comment and Caddyfile.template both explicitly document this). So when the api container is down, the web container's fetch throws TypeError: fetch failed; it does NOT receive an HTTP 502. (2) fetchWithDevRestartRetry hard-disables retry when NODE_ENV === "production" (api-client.ts:99), and its justifying comment (lines 70-72) claims "prod has Caddy in front so the proxy returns 502 (an HTTP response, not a fetch throw)" — a factually wrong premise for this server-to-server path; Caddy only fronts browser traffic. (3) The thrown error propagates to the (app) error.tsx → SegmentError manual error screen (fetch failures are not chunk errors, so no self-heal reload fires); there is no other retry layer. Impact is user-visible: during api-only downtime while web stays up (OOM/crash auto-restart via `restart: unless-stopped` — a scenario CLAUDE.md documents as having actually happened pre-2026-06-01, or a manual api restart), every hard load and RSC navigation shows "App failed to load" instead of being transparently smoothed by retry machinery built for exactly this failure shape. One bounding correction to the finding's impact: full deploys are NOT in scope — compose recreates web as a dependent of api (depends_on: service_healthy), so both are down together and the browser gets Caddy's 502 before reaching Next.js. The exposure is api-only restart windows (~5-15s), recoverable via "Try again". Not by_design: the dev-only gating was deliberate but rests on a demonstrably false premise, and no locked decision covers it. Severity medium fits the rubric exactly — real but bounded to restart windows, recoverable, no data loss or security impact.

### 35. Redis maxmemory-policy allkeys-lru violates BullMQ's noeviction requirement — silent eviction of job state and run-locks (scheduled broadcasts, workflow waits, wf-run-locks, webhook deliveries) instead of loud enqueue errors

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `docker-compose.yml:119` · **Area:** reliability-failure

**Root cause:** [Merged from two auditors: reliability-failure + deploy-infra — same root cause.] Redis runs with `--maxmemory 200mb --maxmemory-policy allkeys-lru`. BullMQ explicitly requires `noeviction` (its workers log a warning on any other policy, and its Lua scripts assume queue keys exist — eviction of job/meta keys silently corrupts queue state): under memory pressure allkeys-lru evicts ARBITRARY keys, and the coldest keys are precisely the durable long-lived ones — delayed jobs (scheduled broadcasts queued days ahead — one delayed job per broadcast, untouched for hours/days; workflow wait/resume jobs; pending webhook deliveries), the wf-run-lock `SET NX` keys (queue.ts:195-212) whose eviction lets a second lane acquire the lock and double-execute a run (double Meta sends — the exact class the lock exists to prevent), and queue meta/wait structures. The compose comment justifies the policy as 'graceful eviction… evicts cold entries instead of crashing the queue', but for BullMQ the eviction outcome is strictly worse than a loud OOM error. No non-queue Redis usage exists to justify LRU.

**Impact:** Under Redis memory pressure (runaway stream, future tenant growth, retained failed jobs — the compose comment itself anticipates a runaway pub/sub backlog as the trigger), scheduled broadcasts and waiting workflow steps are silently deleted — they never fire and emit no error until a restart — and run-lock eviction re-opens the double-send window for workflow steps. Bounded today because steady-state usage is far below 200mb, but the failure mode when triggered is undetectable data loss instead of a loud error.

**Recommended fix:** Change `--maxmemory-policy` to `noeviction` (BullMQ's documented requirement). Keep `--maxmemory 200mb` — at the cap, enqueues then fail loudly with OOM errors that the existing worker error paths / retry + sweeper + queued-row reconcilers surface, instead of silent job eviction. Optionally add the scheduled-broadcast re-arm query to a periodic sweeper (it already exists as boot code) so a lost delayed job recovers without a restart — the boot-fix re-arm partially mitigates today, but only for broadcasts and only at process restart.

> Verifier (high confidence): The finding is verified end-to-end. docker-compose.yml:119 runs Redis with `--maxmemory 200mb --maxmemory-policy allkeys-lru`, and this single Redis backs FOUR BullMQ queues plus the workflow run-locks: workflows (apps/api/src/lib/workflows/queue.ts:88), broadcast-schedule (apps/api/src/lib/broadcasts/schedule-queue.ts:32), message-sends — free-form customer text sends ride this queue (apps/api/src/messages/send-queue.ts:86), webhook-deliver (apps/api/src/lib/outbound-webhooks/queue.ts:83), and the wf-run-lock SET NX keys whose own doc-comment says they prevent "duplicate WhatsApp send" (queue.ts:178-212). BullMQ's noeviction requirement is not folklore: the installed bullmq@5.76.10 checks the policy on every connection and warns `IMPORTANT! Eviction policy is allkeys-lru. It should be "noeviction"` (node_modules/.pnpm/bullmq@5.76.10/.../dist/cjs/classes/redis-connection.js:434-435) — its Lua scripts assume queue meta/job keys exist, and under allkeys-lru the LRU-coldest keys are exactly the long-lived delayed-job hashes (scheduled broadcasts days out, workflow waits). This is NOT by_design: Redis eviction policy appears in no locked decision, and the compose comment's rationale (docker-compose.yml:114-118, "evicts cold entries instead of crashing the queue") is technically mistaken — noeviction does not crash Redis at maxmemory; writes fail loudly with OOM errors that the existing enqueue error paths, dispatcher retry, and sweepers already surface, while reads and in-flight worker processing continue. It is also internally inconsistent with `--appendonly yes` on the same line: AOF durability is chosen, then undermined by allowing silent key deletion. Severity: medium is correct, not higher, because (a) trigger probability today is low — job payloads are tiny pointers, there is no Socket.io Redis adapter, and 200mb is far above steady state — and (b) the blast radius is partially bounded by existing reconcilers: the 60s workflow-waiting sweeper explicitly recovers "Redis eviction" strands for waiting AND queued runs (apps/api/src/lib/sweepers/workflow-waiting.ts:6-21); lost message-send jobs surface as red bubbles via the client 30s watchdog (visible, retryable). The unmitigated residue is real though: the scheduled-broadcast re-arm is BOOT-ONLY (apps/api/src/broadcasts/broadcasts.service.ts:67-90 in onModuleInit), so an evicted delayed job means the broadcast silently never fires until the next deploy/restart; the webhook orphan sweeper only recovers attemptCount=0 rows, so a delivery evicted mid-retry-backoff is lost; and eviction of a heartbeat-renewed wf-run-lock (warm, but allkeys-lru sampling is approximate) reopens the exact double-WhatsApp-send window the lock exists to close. The proposed fix (flip to noeviction, keep the 200mb cap) is the dependency's documented requirement and converts silent state corruption into loud, already-handled enqueue errors.

### 36. A Redis outage is invisible to every runtime health surface — all containers report healthy while sends, workflows, broadcasts and webhooks are dark

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/web/src/app/api/health/route.ts:37-54` · **Area:** reliability-failure

**Root cause:** The api's /health deliberately returns HTTP 200 with `ok:false` when Redis is down (503 only on Postgres) — a sound choice for Caddy rotation since Postgres-only webhook ingest must keep flowing. But every downstream consumer checks only the HTTP status: (1) the api container healthcheck is `wget -qO- http://127.0.0.1:4000/health || exit 1` (docker-compose.yml:416) — wget succeeds on 200, so the api container stays 'healthy'; (2) the web deep /api/health's `probeApi` checks `if (!res.ok)` only (route.ts:49), so the web container healthcheck also stays green — directly contradicting that file's own doc-comment ('Without the api probe, an api or Redis outage left docker compose ps reporting we…

**Impact:** A wedged/down Redis degrades the core product (agent sends fail) with zero red signal on any operator surface — the recurring '3am pilot incident' has no detection path until a human notices failed bubbles.

**Recommended fix:** Make the web deep /api/health parse the api body and propagate `ok:false` (it already 503s on its own — that flips the web container healthcheck red and gives docker ps a true data-plane signal, matching the comment's stated intent), and/or change the api container healthcheck to grep the body for '"ok":true' while leaving Caddy's `health_uri /health` on status-code-only so routing behavior is unchanged. Set up the already-planned external monitor on a body-aware probe.

> Verifier (high confidence): Every code citation in the finding checks out, and git history proves this is a silent REGRESSION of a deliberately-built property — not a design.

Mechanics (all independently verified):
1. apps/api/src/health/health.controller.ts:72-82 — Redis-down returns HTTP 200 with `ok:false` in the body; 503 fires only when Postgres is down. This half IS deliberate and well-reasoned (keeping the api in Caddy's rotation so Postgres-only webhook ingest keeps flowing), and the finding explicitly endorses it.
2. docker-compose.yml:416 — api container healthcheck is `wget -qO- http://127.0.0.1:4000/health >/dev/null || exit 1`. wget exits 0 on any 2xx and the body is discarded, so the api container stays "healthy" during a Redis outage.
3. apps/web/src/app/api/health/route.ts:49 — `probeApi` checks only `if (!res.ok)`; it never parses the body. Redis-down api returns 200 → web deep health returns 200 → web container healthcheck (compose:254) AND caddy container healthcheck (compose:502, which probes /api/health through the proxy) both stay green.
4. The web route's own doc-comment (route.ts:12-18) states the contract this code now violates: "It MUST cover every downstream the customer chat path requires... Without the api probe, an api or Redis outage left docker compose ps reporting web: healthy while chat was completely broken, masking the outage for operators." The Redis half of that claim is now false.
5. Regression proof: commit 4704834 (2026-05-26, "Pre-deploy review fixes") deliberately made /health 503 on "DB or Redis is down" specifically so "the Docker/Caddy probes react to a wedged data plane". Commit 6db3358 (2026-06-01, "calls") narrowed the 503 to Postgres-only for the sound Caddy-rotation reason — its comment even admits "the continuous Docker/Caddy probe only reacts to a wedged Postgres" — but no compensating body-aware probe was added anywhere, and the now-false web route doc-comment was left in place.
6. Impact is concrete: free-form agent text sends — the dominant agent action — ride BullMQ (messages.service.ts:430 awaits `enqueueMessageSend` with NO synchronous fallback; send-queue.ts:113-120). Redis down → the awaited `q.add` fails → sends fail (optimistic bubbles go red). Workflows, scheduled broadcasts, the message-send worker, and outbound webhook delivery are all dark too. The health controller's own doc concedes "a Redis outage degrades queues / workflows / sends."

One overstatement to correct: "all containers report healthy" isn't literally true in the full-crash mode — the redis container's own healthcheck (`redis-cli ping`, compose:124-133) would show unhealthy in `docker compose ps`, and `restart: unless-stopped` self-heals a plain crash. But (a) nothing alerts on container-health state, (b) the recommended external uptime monitor target (deploy/README "Uptime monitoring — UptimeRobot or BetterStack on /api/health") is status-code-based and stays 200, and (c) failure modes where Redis is unreachable from the api while its own container ping stays green (wedged ioredis socket, config/auth drift, partial network failure) show zero red anywhere. The deploy-time `"ok":true` body grep (deploy.yml:477-480) only covers deploy moments, not runtime. So the substantive claim — no continuous operator-facing surface reflects a Redis outage — survives.

Why not by_design: the 200-on-Redis-down policy is locked-in and sound, but the finding targets the monitoring side-effect, which contradicts the codebase's own stated intent in two places (route.ts:14-18 doc contract; the explicitly-built-then-lost property from commit 4704834). The proposed fix (parse the api body in probeApi and/or grep the body in the container healthcheck, while leaving Caddy's health_uri status-only) preserves the routing tradeoff exactly — proving the visibility gap is collateral damage, not the design.

Severity stays medium, not high: this is a missing-detection bug, not an outage-causing bug. It requires an independent Redis failure first; the most common mode (container crash) self-heals via restart policy and is at least visible in docker compose ps to an SSH'd operator. The gap extends time-to-detection of a degraded state rather than causing it. Real but bounded → medium.

### 37. No automated offsite backup: nightly pg_dump backups, pre-deploy snapshots, and the database all live on the same single VPS disk — offsite copy is documented but not implemented

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `scripts/pg-backup.sh:25-41` · **Area:** reliability-failure

**Root cause:** [Merged from two auditors: reliability-failure + deploy-infra — same root cause.] pg-backup.sh writes gzipped pg_dump output to `/opt/ccp/backups` on the same Hostinger VPS that hosts the postgres_data volume, with 14-day retention; the cron line is auto-installed by the deploy workflow (deploy.yml:634-642) and only covers this local dump. The offsite copy is only a 'recommended' README snippet (rclone / aws s3 sync) that has not been wired into the cron line — deploy/README.md:496-506 explicitly acknowledges 'the on-VPS backups don't survive a disk failure' but neither rclone nor S3 is implemented, scripted, or cron'd. Cron failures land in `/opt/ccp/pg-backup.log` with no alerting (uptime monitoring is also deferred), so a silently-failing backup chain combined with the 14-day `find … -delete` retention converges to zero usable backups. Net exposure: one disk failure, provider/hypervisor loss, account compromise, or an accidental `down -v` + volume wipe loses the database AND all 14 days of backups simultaneously.

**Impact:** Single-event total loss of all customer conversation data for every tenant, on a product whose data (WhatsApp history) cannot be re-fetched from the provider — there is no history sync on the Meta Cloud API. Probability low per-day, consequence maximal; this is the single biggest unmitigated data-loss path in the whole deployment.

**Recommended fix:** Append the offsite leg to the cron line the deploy job installs, as the README already prescribes (e.g. `&& rclone copy /opt/ccp/backups remote:ccp-backups --max-age 24h` or `aws s3 sync`), shipping the rclone config once during VPS setup; encrypt per the README note (optionally pipe through `gpg -c` before upload); and add a dead-man's-switch style check (e.g. healthchecks.io ping at the end of pg-backup.sh) so a failing backup pages someone.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced every claim. scripts/pg-backup.sh writes gzipped pg_dump output only to /opt/ccp/backups on the same VPS hosting the postgres_data docker volume; the deploy workflow auto-installs a cron line with no offsite leg and no alerting (local log file only); pre-migration snapshots in deploy.yml also land in the same /opt/ccp/backups directory on the same disk. A repo-wide grep for rclone / aws s3 / healthchecks hits only README prose — the offsite copy exists solely as a 'recommended' documentation snippet, never as code or cron. This is NOT by_design: unlike every other deferred ops item in this project (which appear in the README's 'Things to set up later (with trigger conditions)' list or CLAUDE.md's 'Skip until forced to revisit' list), offsite backup appears in neither — the README itself flags it as recommended and explicitly states 'the on-VPS backups don't survive a disk failure'. No locked decision covers it. Concrete impact: Meta Cloud API has no history sync, so a single disk/VPS failure permanently destroys all tenants' WhatsApp conversation history with zero recovery path — DB, nightly dumps, and pre-deploy snapshots all share one disk. Severity stays medium per the rubric: not an active bug or normal-use outage (not high), not code-caused data loss (not critical) — a missing safeguard against a low-probability event, partially mitigated by working daily local backups plus a documented/tested restore procedure, at one-pilot scale. Fix is the one-line cron amendment the README already prescribes plus a dead-man's-switch ping.

### 38. A migration that fails mid-apply defeats auto-rollback: the rolled-back api image also refuses to boot (P3009) — full api outage until manual prisma migrate resolve

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `docker-compose.yml:409-414` · **Area:** deploy-infra

**Root cause:** The api container's CMD is `(cd /app && pnpm prisma migrate deploy) && exec node …`. If a migration fails mid-apply, Prisma records a FAILED row in `_prisma_migrations`, and every subsequent `prisma migrate deploy` — including the one the rolled-back :previous-api image runs on boot — exits with P3009 ('migrate found failed migrations') until a human runs `prisma migrate resolve --rolled-back` or restores the pre-deploy snapshot. So the auto-rollback path (deploy.yml:830-889) cannot restore service for this failure class: the rolled-back container crash-loops on the same marker, and `docker compose up -d` never recreates `app` either because it gates on `api: service_healthy` (docker-compose…

**Impact:** Any genuinely failing migration (constraint violation against real pilot data is the classic case — CI smoke runs against a fresh DB so it cannot catch data-dependent failures) produces a total api outage that the safety net cannot recover; resolution requires SSH + manual `prisma migrate resolve` or snapshot restore, extending downtime well past the advertised '~30s rollback'.

**Recommended fix:** Document the P3009 runbook in deploy/README.md 'Rolling back' (exact `prisma migrate resolve --rolled-back <name>` + snapshot-restore steps), and have the auto-rollback step detect the crash-looping migrate (e.g. grep api logs for P3009) and print the runbook pointer instead of claiming success.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced the full failure chain. (1) The api CMD is migrate-then-serve: a migration that fails mid-apply exits non-zero before `exec node`, so the container crash-loops under `restart: unless-stopped`.
