The global unhandledrejection listener (chunk-error-reload.tsx) is gated on isChunkLoadError patterns that don't match these errors, so there's no recovery and no spurious reload — just console noise. Impact is user-visible but bounded: an incomplete filtered list or stalled pagination on a flaky connection or during an api restart, converging on the next reconnect/visibility resync. Severity 'low' is correct: real correctness gap, but edge-triggered, no data loss, and eventual convergence exists via resyncWithBackoff/visibility triggers. Note the cited file genuinely lives at features/team-chat/hooks/use-team-events.ts despite serving the inbox conversation list.

### 16. Reconnect resync keeps stale status/assignment on list rows beyond page 1 — only the contact overlay is reconciled for tail rows

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/web/src/features/team-chat/hooks/use-team-events.ts:441-447` · **Area:** realtime-client-state

**Root cause:** resyncOnce after a reconnect replaces the head with the fresh page and keeps every other previously-loaded row as `tail`, correcting only its embedded contact via latestContactRef: `const tail = prev.filter((c) => !freshIds.has(c.conversation.id)).map((c) => { const latest = overlay.get(c.contact.id); return latest ? { ...c, contact: latest } : c; });`. Status, assignedUserId, unreadCount and preview on tail rows (anything the agent paged into beyond the 25-row head) retain whatever they showed before the offline gap; the subsequent `.filter(rowMatchesFilter)` then evaluates the filter against that stale data — e.g. under Mine, a deep row reassigned to a teammate while the laptop slept stays listed.

**Impact:** After sleep/wake, rows beyond the first page can show stale assignee/status/unread until some event touches them, the row is clicked (thread fetch is fresh), or the filter is switched. Requires deep pagination plus an offline gap, and the wrong rows are individually self-healing on interaction — edge-case at pilot scale.

**Recommended fix:** On the reconnect path (as opposed to the 50ms coalesced resync), drop tail rows older than the fresh page instead of preserving them (hasMore/loadMore re-pages them with fresh data), or re-fetch the tail window with the cursor.

> Verifier (high confidence): The claim reproduces exactly from the code. On a reconnect after a drop longer than the 30s connection-state-recovery window, `onConnect` (apps/web/src/features/team-chat/hooks/use-team-events.ts:564-571 — note this file IS the inbox conversation-list hook despite living under features/team-chat/) runs `resyncWithBackoff` → `resyncOnce`, which fetches only PAGE 1 of `/api/conversations` for the active filter. The merge at lines 417-448 fully replaces rows present in the fresh page, but every previously-loaded row NOT in that page becomes `tail` (441-446), where only the embedded `contact` is reconciled via `latestContactRef` — `conversation.status`, `assignedUserId`, `unreadCount`, `lastMessagePreview` are carried over untouched. Worse, the overlay itself only holds `contact:updated` frames seen while CONNECTED, so even contact data missed during the gap isn't corrected for tail rows. The stale tail rows are then evaluated by `rowMatchesFilter` at line 447, so e.g. under "Mine" a deep row reassigned to a teammate during the sleep keeps matching with its stale assignedUserId. Deep pagination is reachable in normal use (infinite scroll, conversation-list.tsx:284-291), and nothing else heals tail rows on reconnect: the LRU `clearExcept` on reconnect (inbox-shell.tsx:287 area) evicts THREAD snapshots, not list rows; `runFullRefetch` covers only the displayed thread; the filter-change refetch (use-team-events.ts:316-338) replaces the list with `page.items` only, which is why a filter switch self-heals. Not by_design: the tail-preservation comment (lines 439-440) states its intent as "newer realtime arrivals mid-fetch" — the stale-deep-row case is an unconsidered side effect, and CLAUDE.md's "every recovery path must converge to server state" rule covers the displayed thread and unread-clear paths, not list tail rows. However the gap is tightly bounded: it needs >30 rows paged in + an offline gap >30s + a metadata-only change on a deep row (any new message bumps the row into the fresh head page and fully reconciles it), and it self-heals on row click (fresh thread fetch), filter switch (list replaced), any subsequent socket event touching the row, or navigation (SSR re-seed). No data loss, no security impact, wrong-filter membership is transient. Low is the correct severity at pilot scale.

### 17. RUN_WORKER_INLINE=0 silently disables ALL background processing (no external worker entrypoint exists) while message sends still return success

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/api/src/workflows/workflow-worker.service.ts:107-111` · **Area:** queues-jobs

**Root cause:** Setting `API_RUN_WORKER_INLINE=0` (compose-wired at docker-compose.yml:329) makes WorkflowWorkerService.onModuleInit return before starting the workflow worker AND all 13 sweepers AND the broadcast-schedule worker (workflow-worker.service.ts:107-111 gates the entire method); SendWorkerService (send-worker.service.ts:63-68) and OutboundWebhooksWorkerService (outbound-webhooks-worker.service.ts:30-34) are likewise disabled. Mea…

**Impact:** One mistyped env var on a deploy = total silent send/workflow/broadcast/webhook outage with healthy health checks and 2xx send responses. Bounded: requires explicit operator misconfiguration of a documented flag, and the boot log does state the mode.

**Recommended fix:** Until a standalone worker entrypoint is re-introduced, fail boot loudly when RUN_WORKER_INLINE=0 (same posture as the ENABLE_DEV_TOOLS and INTEGRATIONS_ALLOW_PRIVATE_HOSTS guards in main.ts:243-270), or downgrade to a prominent error-level log plus a /health flag so the deploy gate catches it.

> Verifier (high confidence): Every factual claim verifies line-by-line. Setting API_RUN_WORKER_INLINE=0 gates off ALL background processing in onModuleInit — workflow worker + 13 sweepers + broadcast-schedule worker (workflow-worker.service.ts:107-112), the message-sends consumer (send-worker.service.ts:63-68), and the outbound-webhook worker (outbound-webhooks-worker.service.ts:30-34) — while no external worker entrypoint exists (apps/api/src has only main.ts + app.module.ts; no `worker` compose service; compose's own comment at docker-compose.yml:326-328 admits this). Meanwhile sendText is queue-backed two-phase (messages.service.ts:404-455): it enqueues and returns { ok: true } in ~5ms, so every send returns 2xx into a queue with no consumer — concrete user-visible impact: total silent send/workflow/broadcast/webhook/sweeper outage with healthy health checks. This is NOT by_design: the locked decision covers the flag's existence and the inline DEFAULT, not the absence of a guard against the currently-impossible =0 configuration. Three aggravators push it past by_design: (a) the stale comment at docker-compose.yml:321-323 actively instructs the broken procedure ("run `docker compose --profile worker up worker` then") referencing a service removed post-Phase-5 — an operator following in-repo docs triggers the outage; (b) the boot log asserts a falsehood ("worker is external to this process") with zero validation (main.ts:418-420); (c) the repo already has a fail-fast posture for dangerous env misconfigs (main.ts:243-270 exits on ENABLE_DEV_TOOLS / INTEGRATIONS_ALLOW_PRIVATE_HOSTS) that was simply not applied here, so the suggested fix conflicts with no locked decision. Severity stays LOW (not medium): requires explicitly setting a documented flag to a non-default value (default is 1 in compose and .env.example), the boot log does state the mode, and the failure is loud at the UX level within ~30s (optimistic send bubbles go red via the watchdog), so a pilot notices fast — this is a misconfiguration footgun plus doc drift, not a bug hit in normal operation.

### 18. Status updates for the same wamid in one batch can race across ingest lanes — read can be overwritten by delivered (non-CAS update)

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/api/src/lib/providers/ingest.ts:179-229` · **Area:** messaging-provider

**Root cause:** ingestEvents processes a webhook batch with 8 parallel lanes (ingest.ts:72-144). A single Meta batch (or two near-simultaneous deliveries) can contain both `delivered` and `read` for the same message. Both lanes findUnique the row (ingest.ts:179-194) before either writes; both see e.g. status "sent", both pass the rank guard (statusRank read=2 > sent=0, delivered=1 > sent=0), and both run an unconditional `db.message.update({ where: { id }, data: { status } })` (ingest.ts:226-229) — no CAS pinning the previously-read status. If the delivered UPDATE commits after the read UPDATE, the row regresses read→delivered permanently (read is the terminal green state; no later frame corrects it), and b…

**Impact:** Cosmetic checkmark regression (two grey ticks instead of blue) on a small fraction of messages under bursty status delivery; no data loss.

**Recommended fix:** Make the write a CAS: `updateMany({ where: { id, status: existing.status }, data: { status: evt.status } })` and skip the publish when count === 0 (mirrors the failed-terminal guard already present at ingest.ts:214-216).

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced from the code. (1) ingestEvents (apps/api/src/lib/providers/ingest.ts:72-144) processes a webhook batch through 8 parallel lanes; Meta's parser (apps/api/src/lib/providers/meta.ts:596-617) emits one NormalizedStatusUpdate per entry in value.statuses, so one batch can carry both `delivered` and `read` for the same wamid (customer reads instantly). Two near-simultaneous webhook POSTs race identically — meta.controller.ts:209 has no per-wamid serialization. (2) ingestStatusUpdate reads the row with a plain findUnique (ingest.ts:179-194, no tx/lock), evaluates the monotonic rank guard against that stale snapshot (ingest.ts:219-224), then writes unconditionally with an id-only where-clause (ingest.ts:226-229) — no CAS. If both lanes read `sent` before either commits, both pass the guard and the last commit wins; `delivered` committing after `read` regresses the row permanently. (3) Nothing corrects it: `read` is terminal (Meta got the 200, never resends), the client reducer applyMessageStatus (apps/web/src/features/inbox/lib/thread-reducers.ts:110-121) has no rank guard, and every refetch path reads the regressed DB value. Not by_design: the file's own failed-terminal guard (206-216) and rank guard prove monotonicity was the intent; the only 'accepted race' comment (252-254) covers the Redis park path for unknown wamids, not this DB path. Severity stays LOW: impact is a cosmetic checkmark regression (grey delivered instead of blue read) on the small fraction of messages where delivered+read arrive near-simultaneously AND the commit order inverts; no data loss, no double-send, nothing functional keys off read status. The proposed CAS fix (updateMany pinning existing.status, skip publish on count 0) mirrors the existing pattern and is correct.

### 19. Broadcast clone (?from=) silently drops hand-picked and 'all' audiences — only template/variables/tags/group carry over

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/web/src/app/(app)/broadcasts/new/page.tsx:46-64` · **Area:** broadcasts-bulk

**Root cause:** The clone prefill carries templateId, variables, audienceTagIds and audienceGroupId, but for a source broadcast with audienceMode "selected"/"custom" (hand-picked contacts) the contact ids are not reconstructed (they only exist on BroadcastRecipient rows, not the parent), and clone.audienceMode is computed but never used — so the form falls into its default empty custom builder (new-broadcast-form.tsx:92-115: groupId → group mode, tagIds/contactIds → custom, else empty custom). The `audienceDone` gate (audienceCount > 0, form line 294-295) blocks submission, so no wrong-audience blast is possible — but "Duplicate"…

**Recommended fix:** For audienceMode 'selected'/'custom', reconstruct contact ids from the source's BroadcastRecipient rows (e.g. extend GET /api/broadcasts/:id or add ?includeContactIds) and pass as preselectedContactIds; pass `mode: 'all'` through for all-mode clones; or at minimum render a banner: "Audience couldn't be copied — re-select recipients."

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced from the code. The clone prefill in apps/web/src/app/(app)/broadcasts/new/page.tsx builds `clone.audienceMode` (line 50) but never passes it to NewBroadcastForm (props at lines 94-109 carry only preselectedContactIds/TagIds/GroupId + cloneTemplateId/BodyVars/HeaderVar), and `preselectedContactIds` comes exclusively from the URL (line 57) — never from the clone source. Since "selected"-mode contact ids live only on BroadcastRecipient rows (no audienceContactIds column on Broadcast, prisma/schema.prisma Broadcast model) and "all" mode has no tagIds/groupId, the form's audience initializer (new-broadcast-form.tsx:92-115) falls to the default empty custom builder for both modes. Tag ("by_tag") and group clones DO carry over correctly. This contradicts the stated intent in both the page comment (line 30-32: "prefill template + variables + audience") and the shipped-feature memory doc — it is a partial implementation (the dead `audienceMode` field is the fingerprint), not a locked decision. Impact is bounded: `audienceDone` (form line 294-295) and `readyToSend` (line 326) hard-block sending with an empty audience, so no wrong-audience blast is possible; the operator just has to notice and rebuild the audience by hand. That makes it a real but minor UX/expectation gap on a secondary affordance — severity low is correct.

### 21. Outbound-webhook channel cache invalidation is dead code — cache key mismatch means entries are never evicted

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/api/src/outbound-webhooks/outbound-webhooks.subscriber.ts:104-114` · **Area:** external-api

**Root cause:** resolveChannel stores cache entries under the composite key `${teamId}:${channel ?? "_primary"}` (line 490: `const key = \`${teamId}:${channel ?? "_primary"}\`;` … line 514: `this.channelCache.set(key, base)`), but the team.catalog_changed invalidator checks and deletes the bare team id: `if (this.channelCache.has(event.teamId)) { this.channelCache.delete(event.teamId); }`. `has(event.teamId)` is always false, so the documented invalidation ('Drop the row so the next event re-reads from DB') never happens.

**Impact:** Bounded: the cached WireChannelBase only carries the ChannelConnection cuid + created_at. After a team disconnects/reconnects WhatsApp (new connection row), webhook payloads keep stamping the stale channel.id until the api process restarts. No cross-tenant or data-loss risk.

**Recommended fix:** Invalidate by prefix: iterate channelCache keys and delete those starting with `${event.teamId}:` (or store a nested Map keyed by teamId).

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced from the code. In apps/api/src/outbound-webhooks/outbound-webhooks.subscriber.ts, resolveChannel() stores cache entries under the composite key `${teamId}:${channel ?? "_primary"}` (line 490, set at line 514), but the team.catalog_changed invalidator (lines 108-109) checks/deletes the BARE `event.teamId`. Every stored key contains a colon and a channel suffix; a bare teamId (cuid) never matches, so `has(event.teamId)` is structurally always false and the delete is dead code. There is no other eviction path — grep confirms the only channelCache touch points are lines 69, 108-109, 491, 514 (no .clear() anywhere). This is NOT by_design: the class doc-comment (lines 62-67) and resolveChannel's doc (lines 478-479) explicitly promise "Cleared on a team.catalog_changed event" / "cache is invalidated wholesale on team.catalog_changed" — the intent is invalidation; the implementation fails on key shape. The CLAUDE.md "grow-only by design" note covers a DIFFERENT cache (lib/providers/config.ts, which has a working invalidateProviderConfig). Concrete impact path confirmed: WhatsappService.disconnect() (whatsapp.service.ts:310) does channelConnection.deleteMany, so a reconnect via updateConfig's upsert (line 274) CREATES a new row with a new cuid. The cached WireChannelBase (id, name, source, created_at) then keeps stamping the deleted connection's id/created_at into every outbound webhook payload's channel block until the api process restarts. Severity LOW is correct: only `id`/`created_at` can go stale (name/source derive from the immutable channel enum), the trigger is a rare admin flow (full disconnect→reconnect), data is informational metadata in partner payloads (channel.id isn't resolvable via any /v1 endpoint), it self-heals on restart, and at the 1-tenant pilot scale this is near-latent. Side note strengthening "real": even if the key matched, updateConfig/disconnect don't publish team.catalog_changed at all (the only publishes in whatsapp.service.ts are template ops at lines 421/537/587/609), so invalidation timing would still be wrong — but the cited key-mismatch alone already makes the documented invalidation never fire.

Even for message subscriptions the test drifts from production: real deliveries are { team_id, ...toWirePayload(...) } (subscriber.ts:245-248) while the test omits team_id; real message.* payloads include a contact block (public-events.ts:1165/1175) which the test omits; and wireSender always emits apiKeyId (public-events.ts:1101) which the test's hardcoded sender block omits. Impact is concrete but bounded: a partner validating their receiver against the test button wires a parser to the wrong shape and breaks on the first real event; the test:true marker and same delivery pipeline (signing/retry/log) still work, so this is integration-onboarding friction only — severity low is correct. ### 23. Idempotency-Key longer than 255 chars on the two send routes silently disables idempotency instead of erroring

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/api/src/external/v1/external-v1.controller.ts:587-592, 633-639` · **Area:** external-api

**Root cause:** Both send routes treat an over-long key as ABSENT: `trimmed && trimmed.length > 0 && trimmed.length <= 255 ? trimmed : undefined`. A partner whose generated key exceeds 255 chars (e.g. concatenated order-id + timestamp + hash) gets a 200 with no idempotency protection at all — their retry-after-timeout flow, the exact case the header exists for, double-sends a customer-visible WhatsApp message. Every other /v1 mutation route passes `idempotencyKey?.trim() || undefined` with NO length cap (e.g. line 195, 246, 284), so the surface is internally inconsistent too.

**Impact:** Silent loss of duplicate-send protection on the highest-risk operation (customer-visible, possibly billed template sends). Stripe-convention clients expect a 400 on invalid keys, not silent degradation.

**Recommended fix:** Return 400 `{ error: "idempotency_key_too_long" }` for keys > 255 chars on all routes (one shared helper), rather than silently dropping the key.

> Verifier (high confidence): The finding is accurate on every factual claim, independently re-derived from the code. (1) Both send routes silently treat an over-long Idempotency-Key as ABSENT: apps/api/src/external/v1/external-v1.controller.ts:587-592 (sendTopLevelMessage) and :633-639 (sendMessage) resolve `trimmed.length <= 255 ? trimmed : undefined`. (2) Downstream, the service only claims idempotency `if (idempotencyKey)` — external-v1-messaging.service.ts:373-385 — so an undefined key means the send proceeds with zero duplicate-send protection and returns a normal 200; a partner retry-after-timeout (the exact scenario the header exists for) re-sends the customer-visible WhatsApp message. (3) The 255 cap is NOT a system constraint: ApiIdempotencyKey.key is a Prisma String (Postgres text, no length limit, prisma/schema.prisma:1539-1560), and the shared ApiIdempotencyService (api-idempotency.service.ts) has no length handling. (4) The surface is internally inconsistent exactly as claimed: twelve other /v1 mutation routes pass `idempotencyKey?.trim() || undefined` with no cap (controller :195, :214, :246, :284, :327, :347, :374, :506, :520, :545, :564, :661), so a 300-char key WORKS on bulkAddTags/assign/status/notes but is silently discarded on the two send routes. (5) The contrast is sharpened by the body-level `client_temp_id` field, which enforces max 255 via Zod (external-v1.schemas.ts:99) and returns a 400 `invalid_body` — so the intended house contract for over-long dedupe tokens is reject-not-drop. Not by_design: docs/integrations-classA-prompt.md:72 documents the convention as "trim, 1-255 chars" but never specifies silent-drop as the failure mode, and the same doc's own checklist (:358) flags the copy-pasted length check as debt to extract into a shared helper; no locked decision covers this. Severity stays LOW (not higher): the harm class when triggered is a duplicate customer send, but it requires two stacked rare conditions — a partner-generated key exceeding 255 chars (UUIDs/hashes, the overwhelming norm, are far under) AND an actual retry-after-timeout/5xx event. No pilot hits this in normal use; partners who also pass client_temp_id get a second dedupe layer. The right fix is the finding's: one shared helper that 400s (`idempotency_key_too_long`) consistently across all routes.

### 24. Plain-http outbound webhook URLs are accepted in production while the rejection message claims https is required

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/api/src/team/outbound-webhooks/outbound-webhooks.schemas.ts:8-21` · **Area:** external-api

**Root cause:** WebhookUrlSchema accepts `http://` ('we ALSO accept http for dev'), and the registration guard assertRegistrableHost only rejects non-http(s) schemes (safe-fetch.ts:321-322) plus private hosts — there is no NODE_ENV/production gate forcing https. Yet the BadRequest message shown when a URL IS rejected says 'Webhook URL must be a public https endpoint' (outbound-webhooks.service.ts:130-132), describing a rule the code doesn't enforce. Deliveries carry full customer message bodies, phone numbers, emails, and custom fields.

**Impact:** An admin registering an http:// receiver in production ships customer PII + message content in cleartext across the public internet on every delivery. Bounded: admin-only configuration, requires an explicit http URL choice.

**Recommended fix:** Gate http to dev (allow only when INTEGRATIONS_ALLOW_PRIVATE_HOSTS / non-production), reject http in prod with the existing url_not_allowed error, and align the error text with whatever rule is actually enforced.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced every step of the claim. (1) WebhookUrlSchema accepts http:// with no NODE_ENV gate, while its own doc-comment states "Must be absolute https in production; we ALSO accept http for dev" — the production half of that rule is unimplemented. (2) assertRegistrableHost (registration) and assertPublicHost (delivery, via safeFetch in the worker) both allow the http: scheme to any public host; only private/internal hosts and non-http(s) schemes are blocked. (3) The only production boot guard (main.ts:260-270) covers INTEGRATIONS_ALLOW_PRIVATE_HOSTS, not the scheme. (4) The BadRequest text claims "must be a public https endpoint" — a rule never enforced. Deliveries carry full contact PII (phoneNumber, email, customFields) + message bodies, so an http receiver in prod ships that in cleartext on every event. Not by_design: no locked decision covers transport security for webhook delivery, and the in-code comment documents the opposite intent. Severity stays low: no attacker-controlled path — requires the tenant's own admin to explicitly register an http:// URL, and that admin already has full access to the data; real hardening gap + misleading error text, but bounded misconfiguration, not a normal-use pilot bug.

### 25. Inconsistent error-body machine-parseability across the /v1 surface: `error` is sometimes a stable code, sometimes a human sentence

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/api/src/external/v1/external-v1.service.ts:331-337` · **Area:** external-api

**Root cause:** Newer/messaging routes return stable machine codes in `error` with prose in `detail` (e.g. `chain_depth_exceeded`, `outside_24h_window`, `contact_has_no_phone`, `reply_target_not_found`, `no_conversation_for_contact`, `template_not_found`). But several contact/tag/user paths put the full human sentence IN the `error` field: createContact phone validation (`error: "phoneNumber must be a valid international number (e.g. +1 555 555 0100)"`, line 333-336), duplicate phone (`error: "a contact with this phone number already exists"`, line 437-439), assign (`error: "user not in team"`, messaging :219), note author (`error: "authorUserId is not a member of this team"`, messaging :994), CAS conflict …

**Impact:** n8n/Zapier error-handling branches must string-match prose on roughly a third of the mutation surface; future copy edits break live partner automations without any version bump.

**Recommended fix:** Normalize every /v1 error to `{ error: <stable_snake_code>, detail?: <prose> }` (e.g. invalid_phone, duplicate_phone, user_not_in_team, write_conflict, phone_immutable) — additive for routes already using codes, and keep the old sentence in `detail` for the migrated ones.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced. The /v1 surface genuinely mixes two error-body conventions: newer/messaging paths return stable snake_case codes in `error` with prose in `detail` (chain_depth_exceeded, outside_24h_window, contact_has_no_phone, reply_target_not_found, no_conversation_for_contact, template_not_found, invalid_phone, conversation_rate_limited, whatsapp_not_connected), while contact/tag/user/CAS paths put full human sentences in `error` with no code field. This is drift, not design: the identical phone-validation failure returns the code `invalid_phone` + detail on the messaging contact-lookup path but the raw prose sentence as `error` on createContact; and addNote mixes `authorUserId_required` (code) with "authorUserId is not a member of this team" (prose) 13 lines apart in the same method. No normalizing exception filter exists (PrismaExceptionFilter only maps Prisma errors; HttpException bodies pass through verbatim), and no partner-facing error-code documentation exists (OpenAPI spec is a paused workstream). Concrete impact: n8n/Zapier error branches must string-match prose on a large fraction of the mutation surface, and several distinct errors share an HTTP status (400 = user-not-in-team / invalid phone / stage-not-found), so status-code branching can't substitute; any future copy edit silently breaks live partner automations. Not covered by any locked decision. Severity stays low — DX/forward-compat polish on an API with effectively one pilot consumer, not a correctness or security bug; the suggested additive fix (code in `error`, move prose to `detail`) is the right shape.

### 26. GET /v1/contacts/:id and /:id/channels return soft-deleted contacts, inconsistent with list and mutation guards

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/api/src/external/v1/external-v1.service.ts:231-238` · **Area:** external-api

**Root cause:** Every list branch filters `deletedAt: null` (lines 254, 263, 272, 287) and every /v1 mutation was explicitly hardened with `deletedAt: null` guards ('a partner holding a tombstoned id … must not be able to edit a soft-deleted contact', line 638-641; same on tag ops at 902-903, 984-985, bulk at 1101). But the direct reads were not: getContact (`where: { id, teamId }`, line 232-234) and getContactChannels (`where: { id: contactId, teamId }`, line 853-856) return tombstoned rows as if live, with no deleted indicator in the response shape.

**Impact:** A partner sync that GETs by cached id sees a deleted contact as alive (list says gone, GET says present), then gets a confusing 404 on PATCH for the same id. Read-only inconsistency; no data exposure beyond the team's own tombstones.

**Recommended fix:** Add `deletedAt: null` to getContact and getContactChannels (matching every other /v1 contact accessor), or surface a `deleted: true` flag if reading tombstones is intended.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced from the code. GET /v1/contacts/:id (getContact, apps/api/src/external/v1/external-v1.service.ts:231-238) and GET /v1/contacts/:id/channels (getContactChannels, :852-857) query `where: { id, teamId }` with no `deletedAt: null` filter, while every other /v1 contact accessor in the same file filters tombstones: all four list branches (:254, :263, :272, :287), updateContactInternal (:641, with an explicit comment that a partner holding a tombstoned id "must not be able to edit a soft-deleted contact"), tag ops (:903, :985), bulk pre-filter (:1101), and deleteContact itself (:822). No global Prisma soft-delete middleware exists (DbService at apps/api/src/db/db.service.ts is a plain PrismaClient; zero `$use`/`$extends` hits in apps/api/src), the controller delegates with no extra guard (external-v1.controller.ts:162-163, :305-309), and the serializer (apps/api/src/lib/external-shapes.ts) carries no `deleted` indicator. Concrete repro inside the /v1 surface alone: DELETE /v1/contacts/:id succeeds (soft-delete at :829), then GET /v1/contacts/:id returns the contact as fully alive while the list omits it and PATCH/tag-ops 404. Not by-design: the comment at :901-902 states tombstoned contacts are "hidden everywhere else," and the upsert path (:785-796) routes tombstones through createContact revival — the unguarded reads contradict the system's own stated invariant. Impact is bounded: read-only, same-tenant (teamId filter intact, no cross-tenant exposure), no data loss; worst case is a partner sync seeing a deleted contact as alive then getting a confusing 404 on mutation. Reported severity 'low' is correct — real correctness inconsistency, but polish-grade consequences.

### 27. markConnected and endCall are not scoped to call participants — any capable teammate can flip or kill another agent's live call

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/api/src/calls/calls.service.ts:739-758` · **Area:** calls-module

**Root cause:** markConnected's CAS matches only {id, teamId, direction: out, status: ringing, answeredAt: null} — it never checks initiatedByUserId === session.userId, so any user with the default-true calls:make capability can mark ANY ringing outbound team call as connected (stamping answeredAt, which feeds the 5-per-24h connected-call cap at lines 205-217 and flips the thread pill to in_progress for all viewers). Similarly endCall (787-797) accepts any make-OR-receive user terminating any in-progress call. Team-scoped, so no tenant issue, and 'any agent can hang up' is arguably the shared-inbox model — but markConnected exists specifically as the originating BROWSER's audio-detection signal (controller …

**Impact:** Within-team only: a buggy or mischievous second browser can mark never-answered calls connected (burning the daily cap and recording false 'completed' calls) or end a colleague's live customer call.

**Recommended fix:** Add `initiatedByUserId: session.userId` to markConnected's CAS where-clause (the caller is by definition the originating agent). For endCall, optionally restrict to initiator/answerer-or-admin, or accept as a product call and document it.

> Verifier (high confidence): The claim is code-accurate. markConnected's CAS (calls.service.ts:748-757) matches only {id, teamId, direction:out, status:ringing, answeredAt:null} — no initiatedByUserId check — so any teammate with the default-true calls:make capability (checked at 743-746) can stamp answeredAt on ANY ringing outbound team call. That stamp directly feeds the 5-per-24h connected-call cap (205-217, answeredAt:{not:null}) and flips a later hangup to a false `completed` record (832-841, wasConnected = answeredAt !== null). endCall (791-857) likewise has only a make-OR-receive capability check plus team scope — no participant constraint — and best-effort calls the provider's end (860-868), so a capable teammate can terminate a colleague's live customer call. Call IDs are team-discoverable (GET /api/calls, call.ringing_out team fanout), so it is exploitable. Bounding: strictly within-team (teamId on every query, no tenant issue), no privilege escalation (the actor already holds calling capability), and NO normal-use path — the End button and /connected POST fire only from the participant's own browser (use-call.ts:227, 758 operate on local liveCall/PC state; call-bubble.tsx renders status only). The endCall half is borderline by-design (shared-inbox model: answerCall is deliberately any-agent first-wins; the doc comment contemplates the capability-OR), but markConnected contradicts its own documented contract ('reported by the originating agent's browser' — controller 186-194, service 725-738), so it's a genuine missing object-level check, not a decision. Severity stays LOW: insider-mischief-only data-integrity impact (false completed records, per-contact cap burn, dropping a colleague's call), not a pilot-visible correctness bug. Caveat on the proposed fix: a strict initiatedByUserId:session.userId CAS would BREAK the documented P2002 webhook-race path (calls.service.ts:285-320) where the row's initiator is null pending a best-effort async backfill — the guard must accept null (OR-of initiator-is-me / initiator-is-null).

### 28. consecutiveUnansweredOutCalls undercounts: agent-side no-answer hangups terminalize the row first, suppressing the webhook increment

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/api/src/calls/calls.service.ts:893-901` · **Area:** calls-module

**Root cause:** The Meta auto-revocation mirror (4 consecutive unanswered outbound calls) is incremented ONLY in the webhook ingest path (ingest-call.ts:416-421, gated on a genuine non-terminal→terminal transition). But the most common no-answer endings go through REST endCall — the browser's 60s ring-timeout auto-POST (use-call.ts:468-485) and manual agent cancel — which terminalizes the row as `missed` and publishes call.missed WITHOUT touching the counter. Meta's own later terminate webhook for the same call then lands on an already-terminal row, and the alreadyTerminal guard deliberately skips ALL side effects including the increment (ingest-call.ts:192-194, 369-383). Net: the counter only advances when…

**Impact:** Bounded: Meta's authoritative permission_revoked webhook still arrives and sets callPermissionRevokedUntil (ingest-call.ts:503-518), so the hard gate works; the local early-warning mirror just drifts low (and currently has no UI consumer, making the ingest comment about 'the contact-panel warning' doc-drift too).

**Recommended fix:** Increment the counter in endCall's never-connected branch for direction=out (alongside the call.missed publish), or have ingest apply the increment when the alreadyTerminal row was terminalized without webhook evidence (e.g. track which side terminalized).

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced the full mechanism. The consecutiveUnansweredOutCalls increment lives ONLY in webhook ingest (ingest-call.ts:416-421), gated on a genuine non-terminal→terminal transition (!alreadyTerminal, line 383). But REST endCall (calls.service.ts:832-901) terminalizes a never-connected outbound row as `missed` and publishes call.missed WITHOUT incrementing — and the browser deliberately front-runs the webhook via three paths: manual agent hangup, the 60s ring-timeout auto-POST (use-call.ts:468-485), and the unmount beacon (use-call.ts:433-445). When Meta's terminate webhook then arrives, ingest-call.ts:192-194 marks the row alreadyTerminal and line 383 suppresses the increment, so agent-side no-answer endings never advance the counter. The same suppression also kills the reset path (ingest-call.ts:399-404) for connected calls ended agent-side or by the connectionstatechange watcher (use-call.ts ~517-527, whose comment says 'Don't wait on Meta's terminate webhook'), so the mirror drifts in BOTH directions — slightly worse than the finding stated. Not by_design: no locked decision covers this, and the schema doc (schema.prisma:619-625) states intent the code doesn't deliver ('resets to 0 on any answered/completed outbound call', warning at 3 unanswered). Severity stays low, not higher: repo-wide grep proves the field is write-only today (no UI consumer — only callPermissionRevokedUntil reaches the web via inbox-shell.tsx:1098; _shared.ts:191-198 explicitly omits the counter from the wire shape), and the hard gate works: Meta's authoritative permission_revoked webhook sets callPermissionRevokedUntil (ingest-call.ts:503-518) which placeCall checks (calls.service.ts:137-138). The finding's secondary claim of doc-drift is also confirmed: ingest-call.ts:189-191 claims the counter 'drives the contact-panel warning' but no such UI exists. Impact is latent: wrong DB data versus its documented purpose, and the planned 3-strikes warning UI would misfire when built.

### 29. Incoming-call toast ignores the calls:receive capability — scoped-out roles still get ringing popups they can't answer

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/web/src/features/calls/components/incoming-call-toast.tsx:47-99` · **Area:** calls-module

Try again.' (403 isn'…

**Impact:** Roles explicitly excluded from calling are interrupted by every inbound call and get a misleading error if they engage — undermines the admin's role-permissions configuration, though no security boundary is crossed.

The IncomingCallToast's only suppression is the per-device localStorage receiveCalls preference (apps/web/src/features/calls/components/incoming-call-toast.tsx:47-52, 76; provider default true in notification-sound-provider.tsx) — no read of the role's calls:receive capability exists anywhere in the toast, CallProvider, or use-call.ts. calls:receive is a real admin-configurable capability (packages/shared/src/auth/permissions.ts:107/121/141/180) and the rest of the system honors it: the Calls page server-redirects scoped-out roles (apps/web/src/app/(app)/calls/page.tsx:18-21), and answer/reject are enforced via @RequireCapability("calls:receive") (apps/api/src/calls/calls.controller.ts:166/177) plus a documented service re-check (calls.service.ts:551-556). So a scoped-out agent still gets the full-volume ring (notificationSound.startCallRing, toast line 98) and the "is calling you" card on every inbound call; clicking Answer first triggers the mic prompt (use-call.ts:500 getUserMedia inside setupPeer, called at line 558 before the POST), then the 403 from the capability guard falls into the generic non-409 branch "Couldn't accept the call. Try again." (use-call.ts:579-584) — misleading. Not by_design: no locked decision covers capability-blind call fanout; the fanout comment ("Any agent might pick up; toast all") predates/ignores the per-role scoping, while the page gate + service defense-in-depth demonstrate the product intent that scoped-out roles don't participate in calls. The proposed fix is feasible — the (app) layout already resolves permissions (layout.tsx:48) and already threads one capability into the shell (canManageAvailability, line 92). Severity stays low: UI-only (API enforces correctly, no security boundary crossed), only manifests when an admin explicitly flips the default-true calls:receive off for a role, and the harm is interruption noise plus a confusing error message — bounded polish, not a correctness outage a pilot hits in normal (default-permission) use.

### 30. useConversationCounts is mounted twice on /inbox — every triggering socket event fires two GET /api/conversations/counts per open tab

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/web/src/features/inbox/components/inbox-shell.tsx:955` · **Area:** frontend-performance

**Root cause:** Two independent instances of the hook are live simultaneously on the inbox route: InboxSubSidebar (rendered by /inbox/layout.tsx via InboxSubSidebarLive) calls it at inbox-sub-sidebar.tsx:99 for the filter badges, and InboxShell calls it at inbox-shell.tsx:955 solely for the document-title unread count. Each instance registers its own socket listeners (conversation:assigned, conversation:status, conversation:deleted, message:new, contact:updated, contacts:bulk_updated — use-conversation-counts.ts:111+) and its own useCoalescedAsync refetcher, so the coalescing is per-instance: every bucket-moving team event triggers two server round-trips per connected inbox tab, plus two on mount.

**Impact:** Doubles a cheap-but-not-free aggregate query (team-wide groupBy over conversations) on every assignment/status/delete/new-conversation event, multiplied by every agent with the inbox open. At pilot scale it is invisible; at 20+ agents on a busy team it is a steady stream of redundant identical queries. Purely wasteful — both instances always converge to identical data.

**Recommended fix:** Lift the hook into a small provider mounted once in /inbox/layout.tsx (alongside InboxFilterProvider) and have both the sub-sidebar and the shell consume the context; or keep one mount (sub-sidebar) and bridge totalUnread to the shell via the existing window CustomEvent pattern (ccp:contact-stage-delta precedent). Either keeps a single GET + listener set per tab.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced from the code. Both mount sites are simultaneously live on /inbox (layout.tsx:82 → InboxSubSidebarLive → inbox-sub-sidebar.tsx:99; page.tsx:117 → InboxShell → inbox-shell.tsx:955, the latter used only for the document-title unread count). The hook is strictly per-instance: useState + refs + a useCoalescedAsync whose inFlight/queued coordination lives in per-instance useRefs (coalesce.ts:23-24) — no module-level singleton, shared cache, or fetch dedup. Each instance registers its own listener set for conversation:assigned/status/deleted/read, contact:updated, contacts:bulk_updated, message:new and the ccp:contact-stage-delta window event (use-conversation-counts.ts:221-231), so every bucket-moving team event fires two GET /api/conversations/counts per open inbox tab, plus two on mount. The finding even slightly understates the trigger set (it omits conversation:read at line 225, which the local markRead dispatch fires on every thread open). Each GET costs 11 parallel indexed queries server-side (conversations.service.ts:130-198). No comment or memory documents the double mount as an accepted tradeoff — it arrived with the 2026-06-09 title-unread fix; nothing in the locked-decision list covers it. Impact is purely redundant identical load: both instances always converge to the same data, so there is no correctness or UI-divergence consequence, and at pilot scale (1 tenant, few agents) it is invisible. That makes it real but pure efficiency polish — severity 'low' as originally reported is correct (not medium: no bounded correctness edge exists at all). The suggested fix (single provider in /inbox/layout.tsx, or bridging totalUnread via the existing CustomEvent pattern) is sound and small.

### 33. Native window.confirm used for API-key rotation — the only destructive flow not using the app's styled confirm dialog

The rotate() handler in integration-connect-panel.tsx uses browser-native window.confirm, and a repo-wide grep confirms it is the ONLY window.confirm call site in apps/web — the only other grep hit is the doc-comment in components/ui/confirm-dialog.tsx:11 which explicitly names useConfirm the "App-wide replacement for window.confirm / window.alert", proving the convention is codebase-documented, not just auditor preference. ~20 useConfirm() call sites across 19 files handle every other destructive flow with destructive:true styling, focus-trap, Escape handling, and portal rendering, including the two confirms at lines 259 and 278 of the sibling outbound-webhooks-manager.tsx in the same settings area. No locked decision or in-file comment justifies the deviation. Impact is real but bounded: the confirmation guard still works (no correctness/security hole), so the cost is an inconsistent unstyled OS dialog on one admin-only, genuinely destructive flow (atomic revoke+create; old key dies immediately). ### 34. API reference page documents itself 'deliberately public' but the edge proxy forces login

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/web/src/app/docs/api/page.tsx:21-26` · **Area:** nextjs-platform

**Root cause:** The `/docs/api` page is a pure server component (no `getSession`, renders only static reference content) and its own header comment states it is 'Kept deliberately public — there's no sensitive info on this page, and partners may want to read it before signing up.' But proxy.ts's `isPublicPage` allowlist (lines 292-296) covers only `/login`, `/register`, `/logout`, and `/invite/*` — `/docs` is absent. The matcher (`/((?!_next/static|_next/image|favicon.ico|.*\..*).*)`) matches `/docs/api` (no dot), so an unauthenticated request hits the `!hasCookie` branch and is 307-redirected to `/login?next=/docs/api`.

**Impact:** A prospective integrator/partner cannot read the API reference without first creating an account, contradicting the page's stated purpose. Low impact (the docs are reachable once logged in, and the page links back to /settings/integrations), but it's a concrete intent/behavior mismatch.

**Recommended fix:** Add `pathname.startsWith("/docs")` to `isPublicPage` in proxy.ts (the page renders no privileged data), or update the page comment if the intent is actually authenticated-only.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced the full claim from the code. The /docs/api page (apps/web/src/app/docs/api/page.tsx) is a pure server component with a header comment stating it is "Kept deliberately public ... partners may want to read it before signing up", yet the edge gate in apps/web/src/proxy.ts does not allowlist /docs anywhere: isPublicPage (lines 292-296) covers only /login, /register, /logout, /invite/*; isPublicApi (297-321) only matches /api/* and /webhooks/* prefixes, none of which match /docs/api. The middleware matcher (line 369) matches /docs/api (no dot, not a Next internal), isApiPath is false (path doesn't start with /api/), so an unauthenticated request falls into the !hasCookie branch (lines 354-360) and is 307-redirected to /login?next=/docs/api. No other layer makes it public: page.tsx is the only file under app/docs (no layout auth, outside the (app) route group) and deploy/Caddyfile.template has no /docs carve-out — the default route sends it to the web upstream where this middleware runs. Not by_design: no locked product decision covers docs visibility, and the two in-repo artifacts (page comment vs proxy allowlist) directly contradict each other, so one is wrong by definition. Severity stays LOW: the failure direction is safe (over-gated, nothing leaked — the page renders only static reference content and sample payloads), and the user-visible impact is bounded to a prospective partner being unable to read the API reference before creating an account, plus a misleading comment for maintainers. Fix is a one-line allowlist addition (pathname.startsWith("/docs")) or correcting the comment if authenticated-only is the actual intent.

The one surviving internal endpoint, /api/internal/session-invalidated, is a NestJS controller (internal-session.controller.ts:46) whose sole caller (apps/web/src/lib/auth/session-invalidation.ts:26,34) targets INTERNAL_API_URL directly container-to-container — it never passes through the Next.js proxy. Additionally, deploy/Caddyfile.template block (5a) REFUSES /api/internal/* at the edge in prod, so the proxy branch is doubly unreachable AND inconsistent with the documented edge-refusal posture. Not refuted (the code fully supports the claim) and not by_design (no locked decision covers a stale comment about a removed bridge; the Caddyfile shows the current design is edge-refusal, which the allowlist contradicts). ### 36. Broadcast runner has a 429 circuit breaker but no permanent-error breaker — a token expiry/revocation mid-broadcast fails every remaining recipient at full lane speed

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/api/src/lib/broadcast-runner.ts:1014-1076` · **Area:** reliability-failure

**Root cause:** Per-recipient send errors are classified two ways: `rate_limited` gets a sleep-retry plus a global streak breaker (10 consecutive 429s → pause all lanes 60s, lines 1021-1038); EVERY other error — including auth_expired/401 from a revoked or expired Meta access token, or whatsapp-deconfigured mid-flight — hits the else branch (1066-1076): mark recipient failed, return, and the lanes proceed to the next recipient. The send config is resolved once at broadcast start, so a dead token means every remaining recipient produces one guaranteed-failing Meta call. For a few-thousand-recipient broadcast that's tens of minutes of hammering Meta with 401s while marking the whole remainder failed. Recovery…

**Impact:** Bounded: no double-send, recipients recoverable via retry-failed; cost is a wall of false 'failed' recipients, wasted Meta calls against a flagged credential, and a delayed operator discovery of the real cause (dead token).

**Recommended fix:** Mirror the 429 streak pattern for permanent error classes: after N consecutive `auth_expired`/`provider_not_configured` failures, CAS the broadcast to `paused` (the existing boot/resume machinery already handles paused rows) and publish a status event so the UI surfaces 'paused — WhatsApp connection error'.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced the finding's reasoning from the code. The broadcast send config is resolved exactly once before the lane pool starts (getSendConfig at broadcast-runner.ts:459-472), so a Meta token revoked/expired mid-broadcast is never re-detected. The only error class with a circuit breaker is rate_limited (429 streak >= 10 -> pauseAllLanes 60s, lines 1021-1038); every other error -- including auth_expired (Meta code 190, explicitly classified in meta.ts:1624-1631 but never branched on by the runner) -- falls into the else branch at 1066-1076 which marks the single recipient failed and lets all 5 lanes continue through the remaining queue at full speed (SEND_CONCURRENCY=5, SEND_GAP_MS=200ms, cap 10k recipients => minutes-to-tens-of-minutes of guaranteed-failing 401 calls on a large broadcast). The lane loop (695-750) checks only operator-cancel, shutdown, and the 429 pause -- no permanent-error abort exists. Not by_design: no locked decision or documented tradeoff covers this (the deferred broadcast items are per-team fairness and the >10k worker move). Severity stays low because impact is bounded exactly as the auditor stated: no double-send (OutboundSendAttempt claim intact), recipients recoverable via the existing retry-failed endpoint (broadcasts.service.ts), per-recipient failure detail does surface 'auth_expired: WhatsApp access token expired -- reconnect the number in Settings' (errorDetail, 1668-1676), and the trigger (credential death precisely mid-flight) is rare. Cost = false-failed wall + wasted Meta calls + delayed operator discovery; the proposed fix (mirror the 429 streak for auth_expired/provider-class errors -> CAS to paused, which the boot/resume machinery already handles) is sound and cheap.

### 37. Early ship-step failure restores a STALE Caddyfile.prev from a previous deploy generation — the 'harmless rollback' claim in the workflow comment is wrong

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:826-831, 871-879` · **Area:** deploy-infra

**Root cause:** Auto-rollback fires on `steps.ship.outcome == 'failure'` and restores `/etc/caddy/Caddyfile.prev` whenever the file exists. The comment (deploy.yml:826-829) claims a rollback after a ship failure that never touched the VPS 'skips the Caddyfile restore when no .prev snapshot exists' — but on any VPS that has deployed at least twice, Caddyfile.prev ALWAYS exists, left over from the previous deploy's snapshot (deploy.yml:622). If the ship script fails BEFORE the snapshot line (tar extract at 578, .env render at 585-609, compose mv at 615), rollback copies the generation-N-1 Caddyfile over the live generation-N config while images roll to generation N. If deploy N changed routing (e.g. the chang…

**Impact:** A rare-but-real double fault (early ship failure + routing change in the prior deploy) reverts the proxy config one generation too far while images stay current — mismatched proxy/app, e.g. change-password 404s or a webhook path going dark, caused by the safety net itself.

**Recommended fix:** Make the snapshot run-scoped: `sudo rm -f /etc/caddy/Caddyfile.prev` as the first ship-script line (so .prev only exists if THIS run snapshotted), or write a `/tmp/caddy-snapshotted-$GITHUB_SHA` marker after the cp and gate the rollback restore on it.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced the full chain from .github/workflows/deploy.yml. (1) The ONLY writer of /etc/caddy/Caddyfile.prev is line 622, mid-ship-script, AFTER several failable commands under `set -euo pipefail`: tar extract (578), the .env heredoc render into /opt/ccp (585-609), and the compose-file mv (615). Grep across the repo confirms nothing ever deletes .prev — so on any VPS that has executed line 622 once, .prev permanently exists holding the PREVIOUS generation's config. (2) The rollback step fires on `steps.ship.outcome == 'failure'` (831) — a trigger deliberately added to fix the 2026-06-01 audit's HIGH "rollback blind to ship failure" (docs/production-audit-2026-06-01.md:1443-1451) — and its restore gates ONLY on file existence (871), not on whether THIS run took the snapshot. (3) If ship fails before 622, the live gen-N Caddyfile was never touched, but rollback copies the stale gen-N-1 .prev over it; `caddy validate` (873) passes because that config was previously live, then Caddy reloads and the step logs a success line, masking the skew. (4) The image side is genuinely harmless in this scenario (the changes job at 139-147 set :previous = gen N before the failure, containers stay gen N), so the ONLY effect of the misfired safety net is a one-generation proxy regression under current images — exactly mismatches this repo has shipped routing rules for (change-password→NestJS ordering, /api/health/web health_uri, legacy meta-webhook route). The comment's harmlessness claim (825-827) is therefore wrong in steady state — "no .prev snapshot exists" is only true on a virgin host. Severity LOW is correct: requires a double fault (early ship failure — corrupt tar / disk-full / SSH drop mid-script — AND a Caddyfile routing change in the immediately prior deploy), the window self-heals on the next successful deploy (line 623 overwrites), and CI is red so the operator is already alerted. Not by_design: no locked decision covers this; the comment shows the author believed the early-failure path was safe, i.e. it's an unnoticed gap, not a tradeoff. The proposed fixes (rm -f .prev as the first ship-script line, or a per-SHA marker gating the restore) are sound and tiny.

### 38. Disaster-restore runbook references nonexistent compose service `web` (service is named `app`) and the README secrets list omits two required secrets

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `deploy/README.md:485, 493` · **Area:** deploy-infra

Min…

**Impact:** The restore procedure errors out under disaster pressure (or, if the operator improvises and only stops `api`, the still-running web container keeps writing Better Auth sessions during the psql restore); a fresh-environment bootstrap from the README fails its first deploy at secret validation.

**Recommended fix:** s/web/app/ in the restore commands (or rename the service to `web` once, which the topology diagrams already assume); add the two missing secrets to the list and one-shot script; correct the npm-ci and /socket.io/* mentions.

> Verifier (high confidence): Both halves of the finding reproduce exactly from the cited files. (1) The disaster-restore runbook commands reference a compose service `web` that does not exist: the Next.js container's service key is `app` (the only services are postgres/redis/app/api/caddy), so `docker compose stop web api` errors at step one of the restore and stops nothing; an operator who improvises by stopping only `api` leaves the `app` container's Prisma pool (Better Auth session writes, RSC reads) live during a `--clean --if-exists` full-overwrite psql restore — the precise condition the runbook's "nothing writes during restore" comment exists to prevent. (2) The README's authoritative GH-secrets list and its one-shot `gh secret set` script omit ENCRYPTION_KEY and INTERNAL_BUS_SECRET entirely (neither string appears anywhere in deploy/README.md), while deploy.yml's validate step hard-fails the deploy if either is empty — so a fresh-environment bootstrap following the README fails its first deploy. Severity stays LOW (the reporter's own rating): both are documentation defects that fail loudly with self-explanatory errors (compose names the unknown service; the validate step prints the exact missing secret), recovery is minutes, no data loss occurs unless an operator both improvises the partial stop AND proceeds with the restore. Not by_design: nothing in CLAUDE.md or memory locks a `web`-named service (compose deliberately uses `app`, e.g. WEB_INTERNAL_URL defaults to http://app:3000), and the deploy workflow's own comments treat the README as the setup source of truth.

### 39. Caddyfile enables HTTP/3 but the VPS setup runbook never opens UDP/443 — h3 is advertised via Alt-Svc but can never serve

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `deploy/README.md:93` · **Area:** deploy-infra

**Root cause:** deploy/Caddyfile.template:77-79 explicitly enables `protocols h3 h2 h1`, and its own operational note (lines 59-64) warns 'the FIREWALL must allow inbound UDP/443 … If the VPS provider's firewall only opens TCP/443, browsers will silently fall back'. The one-time VPS setup in deploy/README.md:93 does exactly that: `ufw allow 22/tcp && ufw allow 80/tcp && ufw allow 443/tcp && ufw enable` — no `443/udp`. The config's self-documented requirement is contradicted by the repo's own provisioning runbook.

**Impact:** HTTP/3 never actually serves (silent h2 fallback), and the server keeps advertising `h3=":443"` via Alt-Svc, which costs some clients a failed QUIC attempt before falling back. Purely a perf/consistency issue — nothing breaks.

**Recommended fix:** Add `ufw allow 443/udp` to the README setup block (and run it once on the live VPS), or drop `h3` from the protocols list to match reality.

> Verifier (high confidence): Both halves of the claim verify against the repo. deploy/Caddyfile.template:77-79 enables `protocols h3 h2 h1`, and the template's own operational note (lines 55-64) explicitly warns that UDP/443 must be open or "browsers will silently fall back to HTTP/2 and you'll never know HTTP/3 isn't actually serving." The provisioning runbook at deploy/README.md:93 produces exactly that warned-against state: `ufw allow 22/tcp && ufw allow 80/tcp && ufw allow 443/tcp && ufw enable` — no 443/udp. I checked for compensating config elsewhere and found none: .github/workflows/deploy.yml never touches the firewall (its only ufw mention is a comment about SSH timeouts at line 514), and no file in deploy/ or docs/ contains a UDP firewall rule. Not by_design: HTTP/3/firewall posture appears in no locked decision, and the template note proves the author intended h3 to actually serve (it even prescribes `curl --http3 -I` verification). Impact is real but bounded: TLS/h2/h1 over TCP work fine, so nothing breaks; h3 just never serves while Alt-Svc `h3=":443"` keeps being advertised, costing some clients a doomed QUIC attempt before fallback (modern browsers race/fall back quickly, so user-visible cost is near zero). Severity 'low' (polish/perf-consistency) is correct as filed; fix is one line in the runbook plus a one-time `ufw allow 443/udp` on the VPS, or removing h3 from the protocols list.

The surface is actually larger than reported: a third SnippetDto copy lives in snippets-settings.tsx:45 and broadcast-detail.tsx:41 defines BroadcastDetailDto. No current drift exists (all mirrors match today), but nothing — no shared import, codegen, or contract test — links the pairs, so an api field rename keeps both typechecks green while the runtime wire shape silently diverges (web reads undefined → broken UI), the same typecheck-green/runtime-broken class the project's own memory documents for stale Prisma clients. Not by_design: the queries.ts:36-40 doc comment acknowledges the mirroring but this is not a locked decision (absent from the locked list and all memory files), and the comment's rationale is contradicted by the file's own shared-DTO imports and by the api already exporting named interfaces for half of these. ### 42. answerCall's provider-error rollback leaves answeredAt/answeredByUserId set on the failed row — 'connected' flag lies

**Severity:** low (finder said info) · **Location:** `apps/api/src/calls/calls.service.ts:618-627` · **Area:** calls-module

**Root cause:** When Meta rejects pre_accept/accept after the CAS optimistically flipped the row (562-573 sets answeredByUserId + answeredAt), the rollback updates only {status: failed, endedAt} — answeredAt and answeredByUserId survive on a call that never connected. listTeamCalls then computes `connected: c.answeredAt !== null` (1081-1083) → true for these failed rows, contradicting the field's own definition ('A real two-way connection actually happened'). Today's renderers mask it (describe() handles status:'failed' before consulting connected, calls-history.tsx:251-257; CallBubble likewise), so impact is currently latent data-shape dishonesty rather than visible UI.

**Impact:** Any future consumer of answeredAt/connected (analytics, workflow trigger, export) will count failed answer attempts as connected calls; the answering agent is also permanently attributed to a call they never spoke on.

**Recommended fix:** Null answeredAt and answeredByUserId in the rollback updateMany (it's CAS-gated on status:in_progress, so a racing terminal webhook still wins).

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced the full claim from the code. answerCall's CAS (calls.service.ts:562-573) optimistically sets answeredByUserId + answeredAt + in_progress; the provider-error rollback (618-627) resets only { status: failed, endedAt }, leaving answeredAt/answeredByUserId permanently set on a call that never connected. listTeamCalls (1081-1083) then computes connected = answeredAt !== null → true for these failed rows, contradicting the field's own documented definition. The webhook ingest's terminal-state guard (ingest-call.ts:192-194, 236-237) means no later event ever repairs the row. The finding's "latent" framing is also correct: both renderers branch on status:"failed" before reading connected (calls-history.tsx:251-257 with actor:null for inbound; call-bubble.tsx:69-73), and the 5/24h daily cap counts direction:out only (calls.service.ts:206-214), so there is zero user-visible impact today. But it is a real persistent data-integrity defect, not by design — it directly violates the module's own stated invariant ("a failed handshake torn down with answeredAt=null shouldn't burn the cap", calls.service.ts:201-204) and falsely attributes the answering agent on a row forever; any future consumer of connected/answeredAt (analytics, export, workflow trigger, or an outbound path copying the cap query) miscounts failed answer attempts as connected calls. The proposed fix (null both fields in the existing CAS-gated rollback updateMany) is correct and race-safe. Severity corrected from info to low: a real, permanent DB-row falsehood that contradicts a documented invariant is more than polish, but with all current consumers masked it is well below the medium bar.

### 43. api runtime image ships the full workspace node_modules — including all of apps/web's dependencies (next, react, web toolchain) the NestJS process never loads

**Severity:** low (finder said info) · **Location:** `apps/api/Dockerfile:27-36, 48` · **Area:** deploy-infra

**Root cause:** The deps stage runs an unfiltered `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` across the whole workspace (every member's package.json is COPY'd in, including apps/web's), and the runtime stage is `FROM deps` — so the api image's layers carry apps/web's complete dependency tree (next, react, framer-motion, tailwind toolchain, etc.) plus all devDependencies. The header comment celebrates saving '~100–300 MB' by excluding apps/web SOURCE, but the far larger web node_modules ships anyway. The dev-deps part is partially justified (the locked @swc-node runtime needs the TS toolchain at runtime), but the web app's deps have no consumer in this image.

**Impact:** Larger image pulls on every deploy (slower cutover window, more VPS disk churn for `docker image prune` to reclaim) and a wider CVE surface for the Trivy gate to trip on — web-only dependency CVEs can block api deploys. No runtime correctness impact.

**Recommended fix:** Use `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --filter @ccp/api... --filter .` (api + its workspace deps + root) in the deps stage, or `pnpm deploy --filter @ccp/api` into a clean runtime stage. Low priority; do it when image size or a web-dep Trivy block actually hurts.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced every element of the claim. The api image's deps stage (apps/api/Dockerfile:27-36) copies all workspace manifests including apps/web's and runs an unfiltered `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` — no --filter, no --prod, and NODE_ENV=production is set only in the runtime stage (line 88) after install — so the full web dependency tree (next 16, react/react-dom 19, framer-motion, @xyflow/react, tailwind toolchain) plus all devDependencies lands in the image. The runtime stage is `FROM deps` (line 48) with no prune (only npm itself is removed, line 72), and apps/web source is never copied (lines 75-78), so these deps have zero consumers. Not by_design: the header comment (lines 9-11) celebrates saving ~100-300 MB by excluding web SOURCE, showing slim-image intent the unfiltered install contradicts; the comment at lines 22-25 only justifies copying manifests for workspace:* resolution, which a filtered install would still satisfy. No locked decision covers this. Concrete impact confirmed: deploy.yml:302-310 Trivy-scans the api image with vuln-type os,library / HIGH,CRITICAL / exit-code 1, and the smoke job gates on build-api not failing — so a fixed HIGH/CRITICAL CVE in a web-only dep (next has these regularly) blocks api deploys; this exact failure mode already occurred at the OS layer (CVE-2026-45447, Dockerfile:62-67). The finding's caveat is also correct: @swc-node/register + @swc/core are runtime dependencies of @ccp/api, so the TS toolchain genuinely belongs; only the web tree is dead weight. Severity nudged from info to low: real (if intermittent) deploy-blocking vector plus image bloat/disk churn, but zero runtime correctness or security impact in production.

### 1. Team.contactPanelBuiltins schema comment documents 3 supported keys; the canonical type and shipped feature support 7

**Root cause:** The schema comment on Team.contactPanelBuiltins says 'Supported keys: "email" | "location" | "firstContacted". Adding more built-ins later (firstName / lastName / language / countryCode) just means adding the key' — but that expansion shipped (2026-05-25 per project memory): packages/shared/src/types.ts:165-173 defines the canonical `ContactPanelBuiltins` interface with firstName, lastName, email, location, language, country, firstContacted. This codebase treats schema comments as load-bearing documentation (they encode locked decisions and invariants), so a stale key list here will mislead the next person validating or extending the JSON shape (e.g. the `country` vs `countryCode` key-naming…

**Impact:** Documentation-only. No runtime effect (missing keys resolve to visible-by-default). Risk is a future change validating against the stale 3-key list, or re-introducing `countryCode` as the JSON key instead of `country`.

**Recommended fix:** Update the schema comment to the 7-key list and point at packages/shared/src/types.ts ContactPanelBuiltins as the canonical shape (comment-only change, no migration).

> Verifier (high confidence): The finding reproduces exactly. prisma/schema.prisma:308-310 still documents contactPanelBuiltins as 'Supported keys: "email" | "location" | "firstContacted"' with firstName/lastName/language/countryCode listed as future additions — but that expansion shipped 2026-05-25 (per project memory "Contact panel built-ins expanded"). The canonical shape is now 7 keys in BOTH packages/shared/src/types.ts:165-173 (ContactPanelBuiltins interface) and the server-side zod schema apps/api/src/team/contact-fields/contact-fields.schemas.ts:11-19. The naming nuance is also real: the shipped JSON key is `country` (mapping to the countryCode column, documented at types.ts:162-163), while the schema comment predicts `countryCode` — a future maintainer reading only the schema comment could write `countryCode` into the JSON, which resolveBuiltins (contact-fields.service.ts:40-53) silently ignores, making a hide-toggle a no-op. The impact assessment in the finding is accurate: documentation-only today, no runtime effect (contact-fields.service.ts:30-53 resolves missing/unknown keys to visible-by-default). This is not by_design — no locked decision covers stale comments; on the contrary, this project treats schema comments as load-bearing (the 2026-05-26 predeploy audit fixed an analogous provider→channel schema comment drift as a real finding). Severity info is correct: comment-only drift, no current user-visible or security impact, bounded future-misleading risk. Fix is a comment-only edit, no migration.

### 2. Conversation hard-delete leaves dangling FK-less soft references in BroadcastRecipient.conversationId and WorkflowRun.conversationId

**Severity:** info · **Location:** `prisma/schema.prisma:1243, 1381` · **Area:** db-schema

**Root cause:** Conversations are hard-deletable (apps/api/src/conversations/conversations.service.ts:366 bulkDelete and :625 single delete, both correctly tenant-scoped via deleteMany on (id, teamId)). Cascades cover Message/InternalNote/ConversationEvent/Call, and media blobs are gathered pre-delete. But two tables hold the conversation id as a plain string with no FK and no cleanup: BroadcastRecipient.conversationId (schema.prisma:1243, 'Set after the runner picks/creates a conversation') and WorkflowRun.conversationId (:1381). After a delete, those ids dangle permanently. OutboundSendAttempt.conversationId also dangles but is bounded by its 7-day retention sweeper. Verified non-escalating: the broadcast…

**Impact:** Broadcast-detail and workflow-run UIs can render links/references to conversations that 404 after an agent deletes a thread. No crash, no data loss, no cross-tenant exposure. Cosmetic/audit-quality only.

**Recommended fix:** Either leave as-is with a one-line comment on both columns matching the audienceGroupId pattern ('soft reference — survives conversation deletion by design'), or have the delete path null BroadcastRecipient.conversationId for the deleted ids (one updateMany). The UI should already tolerate a 404 on the link either way.

> Verifier (high confidence): Every factual element reproduces from the code. Both columns are FK-less soft references (schema.prisma:1243 BroadcastRecipient.conversationId, :1380 WorkflowRun.conversationId — finding said 1381, off by one, immaterial), and both conversation hard-delete paths (conversations.service.ts:366 bulkDelete, :625 remove) leave them dangling; no conversation.deleted subscriber cleans them. The finding's non-escalation analysis is also correct: the broadcast runner re-resolves the conversation per contact at send time (broadcast-runner.ts:872-944) and never reads the stale column, the workflows UI never renders WorkflowRun.conversationId, and OutboundSendAttempt is bounded by its 7-day retention sweeper. The only user-visible consequence is the broadcast-detail "Open chat" link (broadcast-detail.tsx:429-436) pointing at a deleted thread — and even that degrades gracefully: /inbox/[conversationId] 307s to /inbox?c=<id>, and the inbox page's SSR explicitly handles "deleted, different team, typo'd id" by stripping ?c= with a server redirect (apps/web/src/app/(app)/inbox/page.tsx:91). So: a dead link after a rare operation, no crash, no data loss, no cross-tenant exposure. I considered ruling by_design — the schema documents the identical soft-reference-survives-deletion pattern for Broadcast.audienceGroupId (schema.prisma:1210-1213, kept as audit trail on purpose) and these columns plainly follow the same audit-preservation philosophy — but neither conversationId column carries a comment documenting its deletion semantics, so I cannot certify it as a documented deliberate tradeoff. Ruling real at the auditor's own severity (info): a true, bounded, polish-grade observation whose correct fix may be nothing more than the one-line comment the finding itself proposes.

### 3. Outbound ringing frame is conversation-room-scoped while the live-calls badge (and liveCount) expect team-wide visibility

**Severity:** info · **Location:** `apps/api/src/realtime/fanout-rules.ts:552-560` · **Area:** calls-module

**Root cause:** call.ringing_out emits only to the conversation room ('Only the originating thread'), but useLiveCalls subscribes to call:ringing as one of its badge-refetch triggers (use-live-calls.ts:38-41) and the server count it refetches INCLUDES ringing rows (calls.service.ts:1105-1110). Agents not viewing that thread never receive the frame, so their Calls badge misses the entire outbound ringing phase and only catches up when call:answered or call:ended (both team-room) fires — i.e. the subscription is dead code for every browser the badge change is meant to reach. Inbound is unaffected (call:incoming is team-room).

**Impact:** Cosmetic staleness bounded by the ring duration (≤60s): the team badge undercounts outbound ringing calls for non-viewers; the hook's own comment is also wrong about the fanout scope.

**Recommended fix:** Either also emit a team-room frame for ringing_out (payload is tiny) or drop the call:ringing subscription from useLiveCalls and fix the comment — pick one so the wiring matches the documented intent.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced from the code. call.ringing_out fans out ONLY to the conversation room (fanout-rules.ts:552-560, doc table at :529 says "Only the originating thread"), while useLiveCalls — mounted in team-wide chrome (inbox-sub-sidebar.tsx:100) — subscribes to call:ringing as a badge-refetch trigger (use-live-calls.ts:39) under a doc comment wrongly claiming all call:* frames "fan to the team room" (lines 11-12). The server count the badge refetches includes ringing rows (calls.service.ts:1105-1110), so the badge is meant to reflect the ringing phase. Conversation rooms are only joined while viewing a thread (realtime.gateway.ts:494/575), so agents not on the originating thread never receive the frame: their Calls badge misses the entire outbound ringing phase and only converges on call:answered / call:ended (both team-room). Not by_design: the per-phase room scoping in fanout-rules is deliberate, but the hook's contradictory comment shows the badge-side wiring is an oversight, not an acknowledged tradeoff, and call fanout scoping is not among the locked decisions. Severity info is correct: impact is a self-healing, ring-duration-bounded undercount of a cosmetic team badge (an unanswered outbound call converges back to the prior count via the team-room call:ended frame), with no stuck state, data loss, or security dimension. Fix is trivial either direction (add a team-room frame for ringing_out, or drop the subscription and fix the comment).
