### 5. ForwardDialog's dynamic chunk is fetched on every thread mount because it renders unconditionally with open=false, contradicting the stated defer-until-used intent

**Severity:** info · **Location:** `apps/web/src/features/inbox/components/message-thread.tsx:44-50, 1502-1507` · **Area:** frontend-performance

**Root cause:** The comment at message-thread.tsx:44-46 says ForwardDialog and MessageSearch are 'both rarely opened, so defer them out of the critical thread bundle.' MessageSearch achieves this — it is conditionally rendered ({searchOpen && <MessageSearch .../>} at line 1308), so its chunk loads only when search opens. ForwardDialog does not: it is rendered unconditionally at line 1502 (`<ForwardDialog open={forwardOpen} .../>`), and next/dynamic triggers the chunk request the first time the lazy component is RENDERED, regardless of props — the component's own `if (!open) return null` (forward-dialog.tsx:42) runs after the chunk has loaded. So the forward-dialog + ContactSelectDialog chunk is fetched on t…

**Impact:** Small and bounded: the chunk (forward-dialog.tsx is 103 lines plus ContactSelectDialog) stays out of the initial route JS and is cached after the first fetch, so the cost is one extra network request + parse shortly after the first thread mount. The split's main goal (initial-bundle weight) is still met; only the lazy-until-interaction intent is not.

**Recommended fix:** Gate the render the same way as MessageSearch: `{forwardOpen && <ForwardDialog open ... />}` — the dialog already handles its own null state, and forwardIds is frozen before forwardOpen flips so no state is lost. Alternatively, if the eager warm-up is preferred, update the comment so the next reader doesn't 'fix' it the other way.

> Verifier (high confidence): Independently reproduced from the code. message-thread.tsx wraps both ForwardDialog and MessageSearch in next/dynamic with a comment (lines 44-46) saying both are "rarely opened, so defer them out of the critical thread bundle." MessageSearch is conditionally rendered ({searchOpen && ...} at line 1308) so its chunk loads only on first open; ForwardDialog is rendered unconditionally (line 1502, `<ForwardDialog open={forwardOpen} .../>`). next/dynamic fires the import() the first time the lazy wrapper is rendered regardless of props — the `if (!open) return null` guard (forward-dialog.tsx:42) lives inside the module and can only run after the chunk loads. So the forward-dialog chunk, which transitively includes ContactSelectDialog (171 lines) and contact-browser (851 lines, the full contact picker with search/pagination — within the inbox route this chunk is the only path to that code), is fetched shortly after the first thread mount in every session, even for users who never forward. Impact is concrete but minimal: one extra non-blocking network request + JS parse per session (module-cached afterward; the title's "every thread mount" is mildly overstated, the body bounds it correctly), wasted bytes on the 3G baseline this project cares about, plus a comment/code intent mismatch that invites a wrong "fix" by the next reader. The primary goal (keeping the code out of the initial critical thread bundle) is still achieved, so this is polish-tier. No locked decision covers chunk-load timing, and the comment itself contradicts an eager-warm-up reading, so by_design doesn't apply. Severity info is correct as self-rated. The proposed fix ({forwardOpen && <ForwardDialog .../>}) matches the sibling MessageSearch pattern and loses no state since forwardIds is held in separate state (line 512).

### 7. validateEnv does not check INTERNAL_API_URL, the address every RSC fetch depends on

**Severity:** info · **Location:** `packages/config/src/index.ts:34-100` · **Area:** nextjs-platform

**Root cause:** The fail-fast env list validates DATABASE_URL/BETTER_AUTH_SECRET/ENCRYPTION_KEY (required) and BETTER_AUTH_URL/APP_PUBLIC_URL/INTERNAL_BUS_SECRET/TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS/UPLOADTHING_TOKEN (prodRequired), but not INTERNAL_API_URL. The entire RSC/server-action data layer (api-client.ts, every queries.ts call) and the legacy webhook proxy + web health probe target this URL; if unset it silently falls back to `http://127.0.0.1:4000`, which inside the compose network is the web container itself → ECONNREFUSED on every page fetch.

**Impact:** In the standard compose deploy this is masked because compose injects `INTERNAL_API_URL: ${INTERNAL_API_URL:-http://api:4000}`, so it's never actually missing. But outside that exact wiring (a hand-rolled prod run, a missing compose env line) the web process boots 'healthy' and then 500s every page on the loopback fallback rather than crashing at boot with a legible message — the failure mode vali…

**Recommended fix:** Add INTERNAL_API_URL to the prodRequired list (web-relevant) so a missing value aborts boot with a clear message instead of degrading to a loopback that ECONNREFUSEDs inside docker.

> Verifier (high confidence): The factual core is accurate and independently reproduced: validateEnv (packages/config/src/index.ts:34-100) validates no INTERNAL_API_URL in any category, while the entire RSC data layer (api-client.ts:24) and the legacy webhook proxy (route.ts:44) silently fall back to http://127.0.0.1:4000 when it is unset. I verified the failure mode is even better-masked than claimed: the web healthcheck (health/route.ts:41) uses a DIFFERENT fallback (http://api:4000), so inside a compose network with the env line removed, the container reports healthy (api:4000 resolves via compose DNS) while every page fetch ECONNREFUSEDs on loopback — a healthcheck-masked whole-site outage. This is not by_design: the file's own header documents its one deliberate exclusion (Meta creds, :16-18), states the exact philosophy this gap violates ("cheaper to crash at boot... than 500 on the first request", :11-13), and UPLOADTHING_TOKEN was promoted to prodRequired after precisely this class of misconfiguration shipped (:97-98). However, severity must stay at info, not higher: (a) the committed deploy makes the var structurally non-missable — docker-compose.yml:216 injects a compose-level default (${INTERNAL_API_URL:-http://api:4000}) that fires even with .env line 67 commented out, as it is; (b) a bare `docker run` already aborts on the existing required checks (DATABASE_URL etc.), so reaching the failure requires hand-editing committed config; (c) on bare-metal single-host the loopback fallback is actually correct. Two corrections to the finding: health/route.ts:41 does NOT share the loopback fallback (it uses api:4000 — this detail error actually strengthens the masking claim), and the proposed fix is wrong as written — prodRequired applies to BOTH processes (index.ts:125 has no label filter, unlike apiRequired at :123-124) and the api service's compose env does not define INTERNAL_API_URL, so adding it to prodRequired verbatim would crash the api container at next prod boot. The correct fix is a new web-only category mirroring apiRequired.

### 8. RSC→NestJS client forwards no correlation/x-request-id header, breaking cross-process request tracing

**Severity:** info · **Location:** `apps/web/src/lib/api-client.ts:130-137` · **Area:** nextjs-platform

**Root cause:** The server-side `api()` client forwards only `content-type`, `cookie`, and `x-forwarded-for` to NestJS. It does not propagate any request/correlation id. NestJS runs correlation middleware first and generates+echoes its own `X-Request-Id` per CLAUDE.md, so each of a page's ~8 parallel RSC fan-out fetches gets an independent correlation id on the api side, none tied back to the originating browser page request. The proxy.ts edge layer also does not mint an `x-request-id`, so there is no id to forward in the first place.

**Impact:** When debugging a pilot incident, NestJS logs for the api calls behind a single page load cannot be correlated to each other or to the web-side request — each carries a distinct server-generated id. Pure observability gap; no functional effect.

**Recommended fix:** Mint a request id in proxy.ts (or read an inbound one), stamp it on the request headers, and have api-client.ts forward it as `x-request-id` so NestJS's correlation middleware adopts the existing id instead of generating a fresh one per fetch.

> Verifier (high confidence): Every factual element reproduces from the code. The RSC-side api() client forwards only content-type, cookie, and x-forwarded-for to NestJS; proxy.ts stamps x-nonce/CSP but never an x-request-id; a grep of all of apps/web/src for request-id/correlation returns zero hits, so no id exists anywhere in the web process to forward. NestJS's correlationMiddleware explicitly supports adopting an inbound sanitized x-request-id (the designed propagation hook) and otherwise mints randomUUID() per request — so each of a page's ~8 parallel RSC fan-out fetches gets an unrelated correlation id, untied to the originating page request. This is not covered by any locked decision or documented tradeoff (correlation.ts's own comment merely notes nothing upstream injects one today), so it is an unwired seam rather than by_design. Impact is purely observability (incident log grouping); no functional, security, or user-visible effect — and it's further dulled in practice by the separately-confirmed gap that NestJS Logger lines don't print the ALS correlation id anyway. The auditor's info severity is correct; fix is a one-line header forward plus minting an id in proxy.ts.

> Verifier (high confidence): Every factual claim reproduces from the code. (1) WEB_INTERNAL_URL is wired at docker-compose.yml:360 with a comment describing a cache-revalidate POST to Next.js, but a repo-wide grep over apps/ and packages/ finds zero read sites — the variable is dead. (2) The /api/internal/revalidate route does not exist (apps/web/src/app/api holds only health/auth/webhooks); the bridge was deliberately removed 2026-05-29 per the in-code comment at apps/web/src/lib/api-client.ts:125-128 ("The fetch-cache opt-in + /api/internal/revalidate bridge that lived here was removed 2026-05-29"), yet docker-compose.yml:217-221 and :354-360 still describe it as live. The only real internal bridge is the inverse web→api /api/internal/session-invalidated (session-invalidation.ts:34 → internal-session.controller.ts:46). INTERNAL_BUS_SECRET stays (it secures that real bridge) but its compose comments cite the wrong purpose. (3) Pool comment drift: compose :305 says "max: 25 in db.service.ts" but apps/api/src/db/db.service.ts:83 is poolMax = 50. (4) BROADCAST_PER_TEAM_RECIPIENT_CONCURRENCY is read at broadcast-runner.ts:266 (documented tunable, default 5) but is absent from the api environment block and .env.example — since compose passes only the explicit list, the knob is untunable in prod, matching the project's own "deploy env vars must be wired in compose" failure class. No runtime effect today, so "info" is the right severity; "real" rather than "by_design" because this is post-removal comment/config drift, not a locked decision — the comments actively misdirect operators (CLAUDE.md and docs/local-setup.md:85 carry the same stale bridge/var references). Minor imprecision in the title ("contradicts itself" — it contradicts the code) doesn't change the substance.


---

## Unverified findings (run interrupted before adversarial verification — treat as credible leads, not confirmed)

### 1. Templates with media headers (IMAGE/VIDEO/DOCUMENT) are creatable and syncable but unsendable — sendTemplate never builds the required media header parameter

**Severity:** high · **Location:** `apps/api/src/lib/providers/meta.ts:1162-1203` · **Area:** messaging-provider

**Root cause:** The app has a full flow for CREATING media-header templates (resumable upload via uploadHeaderMedia, meta.ts:1100-1160, with its own MissingAppIdError plumbing), and syncTemplates imports any approved template from the WABA including ones made directly in WhatsApp Manager. But at SEND time, Meta requires a header component with a media parameter ({type:"image", image:{link|id}}) for media-header templates — and the entire send pipeline only supports TEXT header variables: meta.ts:1172-1184 builds components from `args.variables.header` as `{ type: "header", parameters: [{ type: "text", text }] }` only; send-template-internal.ts:137-148 counts header vars only when `headerComp?.format === "TE…

**Impact:** A pilot that creates an image-header template (using the app's own upload flow) or syncs one from WhatsApp Manager gets a template that appears approved and pickable, but 100% of sends fail — including every recipient of a broadcast — with an opaque "WhatsApp rejected the send" error.

**Recommended fix:** Short term: block selection/send of templates whose HEADER format !== TEXT at the picker, sendTemplateInternal, and broadcast creation, with an explicit "media-header templates aren't supported yet" error. Proper fix: extend SendTemplateArgs with a header media parameter (link or pre-uploaded media id) and build the `{type:"header",parameters:[{type:"image",image:{…}}]}` component.

### 2. Sidebar unread/mention badges never update live: message fanout is channel-room-scoped but the badge listener's socket only ever joins the active channel's room

**Severity:** high · **Location:** `apps/api/src/realtime/fanout-rules.ts:333-352 (emitToChannel); apps/web/src/features/team-chat/hooks/use-team-channel-events.ts:341 (only subscribe:channel emit site); apps/web/src/features/team-chat/hooks/use-team-channels-events.ts:50-80` · **Area:** team-chat

**Root cause:** team_channel.message_created fans out via emitter.emitToChannel(e.channelId, "team:channel:message", ...) — i.e. only to sockets joined to room chan:<channelId> (apps/api/src/realtime/emitter.service.ts:64-77, rooms.ts:7). The ONLY place the client ever emits subscribe:channel is use-team-channel-events.ts:341, which subscribes solely to the channel currently displayed (and unsubscribes on switch, line 615). But the sidebar badge state lives in useTeamChannelsList (use-team-channels-events.ts), whose onMessage handler (lines 50-80) bumps unreadForMe / lastMessagePreview / unreadMentionCount on "team:channel:message" frames — frames that can never arrive for any channel other than the one being viewed. And for the viewed channel the bump is deliberately suppressed (skipUnreadBump = isOwnSend || isActive, line 65). Net effect: the unreadForMe:true and unreadMentionCount+1 branches are unreachable in practice. The /team layout's SSR seed is also NOT refreshed on channel switches (App Router layouts persist across child navigation; team-chat-data.tsx is mounted in app/(app)/team/layout.tsx), so the only paths that ever light a badge are a socket reconnect (refetchChannels on "connect", line 122), a rare team:catalog:changed, or a full page load. The server-side comment at fanout-rules.ts:393-407 even acknowledges the list hook "subscribes to the team room, not per-channel rooms" — that's why team_channel.read was moved to the team room — but the symmetric set-path (message_created) was left channel-scoped.

**Impact:** While an agent sits in #general, a teammate posting (or @-mentioning them) in any other channel produces zero sidebar dot, zero mention badge, and a stale lastMessagePreview until the agent's socket reconnects or they hard-reload. Slack-style "new activity in another channel" awareness — the core purpose of TeamChannelReadReceipt + the unreadForMe/unreadMentionCount machinery — is effectively dead in live use. A pilot team will hit this within minutes of using more than one channel.

**Recommended fix:** Emit a second, content-lean frame to the TEAM room from the team_channel.message_created rule (e.g. team:channel:activity carrying channelId, authorUserId, lastMessageAt, mentionedUserIds — omit body/preview for non-default channels so private-channel content/preview doesn't leak to non-members), and have useTeamChannelsList bump badges from that frame (it already filters to channels present in its membership-scoped list, so non-member clients ignore foreign channelIds). Alternatively, fan the existing frame per-member: the service already knows the member set at post time. Keep the confidential full-message frame channel-room-scoped as-is.

### 3. removeOptimistic drops server-CONFIRMED bubbles and restores their text into the composer — a double-send trap when an HTTP failure races a successful socket confirm

**Severity:** high · **Location:** `apps/web/src/features/inbox/hooks/use-conversation-events.ts:1495-1518 (plus reply-box.tsx:808-832, use-conversation-events.ts:1079-1083)` · **Area:** chat-ux

**Root cause:** When the optimistic bubble is reconciled by `message:new`, the server row deliberately KEEPS `clientTempId` for React-key stability (use-conversation-events.ts:1079-1083: `return { ...payload.message, clientTempId: tempId, ... }`). `markOptimisticFailed` correctly guards on `m.pending` (line 1487), but `removeOptimistic` does NOT — it filters by `m.clientTempId !== clientTempId` alone (line 1515), so it deletes a CONFIRMED, already-delivered message from the thread. The reply-box catch path (reply-box.tsx:823-832) calls `onOptimisticFail` then `onOptimisticRetry(clientTempId)` (= removeOptimistic) and restores the draft via `setValue(snapshotValue)` whenever the HTTP request fails. Media sends are SYNCHRONOUS server-side (messages.service.ts sendMedia: Meta upload+send, DB insert, then `message.sent` publish — the in-process socket emit fires BEFORE the HTTP response is written, per the flow doc at messages.service.ts:984-999). So if the response is lost after the send completed (90s client abort while step D is still running, Caddy bounce/deploy mid-response, mobile network blip on the response leg while the WS stays up), the catch fires AFTER the bubble was confirmed: the delivered message vanishes from the agent's thread, the caption/text reappears in the composer, the cached File is re-seated from pendingFilesRef, and a toast says 'Couldn't send message / Tap Retry'.

**Impact:** The agent is shown a failed-send state for a message the customer already received, with the content pre-loaded for resend and an explicit 'Tap Retry' instruction. Re-sending generates a FRESH clientTempId (reply-box.tsx:622), so `runWithSendIdempotency` cannot dedupe it — the customer receives the message twice. Additionally the delivered message is silently missing from the agent's view until the next reconnect/refetch re-adds it.

**Recommended fix:** Guard removeOptimistic the same way markOptimisticFailed is guarded: `prev.messages.filter((m) => !(m.clientTempId === clientTempId && (m.pending || m.failed)))` (this preserves the legit dismiss/retry paths, which only fire from `message.failed` bubbles). In the reply-box catch, skip the composer restore + error toast when the row was already confirmed — e.g. set a flag from the `ccp:optimistic-confirmed:<id>` event (register that listener BEFORE the fetch, not after it) and on catch show 'Message was sent — connection hiccup' instead of a Retry prompt.

### 4. Outbox drainer per-team fairness cap throttles a single tenant to ~40 events/s and still starves other tenants behind a >800-row backlog

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/api/src/lib/events/outbox.ts:171, 202-231` · **Area:** realtime-server

**Root cause:** PER_TEAM_BATCH_CAP = 4 (outbox.ts:171) limits each `claimBatch` call to 4 rows per team. The drainer's tick loop breaks whenever `rows.length < BATCH_SIZE` (outbox-drainer.service.ts:209, BATCH_SIZE=200), so with one tenant (the actual deployment: 1 pilot customer) every tick claims exactly 4 rows and immediately breaks — MAX_DRAINS_PER_TICK never engages. Sustained single-team throughput = 4 rows / 100ms = 40 events/s, not the '~20k/s at the defaults' the drainer's own doc-comment claims (outbox-drainer.service.ts:41-44). Everything customer-visible rides this path: inbound `message.received` (ingest.ts:788 via publishInTx), reopen `conversation.status_changed` (ingest.ts:776-777), outbound sends.

**Impact:** Normal pilot chat cadence (<40 events/s) is unaffected, but any burst exceeds it: a post-outage Meta webhook retry flood of N messages takes N/40 seconds to surface in the inbox (2,000 backlogged messages ≈ 50s of inbox lag; reopens double the row count), and an n8n/external-API send burst queues behind the same per-team FIFO. At tenant #2 the broken fairness window turns one tenant's burst into multi-tenant lag.

**Recommended fix:** Make the drain loop's break condition aware of the cap (continue draining while claimBatch returns ANY rows, bounded by MAX_DRAINS_PER_TICK), and raise PER_TEAM_BATCH_CAP to something that doesn't dominate the batch (e.g. 50-100), or make the cap apply only when multiple teams have pending rows. For real cross-team fairness, partition the candidates window per team (e.g. lateral per-team oldest-N) instead of ranking inside a global-FIFO window. At minimum, correct the throughput comment.

### 5. Channel markRead fires from hidden/background tabs on every inbound message, silently clearing the user's own unread + mention badges

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/web/src/features/team-chat/hooks/use-team-channel-events.ts:447-490 (onMessage), 183-188 (mount/unmount markRead)` · **Area:** team-chat

**Root cause:** In useTeamChannelEvents.onMessage, any frame authored by someone else triggers `void markRead()` (line 485-489) with no document.visibilityState gate. A tab left parked on a channel overnight (minimized, other monitor, backgrounded) keeps its socket connected, receives every team:channel:message frame, and POSTs /api/team/channels/:id/read for each (coalesced), stamping TeamChannelReadReceipt.lastReadAt = now (channels.service.ts:848-864). Because the sidebar mention count is computed as mentions with m.createdAt > r.lastReadAt (lib/team-chat/queries.ts:173-183) and unreadForMe as lastMessageAt > lastReadAt, this erases the user's unread dot AND @-mention badge on every other tab/device (the team_channel.read frame fans to the team room and the user's own tabs clear on it). This is precisely the bug class the inbox solved: use-conversation-events.ts gates markRead on document.visibilityState === "visible" and defers via sawInboundWhileHiddenRef (lines 535, 646-649, 958-974), per the CLAUDE.md rule "a hidden background tab parked on a thread must NOT clear unread for a message nobody saw." Team chat re-implemented the read flow without the gate. (The hook DOES gate the backfill on visibility — lines 344-349 — so the omission on markRead is an oversight, not a design choice.) Secondary cost: ChannelsService.markRead has no already-read short-circuit (unconditional upsert + bus publish), so each of these phantom reads also emits a team-room frame.

**Impact:** Mentions and unread dots are silently swallowed for any user who leaves a /team tab open in the background — they return to 'no unread anywhere' even though they never saw the messages. For a collaboration product whose mention badge is the notification mechanism, this is dropped-notification data loss for that user (bounded: per-user receipts only, not team-wide).

**Recommended fix:** Mirror the inbox pattern: in onMessage, only call markRead() when document.visibilityState === "visible"; otherwise set a sawInboundWhileHiddenRef and fire markRead from the existing onVisibility handler (which already exists at lines 431-445 for backfill). Also gate the mount-markRead (line 184) on visibility. Optionally add a cheap already-read short-circuit in ChannelsService.markRead to stop the unconditional team-room read frame.

### 6. Forward pagination after search jump-to is broken: /around returns a base64url afterCursor but the ?after= API only accepts a raw ISO timestamp (400 invalid after)

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/api/src/lib/team-chat/queries.ts:386 (afterCursor = encodeCursor(...)); apps/api/src/team-chat/channels.service.ts:416-423 (Date.parse gate); apps/web/src/features/team-chat/hooks/use-team-channel-events.ts:237-269 (loadNewer)` · **Area:** team-chat

**Root cause:** listChannelMessagesAround returns `afterCursor: encodeCursor(newestLoaded.createdAt, newestLoaded.id)` — an opaque base64url(`iso|id`) string (queries.ts:386, 633-636). The client's loadAround stores it in newerCursor (use-team-channel-events.ts:293-301) and loadNewer sends it verbatim as `?after=<cursor>` (line 243). But ChannelsService.listMessages treats `after` as a RAW ISO timestamp: `if (Number.isNaN(Date.parse(opts.after))) throw new BadRequestException({ error: "invalid after" })` (channels.service.ts:417-419), then listChannelMessagesAfter does `new Date(after)` (queries.ts:410). Verified concretely: Date.parse of an encodeCursor output (e.g. "MjAyNi0wNi0xMFQxMjozNDo1Ni43ODlafGNtYnE3eGt6...") is NaN. So the very first auto-load-forward after a search jump (bottom-sentinel IntersectionObserver in channel-thread.tsx:139-154) gets a 400, loadNewer returns 0, newerCursor never advances, and hasMoreNewer stays true. The before-direction is consistent (around's beforeCursor → ?before= → decodeCursor) — only the after path has the asymmetric contract.

**Impact:** Jump-to-message from channel or workspace search loads the context window, but the user can never scroll forward from the anchor toward newer messages — every forward page request 400s silently (loadNewer just returns 0). The only escape is the "Jump to live" pill, which throws away their reading position. Breaks the read-forward-from-search flow in normal use; invisible in logs except as 400 noise.

**Recommended fix:** Make the contract symmetric: either have the service's after-path accept the opaque cursor (try decodeCursor first, fall back to Date.parse for the ISO callers like runBackfill), or change listChannelMessagesAround to return afterCursor as a plain ISO (newestLoaded.createdAt). Preferable: accept the encoded cursor and use the (createdAt, id) keyset in listChannelMessagesAfter — which also fixes the same-millisecond tiebreak gap noted separately.

### 7. Every .webp image is force-classified as a WhatsApp sticker — ordinary webp photos cannot be sent, and the client size guard checks the wrong cap

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/web/src/features/inbox/components/reply-box/utils.ts:14 (plus reply-box.tsx:82-88,1055; apps/api/src/lib/media-storage.ts:14-19,43; messages.service.ts:1169-1175)` · **Area:** chat-ux

**Root cause:** The composer's file input accepts `image/*` (reply-box.tsx:1055), which includes image/webp — the default export format of many screenshot tools and web downloads. Both the client (`kindFromMimeClient`, reply-box/utils.ts:14: `if (lower === "image/webp") return "sticker"`) and the server (`kindFromMime`, media-storage.ts:43: `["image/webp", "sticker"] // before image/* so .webp routes to sticker`) hard-map webp to the sticker kind with no way to send it as a regular image. Two failure modes: (a) the client size guard `pickSizeLimit` treats any image/* as the 4.8MB image cap (reply-box.tsx:82-88), but the server enforces `MEDIA_SIZE_CAPS.sticker = 500KB` (media-storage.ts:18) — so a 2MB webp photo passes the client check, uploads fully, then 413s with 'file too large for sticker', defeating the guard's stated purpose ('a 12MB iPhone photo would upload for several minutes on 3G before the server rejected it'). (b) A <500KB webp that isn't a real sticker (Meta requires 512x512 webp, ~100KB static) is uploaded and sent to Meta AS a sticker — messages.service.ts:1171 only verifies the mime IS webp — yielding an opaque provider rejection or a mangled sticker render on the customer's phone. The optimistic bubble also renders it as a chrome-less 128px StickerImage instead of a photo.

**Impact:** Agents who attach a webp photo (increasingly the default image format) either burn a full upload before a confusing 'file too large for sticker' 413, or get a Meta-side rejection / broken sticker delivered to the customer. There is no in-app path to send webp content as a normal image.

**Recommended fix:** Only classify webp as a sticker when it plausibly IS one (e.g. size <= 500KB AND square dimensions, or an explicit 'send as sticker' affordance); otherwise route image/webp to kind 'image' (Meta accepts webp images, or transcode to jpeg server-side like the voice-note transcode). At minimum, make `pickSizeLimit` mirror the server's kind mapping so webp is checked against the 500KB sticker cap client-side.

### 8. Composer hard-locks ALL sending for up to 90s during a media upload — Enter/Send no-op, no progress or cancel UI, and a voice recording sent in that window is silently destroyed

**Severity:** medium · **Location:** `apps/web/src/features/inbox/components/reply-box.tsx:605-620, 747, 842-843, 1030-1046, 1204` · **Area:** chat-ux

**Root cause:** `sendInFlightRef` is held for the entire fetch of the previous send (reply-box.tsx:619-620, cleared in the finally at 842-843). For media that fetch is capped at 90 seconds (line 747) and the upload itself can take most of that on a slow uplink (the code's own comment cites multi-minute 3G uploads). During that window: the Send button is disabled (`disabled={!canSend || sendInFlight}`, line 1204), Enter no-ops (`if (sendInFlightRef.current) return;`, line 606), and the only in-flight indicator is the small clock glyph on the optimistic bubble — there is no upload progress and no cancel affordance. Worse, the mic button stays ENABLED, and the voice path destroys data: RecordingBar's onSend (lines 1030-1046) first calls `voice.stopAndCollect()` — which tears down the MediaRecorder, chunks, and recording bar — and only then calls `submit({ file, voice: true })`, which silently early-returns on the in-flight guard (line 606) or on `overrideFile && windowClosed` (line 617). The collected File is dropped on the floor: no toast, no failed bubble, no way to recover the recording.

**Impact:** An agent who sends a large video and then tries to fire a quick text or voice reply is blocked for up to 90 seconds with no explanation (button just stays grey), and a voice message recorded+sent during that window — or in the minute after the 24h window flips closed mid-recording — vanishes without any feedback. Lost agent work and a confusing dead composer during the exact moments a busy inbox multitasks.

**Recommended fix:** In the voice onSend path, check `sendInFlightRef`/window state BEFORE destroying the recording (keep recording, surface a toast 'wait for the current upload'), or stash the collected File as the staged attachment instead of discarding it. Longer term, scope the idempotency guard to its real purpose (block re-submitting the same draft) rather than serializing all sends, and add a progress/cancel affordance on the optimistic media bubble (XHR or fetch+ReadableStream upload progress).

### 9. No staleness floor or sweeper for live calls — one permanently-dropped terminate webhook leaves the team Calls badge stuck forever

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/api/src/calls/calls.service.ts:1097-1112` · **Area:** calls-module

**Root cause:** liveCount counts status IN (ringing, in_progress) with no time bound, and there is no calls sweeper (apps/api/src/lib/sweepers/ has backstops for inbound-media, workflow-waiting, drift etc. — nothing for Call rows). Convergence to terminal relies entirely on (a) Meta's terminate webhook landing and ingesting, or (b) a browser POSTing /end. The webhook controller deliberately drops permanently-failing batches with a 200 (meta.controller.ts:229-233 'permanent ingest failure; dropping batch'), and an unanswered INBOUND ring has no browser-side /end path (the toast just expires at 60s, incoming-call-toast.tsx:89-93). A Call row stuck in ringing/in_progress therefore inflates the team-wide badge …

**Impact:** Edge-trigger, but once hit the symptom is permanent and team-visible (badge stuck at ≥1, requires manual DB surgery) — the same 'stuck badge' bug class this codebase has repeatedly paid for in unread counts.

**Recommended fix:** Either add `ringingAt: { gte: now - ~4h }` to liveCount, or add a small sweeper that terminalizes ringing rows older than ~5 minutes and in_progress rows older than Meta's max call duration, mirroring the inbound-media sweeper pattern.

### 10. Thread panel does not react to its root message being deleted — stays open on a dead root, replies into it 404

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/web/src/features/team-chat/components/team-chat-workspace.tsx:279-282; apps/web/src/features/team-chat/hooks/use-thread-events.ts:199-210` · **Area:** team-chat

**Root cause:** When a thread root is deleted (author or admin, possibly in another tab/user), the server emits team:channel:message:deleted with threadRootId: null (channels.service.ts:688-694) and the DB cascades all replies (schema: threadRoot relation onDelete: Cascade). The feed hook splices the root out of `messages` (use-team-channel-events.ts:513-527). But the workspace's liveThreadRoot falls back to the stale snapshot when the root vanishes: `channelState.messages.find((m) => m.id === thread.id) ?? thread` (team-chat-workspace.tsx:281), so the panel stays open rendering the deleted root and its cascaded-away replies. useThreadEvents.onDeleted can't catch it either: it filters `payload.threadRootId !== rootMessageId` (use-thread-events.ts:202), and a root-delete frame carries threadRootId null. Typing a reply into the dead thread POSTs to /messages/:rootId/thread, hits requireThreadRoot's 404 (channels.service.ts:1224-1229), and the optimistic bubble flips to failed with no explanation.

**Impact:** A user reading/replying in a thread whose root an admin just deleted keeps a ghost panel and gets unexplained 'Failed to send' on replies. Rare in practice (concurrent delete while a thread is open) and self-heals on panel close — bounded UX bug, no data integrity issue.

**Recommended fix:** In use-thread-events (or the workspace), also listen for team:channel:message:deleted where payload.messageId === rootMessageId and threadRootId === null, and close the panel (call onClose / setThread(null)) with a small 'This thread was deleted' toast. One extra condition in an existing handler.

### 11. Optimistic reaction toggle only works once per (message, emoji): local dispatch uses version=1, which the version guard discards after the first server frame

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/web/src/features/team-chat/components/channel-message.tsx:93-112; apps/web/src/features/team-chat/hooks/use-team-channel-events.ts:533-540` · **Area:** team-chat

**Root cause:** toggleReaction dispatches a local team:channel:reaction:changed frame with `version: 1` (channel-message.tsx:105-112), commented as 'intentionally far below any server Date.now() so the real frame's later version always wins'. But the receiving reducer keeps a per-(messageId, emoji) high-water mark and drops anything not strictly newer: `if (payload.version <= lastVersion) return;` (use-team-channel-events.ts:539, same in use-thread-events.ts:219-221). The FIRST local toggle applies (1 > 0); the server's confirming frame then sets lastVersion ≈ Date.now(); every SUBSEQUENT local dispatch for that pair (un-reacting, re-reacting) arrives with version 1 ≤ lastVersion and is silently discarded — the UI only updates when the server's round-trip frame lands. The optimistic path is therefore dead for all but the first interaction per emoji per message per mount.

**Impact:** Un-reacting (the second toggle) and any re-toggle feel laggy — the chip doesn't move until the HTTP+socket round-trip completes (noticeable on slow links; looks like 'reaction stuck'). Violates the project's own optimistic-socket-dispatch standard; cosmetic, no data loss.

**Recommended fix:** Dispatch the local frame with `version: Date.now()` (monotonic with the server's stamps since both are wall-clock on the same order) or with `lastKnownVersion + 1` read from the reducer; the server's authoritative frame still wins because its publish-time Date.now() is later than the click-time one.

### 12. Delta backfill uses strict createdAt > cursor with no id tiebreak — a same-millisecond sibling message can be skipped on the deferred-backfill path

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/api/src/lib/team-chat/queries.ts:397-424 (listChannelMessagesAfter); apps/web/src/features/team-chat/hooks/use-team-channel-events.ts:403-429 (runBackfill)` · **Area:** team-chat

**Root cause:** listChannelMessagesAfter filters `createdAt: { gt: new Date(after) }` (queries.ts:410) with no id tiebreak, and the client's runBackfill cursor is the newest known message's createdAt (use-team-channel-events.ts:406-412). createdAt is set server-side via `new Date()` (ms precision). If two messages commit in the same millisecond and the client received the frame for one but missed the other (throttled hidden tab — the exact scenario runBackfill exists for, backfillNeededRef), the strict `gt` excludes both rows and the missed sibling never arrives via the delta; only a genuine reconnect's recoverOnReconnect full-replace (line 371-401) heals it, and only if the gap is within the newest page. This is the same `>` vs `>=` lesson already memorialized for the inbox list (project memory: 'unread replay recency guard' — `>=` + id dedupe), not carried over to team chat. Safe to widen: the client already dedupes by message id (line 422-424).

**Impact:** Permanently missing message in the feed for that tab after a hidden-tab frame drop coinciding with a same-ms burst (two agents posting simultaneously). Narrow window at pilot scale; converges on the next hard reconnect or reload.

**Recommended fix:** Change the after-path to `gte` and rely on the client's existing id-based dedupe (one-line WHERE change), or accept the (createdAt,id) keyset cursor per the fix for the around/after contract finding — which solves both at once.

### 13. threadReplyCount in the message_created fanout is computed from a pre-transaction read — concurrent replies publish stale absolute counts that stick on the parent pill

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/api/src/team-chat/channels.service.ts:1005-1008 + 1066 (postThreadReply); 1097-1110 + 1205 (uploadMedia)` · **Area:** team-chat

**Root cause:** postThreadReply reads the root (including threadReplyCount) in the parallel pre-check Promise.all BEFORE the transaction that does `threadReplyCount: { increment: 1 }`, then publishes `threadReplyCount: root.threadReplyCount + 1` (line 1066). Two near-simultaneous replies both read count=N and both publish N+1; the client applies the event's count as an ABSOLUTE value (use-team-channel-events.ts:573-588 onThreadReply sets threadReplyCount: payload.replyCount), so the parent's 'X replies' pill shows N+1 instead of N+2 until the next reply/delete event or a refetch corrects it. uploadMedia has the identical pattern (rootForFanout read at 1097-1110, published at 1205). The delete path is NOT affected — it publishes the post-decrement value returned by the update (lines 644-664).

**Impact:** Reply-count pill can lag by one (or more under a burst) on every client until the next thread event. Cosmetic counter drift, DB value is always correct.

**Recommended fix:** Have the transaction's root update return the post-increment value (`tx.teamChannelMessage.update({..., select: { threadReplyCount: true } })`) and publish that, exactly as deleteMessage already does for the decrement path. Same one-line change in uploadMedia's thread branch.

### 14. Retry on a failed media bubble after a thread switch silently degrades to a caption-only text send — the attachment is dropped without warning

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/web/src/features/inbox/components/reply-box.tsx:252-264, 582-598 (plus message-thread.tsx:605-616)` · **Area:** chat-ux

**Root cause:** The Retry affordance on a failed media bubble restores the original File from `pendingFilesRef`, but that Map lives in the ReplyBox instance and is dropped on unmount (acknowledged in the comment at reply-box.tsx:255-258 — MessageThread is keyed by displayedId, so every chat switch remounts it). Failed bubbles, however, DO survive chat switches: snapshot-on-leave persists the live slice including failed rows into the LRU (use-conversation-events.ts:495-522), so the Retry button is still there on switch-back. Clicking it then runs the prefill effect (reply-box.tsx:587-595) with no Map entry: `setAttachment` never fires and only `prefill.body` (the caption) is restored. The failed bubble is already removed by `retryFailed` (message-thread.tsx:607), so if the agent hits Enter they send a bare text message — the media is silently never delivered, and for a caption-less media failure the composer is simply empty, making Retry a confusing no-op.

**Impact:** Customer receives a caption with no image/document (or the agent gets a dead Retry button), with no indication the attachment was lost. Bounded: the composer visibly lacks the attachment pill, and it only occurs on retry-after-thread-switch.

**Recommended fix:** When `prefill.clientTempId` has no `pendingFilesRef` entry and the failed message had media, surface it: keep the failed bubble in place (skip removeOptimistic until the entry is confirmed present) or toast 'Original file no longer available — please re-attach', instead of silently prefilling caption-only text.

### 15. No dir="auto" anywhere in the chat surface — RTL (Arabic/Hebrew) customer messages render with LTR alignment and misplaced punctuation

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/web/src/features/inbox/components/message-bubble.tsx:252-263 (also conversation-list-item.tsx:109-117, reply-box.tsx:949-1024, quoted-reply.tsx:51-58, internal-note.tsx:37)` · **Area:** chat-ux

**Root cause:** Message bodies render as `<p className="whitespace-pre-wrap wrap-break-word">{message.body}</p>` with no `dir` attribute, and the same applies to the conversation-list preview, quoted-reply snippet, internal notes, and the composer Textarea. For a WhatsApp product (RTL-heavy markets), an Arabic or Hebrew inbound message is laid out with LTR base direction: text left-aligns inside the bubble, and any trailing punctuation, numbers, or mixed Latin tokens render in the wrong position (e.g. '?' jumping to the wrong end). WhatsApp Web / Telegram set `dir="auto"` per message node for exactly this reason. Long-word/URL overflow is otherwise handled correctly (`wrap-break-word`), so this is the one remaining text-rendering gap.

**Impact:** Every RTL conversation reads visually wrong (alignment + punctuation placement) for both inbound bubbles and the agent's own RTL replies. Cosmetic but constant for any pilot with Arabic/Hebrew-speaking customers.

**Recommended fix:** Add `dir="auto"` to the message body <p>, quoted-reply body span, list preview <p>, internal-note body, and the composer Textarea. One-line change per site; `dir=auto` derives base direction from the first strong character per element, which is the WhatsApp Web behavior.

### 16. Stacked window-level Escape handlers are unscoped — Esc with the lightbox open also closes in-thread search and exits selection mode

**Severity:** low · **Location:** `apps/web/src/features/inbox/components/attachments/media-lightbox.tsx:60-83 (also message-search.tsx:86-96, message-thread.tsx:523-530, snippet-popup.tsx:76-97, emoji-popover.tsx:117-138)` · **Area:** chat-ux

**Root cause:** Five inbox surfaces each register their own `window`/`document` keydown listener for Escape (lightbox, in-thread search bar, selection mode, snippet popup, emoji popover) with no layering/coordination — `preventDefault` does not stop sibling window listeners. Concretely: with in-thread search open, clicking an image bubble opens the MediaLightbox; pressing Escape closes the lightbox AND the search bar (wiping the query, matches, and active-match position the agent was navigating). Same with selection mode active under any popover: one Esc collapses every layer at once instead of peeling the topmost. The lightbox also doesn't trap focus (aria-modal="true" but no focus management), so Tab walks the background thread while the full-screen dialog is up.

**Impact:** One Escape dismisses multiple layers simultaneously; an agent stepping through search matches who peeks at an image loses their whole search session. Minor but repeatedly irritating in normal triage flow; the missing focus trap is an a11y gap for keyboard users.

**Recommended fix:** Give modal-ish layers a simple stack discipline: check `event.defaultPrevented` at the top of each Escape handler (and have the topmost layer preventDefault), or register the deeper layer's listener in capture phase with stopImmediatePropagation. Add a basic focus trap (or use the existing Radix Dialog primitive) for MediaLightbox.

### 17. Capability matrix has no read/export axis: full contact-book CSV export (and equivalent list reads) remain open to a fully capability-stripped agent

**Severity:** info · **Location:** `apps/api/src/contacts/contacts.controller.ts:154-165` · **Area:** authz-capabilities

**Root cause:** GET /api/contacts/export streams the team's entire contact book (phone numbers, names, emails, tags, stage, all custom fields) to ANY session — it sits under the class-level SessionGuard with no @RequireCapability and no inline check, and no export-shaped capability exists in ALL_CAPABILITIES (the 12 capabilities are all manage/delete/call-shaped). To be fair to the design: this is mostly coherent with the shared-inbox product model — GET /api/contacts (list with search/filters, also ungated) already gives every agent paginated read access to the same data, so gating export alone would be theater. The reason it still deserves a line in the report: an admin who uses the Settings → Permissions screen to strip an agent's capabilities may reasonably believe data access is bounded, but no read/export axis exists to restrict.

**Impact:** A capability-stripped (or about-to-quit) agent can exfiltrate the full customer contact database in a single authenticated request. Bounded honestly: the same agent can scrape the list endpoint anyway, so this is a product-model observation about the capability matrix lacking any read-scoping dimension, not an enforcement bug.

**Recommended fix:** If/when a customer asks for data-loss controls, add a `contacts:export` capability (default true to preserve behavior) gating GET /api/contacts/export and the contacts page's Export button — a 3-site change under the existing pattern. Not worth doing speculatively before a pilot asks; flagged so the gap is a decision rather than an accident.

### 18. Stale comments claim thread events are delivered 'through the team room' — they are channel-room scoped; misleading documentation on a confidentiality-relevant fanout surface

**Severity:** info · **Location:** `apps/api/src/realtime/realtime.gateway.ts:735-739; apps/web/src/features/team-chat/hooks/use-thread-events.ts:112-114` · **Area:** team-chat

**Root cause:** realtime.gateway.ts:735-739 states '(No subscribe:channel-thread handler — thread replies, edits, deletes, and reactions are all delivered through the team room and filtered client-side ... every event source already targets the team room'. use-thread-events.ts:112-114 repeats it: 'Thread events ride the channel's team-room frames'. Both are wrong: fanout-rules.ts:333-391 routes message_created/edited/deleted/reaction_changed via emitToChannel (channel room), and the comment block at fanout-rules.ts:326-332 documents that channel-room scoping is the privacy boundary keeping non-members from seeing message bodies. A future change trusting the gateway comment (e.g. assuming non-members already receive these frames, or 'optimizing' by emitting thread events to the team room to match the documented behavior) would leak private-channel content to every team member.

**Impact:** No runtime effect today. Risk is a future regression: the comments describe a weaker security posture than the code implements, in the exact place someone would look before changing fanout scoping.

**Recommended fix:** Correct both comments to say 'delivered through the CHANNEL room (membership-gated at subscribe:channel) and filtered client-side by threadRootId'.

### 19. Mention markup permits display-name spoofing: @[AnyName](userId) renders the authored name, never reconciled against the user table

**Severity:** info · **Location:** `packages/shared/src/team-chat/mentions.ts:27 (MENTION_PATTERN), 47-62 (parseMentions); apps/web/src/features/team-chat/components/body-renderer.tsx:40-51` · **Area:** team-chat

**Root cause:** Mentions are encoded inline as @[Display Name](userId) and the renderer displays tok.name verbatim as a styled chip (body-renderer.tsx:49 `@{tok.name}`), with the userId only used for the highlight check. The server validates only the userId set (channels.service.ts:1275-1302 validateMentions intersects ids with team+channel membership for the side-effect mention rows) — the NAME inside the brackets is never checked. A user can hand-type `@[Alice](bobs-user-id)`: the chip reads 'Alice' while Bob receives the mention row and badge; or `@[CEO](any-string)` renders a legit-looking mention chip for a nonexistent user (tokenizeBody styles any matching token regardless of id validity). stripMentionMarkup propagates the spoofed name into channel previews and search results.

**Impact:** Internal-only social-engineering/confusion vector among authenticated teammates (misattributing a ping, fake-official-looking chips). No privilege or data exposure; bounded to display.

**Recommended fix:** At render time, resolve the chip label from the team-members map already available in every consumer (teamMembers / namesById) when the userId is known, falling back to the authored name only for departed users; optionally have validateMentions canonicalize names in the stored body.

Both triggers the finding name

- **Test-run path executes an unvalidated draft graph, skipping the cycle/graph validation that publish enforces** — The finding's mechanics are individually accurate (test() at workflows.service.ts:512-612 enqueues wf.graph without calling validateGraph; the __test sentinel bypasses the runner's published gate at runner.ts:132-144), but its load-bearing premise is a misread: it claims cycle errors are 'non-blocking draft: warnings' at SAVE tier, so a cyclic graph can be saved and then test-run. In current code 

- **Five raw toLocaleString timestamp renders bypass the LocalTime/team-timezone single source of truth** — The finding's central claim — that the five bare toLocaleString sites drift from a "TEAM timezone" rendered by LocalTime/TimezoneProvider — is based on a misreading of the tz architecture. # REMEDIATION — 2026-06-11

Branch `audit-remediation-2026-06-11`. Implemented via 14 file-disjoint cluster agents (waves of 4), then `prisma generate` + central typecheck, an automatic repair loop, and an adversarial review of every cluster's actual git diff. **The 14 by-design and 3 refuted findings were deliberately NOT touched** (they are locked product decisions).

## Verification performed
- `pnpm typecheck --force` — **green** (both @ccp/web and @ccp/api), re-run after every edit wave including the review-applied fixes.
- **API smoke-boot** — `Nest application successfully started`, all DI wired incl. the two new sweepers (`stale-calls`, `broadcast-schedule-drift`); `/health` → `{ok:true,db:true,redis:true}`.
- **Web smoke-boot** — Next.js compiled, `Ready` clean; new `INTERNAL_API_URL`/`TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS` env validation now warns in dev (finding #108).
- **Migrations** — all 6 new migrations `prisma migrate deploy` applied cleanly to a live DB.
- New routes confirmed mapped: `/api/users/:id/reset-password`, `/api/admin/teams/:id/members/:userId/reset-password`, `/api/broadcasts/:id/cancel`.

## Outcome: ~108 findings fixed across 14 clusters
- **A-provider (7):** `metaFetch` transient-retry made opt-in so non-idempotent send/call POSTs can't double-fire (#1); inbound template quick-reply `button` parsed (#2); inbound location/vCard placeholders (#24); rate-limit error codes normalized (#25); access token scrubbed from upload-session URL (#11); async delivery-failure reason persisted to `Message` (new `message_status_error` migration, #26).
- **B-calls (9):** `markConnected`/`endCall` scoped to participants; answer-rollback nulls `answeredAt`; permission REQUEST vs GRANT split (new `CallPermissionStatus` migration); self-imposed 24h lockout removed; incoming-call toast respects `calls:receive`; calls surface/reorder the inbox list.
- **C-realtime-client (10):** off-slice `message:new` (HIGH #0) + call frames now recover the conversation into the list; reconnect jitter timer cleared on unmount; `contacts:bulk_updated` converges the displayed thread; double-mounted counts hook collapsed; refetch chains get rejection handling.
- **D-realtime-server (6):** per-socket rate-limit no longer throws an uncaughtException per frame; presence snapshot error-isolated; `typing:thread:start` validates membership.
- **E-external-api (9):** bulk-tag fanout no longer fires spurious workflow/webhook triggers; soft-deleted contacts excluded from `/v1` GETs; `Idempotency-Key` length-guarded; plain-http outbound URLs rejected; machine-parseable error codes.
- **F-broadcasts (8):** fire-time soft-delete re-check; dead-token permanent-error circuit breaker; queued-drift sweeper; stop button wired (#5); clone preserves all audience types.
- **G-workflows (5):** waiting-runs sweeper clears retained terminal jobIds; `http_request` header encrypt-at-rest; test-run graph validated; `RUN_WORKER_INLINE=0` production boot-guard.
- **H-db (9):** 4 index migrations (drop 4 redundant hot-table btrees, drop dead customFields GIN + unusable preview-trgm, add 6 User-FK ON DELETE scan indexes); global-search index-miss documented.
- **I-deploy (14):** Redis `noeviction` in compose (#21/#44); config-driven offsite backup + cron + restore-runbook fixes (#46); rollback never promotes an unshipped image (#48); HTTP/3 UDP note; key-rotation/re-encrypt script.
- **J-platform (8):** correlation-id propagated edge→web→api; RSC→NestJS retry re-enabled; Redis surfaced in health; legacy webhook proxy body-size cap; dead `/api/internal` allowlist removed; `INTERNAL_API_URL` env-validated.
- **K-auth (1, HIGH #4):** full **admin-initiated password reset** — two NestJS routes + UI in team members and platform org members; reuses the bcrypt `Account.password` path; revokes the target's sessions; self-reset guarded to change-password.
- **L-team-chat (7):** symmetric `?after=` pagination; live sidebar unread/mention badges; reaction/edit/pin wiring; markRead short-circuit (regression caught + fixed in review).
- **M-settings-admin (2):** org suspension now drops live sockets AND deletes member sessions (#7/#14).
- **N-web-ux (13):** shared `ConfirmDialog` (with typed-confirm for destructive ops) replaces `window.confirm`; mobile contact panel via Sheet (#40); first-run empty states; settings IA naming; status-label consolidation (`status-meta.ts`); expired-window template dead-end.

## Review-caught regressions (auto-fixed during the run)
- A-provider: finding #67's CAS introduced a new status-race — corrected.
- F-broadcasts: stop-button vs cancel-service `paused` state mismatch — corrected.
- L-team-chat: markRead already-read short-circuit regression — corrected.
- D-realtime / H-db: several stale doc-comments — corrected.

## Deferred / needs follow-up (NOT done this pass)
- **#3 (HIGH) media-header templates unsendable** — `sendTemplate` still doesn't build the IMAGE/VIDEO/DOCUMENT header component; deferred (larger change touching the resumable-upload send path). **Recommend next.**
- **#38** full-res images for thumbnails — partial (needs UploadThing transform params).
- **#29** CSV-import event emission, **#94** web/api DTO dedup, **#97** read/export capability axis — deferred (cross-cluster or net-negative).
- **#111** api Docker image still ships web deps — partial (pnpm `--filter` deploy form staged but not boot-validated this pass).

## Manual steps required before/at deploy
1. **Run the 6 new migrations on prod** (`prisma migrate deploy` runs in the api boot path).
2. **Offsite backup**: set the rclone/S3 env vars in prod `.env` to activate the now-wired offsite push (no-op until set).
3. **Redis `noeviction`**: takes effect on next `docker compose up` (recreates the redis container); the running local/prod redis keeps `allkeys-lru` until then.
4. Review the deferred #3 (media templates) — the one remaining HIGH.
